Relative Regular Riemann-Hilbert correspondence II The research of L. Fiorot was supported by project PRIN 2017 Categories, Algebras: Ring-Theoretical and Homological Approaches (CARTHA). The research of T. Monteiro Fernandes was supported by Fundação para a Ciência e Tecnologia, under the project: UIDB/04561/2020.
Luisa Fiorot, Teresa Monteiro Fernandes, Claude Sabbah

Abstract
We develop the theory of relative regular holonomic 𝒟 \mathscr{D} -modules with a smooth complex manifold S S of arbitrary dimension as parameter space, together with their main functorial properties. In particular, we establish in this general setting the relative Riemann-Hilbert correspondence proved in a previous work in the one-dimensional case.



Classification:
14F10, 32C38, 32S40, 32S60, 35Nxx, 58J10

keywords
Holonomic relative
D
-module, regularity, relative constructible sheaf, relative perverse sheaf

1
Introduction

In this article, we are concerned with holomorphic families of coherent
\mathscr{D}
-modules on a complex manifold
X
of dimension
d_{X}
, parametrized by a complex manifold
S
, that is, coherent modules over the ring
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
of linear relative differential operators with respect to the projection
p_{X}:X\times\penalty S\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
(simply denoted by
p
when no confusion is possible). More specifically, we consider families for which the characteristic variety in the relative cotangent space
(T^{*}X)\times S
is contained in the product by
S
of a fixed closed conic Lagrangian analytic subset
\Lambda\subset T^{*}X
. Following
[
FMFS21
,
MFS13
,
MFS19
]
, we call these systems
relative holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules
. Here are some examples.

1.

Deligne considered in
[
Del70
]
the case of vector bundles
E
on
X\times\penalty S
with a flat relative connection
\nabla
, and established an equivalence with the category of locally constant sheaves of coherent
p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
-modules on
X\times\penalty S
. In this case, the relative characteristic variety is the product of the zero section
T^{*}_{X}X
by
S
.

2.

For any holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module
\mathscr{M}
on
X
with characteristic variety
\Lambda
, the pullback
q^{*}\mathscr{M}=\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}\otimes_{q^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{X}}q^{-1}\mathscr{M}
by the projection
q:X\times\penalty S\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X
is naturally endowed with a
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module structure, and the relative characteristic variety of
\mathscr{M}
is equal to
\Lambda\times S
. For
(E,\nabla)
as in (
1
), the characteristic variety of
q^{*}\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}}E
(equipped with its natural
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module structure) is contained in
\Lambda\times S
.

3.

Some integral transformations from objects on
X
to objects on
S
have kernels which are such flat bundles
(E,\nabla)
. One of them is the Fourier-Mukai transformation
\mathrm{FM}
introduced by Laumon
[
Lau96
]
and Rothstein
[
Rot96
]
, which attaches to any bounded complex of
\mathscr{D}
-modules with coherent cohomology on an abelian variety
A
a bounded complex of
\mathscr{O}
-modules with coherent cohomology on the moduli space
A^{\sharp}
of line bundles with integrable connection on
A
(cf.
[
Sch15
]
and the references therein). It is obtained as the integral transform with kernel
\mathscr{P}
on
A\times A^{\sharp}
associated to the Poincaré bundle on the product
A\times\mathrm{Pic}^{0}(A)
. By construction,
\mathscr{P}
is equipped with a flat relative connection, i.e., is a
\mathscr{D}_{A\times A^{\sharp}/A^{\sharp}}
-module. Then
q^{*}\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{A\times A^{\sharp}}}\mathscr{P}
is an instance of (
2
), and
\mathrm{FM}(\mathscr{M})
is the pushforward
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}p_{*}(q^{*}\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{A\times A^{\sharp}}}\mathscr{P})
. It is an object of
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{O}_{A^{\sharp}})
.

4.

Given a holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module
\mathscr{M}
and holomorphic functions
f_{1},\dots,f_{p}
on
X
defining a divisor
Y\!=\!\{\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i}f_{i}\!=\!0\}
, and setting
S=\mathbb{C}^{p}
with coordinates
s_{1},\cdots,s_{p}
, the
S
-analytic counterpart of
[
Mai23
, Prop. 13]
asserts that the
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-submodule generated by
q^{*}\mathscr{M}\cdot(\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i}f_{i}^{s_{i}})
in the twisted coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*(Y\times\penalty S))
-module
q^{*}\mathscr{M}(*(Y\times\penalty S))\cdot(\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i}f_{i}^{s_{i}})
is relative holonomic.

5.

In his construction of moduli spaces for regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}
-modules, Nitsure
[
Nit99
]
fixes a divisor with normal crossings in
X
and deforms pre-
\mathscr{D}
-modules (extending the notion of vector bundle with flat logarithmic connection) relative to this divisor and its canonical stratification. The corresponding holomorphic family of regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}
-modules has its characteristic variety adapted to this stratification, hence of the form
\Lambda\times S
.

6.

Mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-modules (cf.
[
Moc15
]
) are compound objects defined on the product of
X
by the complex line
\mathbb{C}
, whose module components are holomorphic families of holonomic
\mathscr{D}
-modules parametrized by
S=\mathbb{C}^{*}
degenerating at
0\in\mathbb{C}
to a coherent module on the cotangent space
T^{*}X
. On
S
, the characteristic variety of each holonomic
\mathscr{D}
-module is, by definition, contained in a fixed Lagrangian variety
\Lambda
. Of particular interest are the regular mixed twistor
\mathscr{D}
-modules, which have furnished the first example of families we are dealing with (cf.
[
MFS13
]
).

Our definition of relative holonomicity imposes the following:
the only possible changes in the characteristic variety of the restricted
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module to a fixed parameter, when the parameter varies, is a change of multiplicities on each irreducible component of
\Lambda
. This condition is reasonable, as shown by the previous examples.

Besides, not any relative holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module
\mathscr{K}
can serve as the kernel of an integral transformation as in (
3
), because it cannot be ensured that, for a holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module
\mathscr{M}
, the tensor product
q^{*}\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}}\mathscr{K}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-coherent, in contrast with the classical result when
S
is reduced to a point. Indeed, in general, Bernstein-Sato theory only applies on some open subset of
S
depending on
\mathscr{M}
, as exemplified in
[
MFS17
, Ex. 2.4]
. Fortunately, adding the regularity property—as defined in
[
MFS19
]
—of the kernel
\mathscr{K}
and of
\mathscr{M}
overcomes this difficulty, as already shown in
[
FMFS21
]
when
\dim S=1
.

In order to replace Bernstein-Sato theory, the main tool is the stability of regular holonomicity by
\mathscr{D}
-module pullback
[
FMFS21
, Th. 2]
, that we generalize to the case where
\dim S
can be arbitrary.

Theorem 1

(Stability by pullback)
.

Let
\mathscr{M}\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
and let
f:Y\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X
be a morphism of complex manifolds and let us still denote by
f
the morphism
f\times\id
. Then
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f^{*}\mathscr{M}\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S/S})
.

A possible application of our approach is that, for an integral transformation as in (
3
) with regular holonomic kernel
\mathscr{K}
, the analysis of local properties of
q^{*}\mathscr{M}\otimes^{L}_{\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}}\mathscr{K}

leads to a better understanding of the
\mathscr{K}
-transform
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}p_{*}(q^{*}\mathscr{M}\otimes^{L}_{\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}}\mathscr{K})
of
\mathscr{M}
.

Here are natural examples of regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules:

a.

Coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-submodules of regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S}
-modules, provided that the irreducible components of the characteristic variety of the latter decompose as products with respect to the product decomposition
T^{*}X\times T^{*}S
(cf. Section
3.c
); they can be called
integrable
regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules.

b.

In the setting of (
4
) above, let us assume that
\mathscr{M}
is regular holonomic. Then the coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*(Y\times\penalty S))
-module
q^{*}\mathscr{M}(*Y\times\penalty S)\cdot\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i}f_{i}^{s_{i}}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-regular holonomic, hence
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-coherent (cf. Example
4.36
) and this enables us to recover the
S
-analytic version of the result of Maisonobe in (
4
) when
\mathscr{M}
is regular holonomic.

We have developed in
[
MFS13
]
the notion of relative
\mathbb{C}
-constructible complex and relative perverse complex on
X\times\penalty S
, generalizing the notion of relative local system of coherent
p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
-modules considered by Deligne. For each stratum
X_{\alpha}
of a suitable complex stratification
(X_{\alpha})
of
X
, each cohomology of such a complex is locally isomorphic to
p_{X_{\alpha}}^{-1}G_{\alpha}
for some
coherent

\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module
G_{\alpha}
. We prove
here a Riemann-Hilbert correspondence for any
\dim S
(the

\dim S=1
case was proved in
[
FMFS21
]
).

Theorem 2

(Relative Riemann-Hilbert correspondence)
.

The functors

\displaystyle\pSol_{X}:\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})

\displaystyle\RH^{S}_{X}:\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})

are quasi-inverse equivalences of categories.

The proof of the correspondence is now made possible owing to
[
MFP21
]
, as we construct the relative Riemann-Hilbert functor
\RH^{S}_{X}
by means of the site
X_{\mathrm{sa}}\times S
introduced in
[
MFP21
]
instead of the subanalytic site
X_{\mathrm{sa}}\times S_{\mathrm{sa}}
considered in
[
MFP14
]
and used in
[
MFS19
,
FMFS21
]
. The main results are the exact analogues of Theorems 2 and 1 in
[
FMFS21
]
for
S
of arbitrary dimension, with the same notation for relative regular holonomic complexes and
S
-
\mathbb{C}
-constructible complexes, and the same meaning for the perverse solution functor
\pSol_{X}
. We change the order of the theorems with respect to loc. cit. since their proofs are done the other way round in the present paper.

The strategy for the proof of these theorems is similar to that of
[
FMFS21
]
: it is made precise in Sections
4.e
and
6
. However, when
\dim S\geqslant 2
, we have to distinguish between
S
-torsion-freeness and
S
-flatness (also called strictness in loc. cit.). While this is not much trouble for some of the results, owing to the analysis of t-structures made in
[
FMF18
]
, we are led to using Rossi’s flattening theorem of
[
Ros68
]
at some point.

Along the way, we complement the results of
[
MFS19
,
FMFS21
]
by giving further characterizations and properties of regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules or objects of the bounded derived category
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
, which are needed for the proof.

Comparison with other works on regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}
-modules with parameters

1.

In
[
DGS11
]
, the authors introduce a formal parameter
\hbar
and the corresponding rings
\mathbb{C}[\![\hbar]\!]
,
\mathscr{O}_{X}[\![\hbar]\!]
,
\mathscr{D}_{X}[\![\hbar]\!]
denoted by
\mathscr{D}_{X}^{\hbar}
. They define the notion of regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}^{\hbar}
-module by asking that the restriction to the closed point
\hbar=0
is a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module which is different from our point view since we ask regular holonomicity at any value of the parameter. They prove a Riemann-Hilbert correspondence with the category of
\mathbb{C}
-constructible complexes of
\mathbb{C}_{X}[\![\hbar]\!]
-modules by means of a functor they denote by
\THH_{\hbar}
, which would correspond to the notation
\RH_{X}^{S}
in the present paper. The main difference with the notions introduced in the present paper (or more precisely with those introduced in
[
FMFS21
]
, which only considered the case where
\dim S=1
) is that the sheaves considered in
[
DGS11
]
are sheaves of
\mathbb{C}[\![\hbar]\!]
-modules on
X
, while we consider sheaves of
p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
-modules
on the product space
X\times\penalty S
. The authors also consider the restriction at the generic point of
\mathbb{C}[\![\hbar]\!]
, that is, modules over the ring
\mathscr{D}_{X}(\!(\hbar)\!)
which has no counterpart in our setting.

2.

If instead of considering
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-modules one considers flat meromorphic bundles on
X
(i.e., meromorphic connections) with fixed pole divisor, there are many works in the algebraic or formal setting, with base fields or rings that can be different from
\mathbb{C}
. Since the literature is vast, let us only mention
[
ABC20
]
and the more recent preprint
[
HdST21
]
. In the latter preprint, the authors extend Deligne’s equivalence result
[
Del70
, Th. II.5.9]
to an equivalence with a formal parameter. The corresponding equivalence in the present article would be that for
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules of D-type, as defined in Section
4
.

3.

Wu
[
Wu21
]
has established a Riemann-Hilbert correspondence similar to that of Theorem
2
in the case of Alexander complexes that occur in Example (
4
) above.

Organization of the paper

In Section
2
, we review and complete various results on coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules obtained in our previous works
[
MFS13
,
MFS19
,
FMF18
,
FMFS21
]
. We emphasize the behavior of holonomicity with respect to pullback and proper pushforward both with respect to
X
and to the parameter space
S
. Note that the parameter space
S
is always assumed to be a complex manifold, while one should be able to generalize various statements to any complex analytic space.
For example, the sheaf
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty T/T}
is well-defined if
T
is a possibly singular and non reduced complex analytic
space
with sheaf of functions
\mathscr{O}_{T}
. In this work, we restrict the setting to those complex spaces
T
that are embeddable in a smooth complex manifold
S
, with ideal
\mathscr{I}_{T}\subset\mathscr{O}_{S}
, and we regard
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty T/T}
-modules as
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules annihilated by
\mathscr{I}_{T}
.

In Section
3
, we complement various results of
[
FMFS21
]
on the regularity property. In particular, we give details on
[
FMFS21
, Rem. 1.11]
. Furthermore, the relation with the usual notion of regularity of holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S}
-modules is made precise in Section
3.c
. Stability under base pullback and proper base pushforward is established in Section
3.b
(as usual, under a goodness assumption for proper pushforward). The case of pushforward with respect to a proper morphism
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y
(with a goodness assumption) has already been treated in
[
MFS19
]
, and stability by pullback, which is the content of Theorem
1
needs first a detailed analysis of holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules of D-type.

This analysis is performed in Section
4
. The reasoning made in
[
MFS19
]
when
\dim S=1
, relying on the property that the torsion-free quotient of a coherent
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module is locally free, has to be adapted by using base changes with respect to
S
, so that the base functoriality properties considered in Sections
2
and
3
are most useful.
Theorem
1
is proved in Section
4.e
in a way similar to that done in
[
FMFS21
]
. In Section
4.f
, we give a characterization of relative regular holonomicity in terms of formal solutions.

Section
6
gives details on the main steps of the proof of Theorem
2
. The strategy is similar to the one in
[
MFS19
]
, although we need various new technical details contained in Section
5
. On the one hand, the construction of the Riemann-Hilbert functor
\RH_{X}^{S}
is now performed by using the partial subanalytic site
X_{\mathrm{sa}}\times S
introduced in
[
MFP21
]
. On the other hand, the comparison between two definitions of Deligne’s extension, one using the standard notion of moderate growth and the other one obtained via the complex of tempered holomorphic functions on the partial subanalytic site
X_{\mathrm{sa}}\times S
, is also done in Section
5
, relying once more on results of
[
MFP21
]
. We emphasize that the proof given here is simpler, when
\dim S=1
, than that given in
[
MFS19
]
.

2
A review on relative coherent and holonomic
\mathscr{D}
-modules

For complex analytic manifolds
X
and
S
, we denote by

p_{X}:X\times\penalty S\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S\quad\text{and}\quad q_{S}:X\times\penalty S\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X

the projections, and we use the notation
p,q
when there is no confusion possible. The sheaf of relative differential operators
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
is naturally defined as

\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}=q^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X}\otimes_{q^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{X}}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}=\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}\otimes_{q^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{X}}q^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X}.

(2.1)

2.a
Coherence, goodness and holonomicity

We adapt the definitions of
[
Kas03
, §4.7]
, which we refer to for properties and proofs.

Definition 2.2
.

We say that an
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-module
\mathscr{F}
is

1.

\mathscr{O}
-good on
X\times\penalty S
if, on any relatively compact open set
U
of
X\times\penalty S
,
\mathscr{F}_{|U}
is the direct limit of an increasing sequence of
\mathscr{O}_{U}
-coherent submodules (equivalently, the direct limit of an inductive system of
\mathscr{O}_{U}
-coherent modules).

2.

\mathscr{O}
-quasi-coherent
if each point of
X\times\penalty S
has an open neighborhood
U
on which
\mathscr{F}
is
\mathscr{O}
-good.

We say that a
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module
\mathscr{M}
is

3.

good on
X\times\penalty S
if it is
\mathscr{O}
-good on
X\times\penalty S
and
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-coherent.

The category
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathscr{O}\textup{-}\mathrm{q}\textup{-}\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S})
of
\mathscr{O}
-quasi-coherent
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-modules is an abelian full subcategory of
\mathrm{Mod}(\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S})
closed under extensions. Let
Y
be a hypersurface of
X
. We denote by
{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(*Y)
, instead of
{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}(*(Y\times\penalty S))
, the localization functor along the hypersurface
Y\times\penalty S
of
X\times\penalty S
. We will use the notation
X^{*}=X\smallsetminus Y
.

Lemma 2.3
.

1.

Let
\mathscr{L}
be an
\mathscr{O}
-quasi-coherent module. Then
\mathscr{L}(*Y)
is
\mathscr{O}
-quasi-coherent.

2.

Let
\mathscr{L}
be an
\mathscr{O}
-quasi-coherent module supported on
Y\times\penalty S
and localized along
Y\times\penalty S
(i.e.,
\mathscr{L}\simeq\mathscr{L}(*Y)
). Then
\mathscr{L}=0
.

3.

Let
\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt\phi\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\phi}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\mathscr{N}
be a morphism of
\mathscr{O}
-quasi-coherent modules which are localized along
Y\times\penalty S
. If the restriction of
\phi
at
X^{*}\times\penalty S
is an isomorphism (
resp.
zero) then
\phi
is an isomorphism (
resp.
zero).

Proof.

1.

By definition any point of
X\times\penalty S
has an open neighborhood
U
, which we can suppose
to be a relatively compact open set,
on which

\mathscr{L}_{|U}\!=\!\mathop{\textstyle\bigcup}\displaylimits_{i}\mathscr{L}_{i}
is the direct limit of an increasing sequence of
\mathscr{O}
-coherent submodules
\mathscr{L}_{i}
. Since
\mathscr{L}_{i}(*Y)
, being equal to
\mathop{\textstyle\bigcup}\displaylimits_{n}\mathscr{I}_{Y\times\penalty S}^{-n}\mathscr{L}_{i}
, is
\mathscr{O}
-quasi-coherent for every
i
, so is
\mathscr{L}_{|U}(*Y)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigcup}\displaylimits_{i}\mathscr{L}_{i}(*Y)
.

2.

The question is local. Since
\mathscr{L}
is an
\mathscr{O}
-quasi-coherent module supported on
Y\times\penalty S
, we can suppose (up to shrinking the neighborhood) that it is a direct limit
\mathscr{L}\!=\!\mathop{\textstyle\bigcup}\displaylimits_{i}\mathscr{L}_{i}
of an increasing sequence of
\mathscr{O}
-coherent submodules
\mathscr{L}_{i}
which are supported on
Y\times\penalty S
. Therefore
\mathscr{L}_{i}(*Y)=0
for every
i
and thus
\mathscr{L}(*Y)=\mathop{\textstyle\bigcup}\displaylimits_{i}\mathscr{L}_{i}(*Y)=0
.

3.

Let us denote by
\mathscr{L}
and
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}
respectively the kernel and the cokernel of
\phi
. They are
\mathscr{O}
-quasi-coherent modules. If the restriction of
\phi
to
X^{*}\times\penalty S
is an isomorphism, they are supported on
Y\times\penalty S
. By applying the localization functor along
Y
, we get the following exact sequence of
\mathscr{O}
-quasi-coherent modules (by the first point)

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{L}(*Y)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{M}(*Y)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{N}(*Y)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(*Y)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0.

By hypothesis,
\mathscr{M}(*Y)\simeq\mathscr{M}
and
\mathscr{N}(*Y)\simeq\mathscr{N}
, thus

\mathscr{L}(*Y)\simeq\mathscr{L}
,
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}(*Y)\simeq\mathscr{L}^{\prime}
and by the previous point
\mathscr{L}
and
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}
are zero.
It
\phi_{|X^{*}\times\penalty S}
is zero,
\mathscr{L}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{M}
is an isomorphism on
X^{*}\times\penalty S
, hence it is an isomorphism and so
\phi
is
zero.∎

Let
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)
be the coherent sheaf of rings
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*Y)\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
. Then any coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
- or
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)
-module is
\mathscr{O}
-quasi-coherent. On the other hand, any
\mathscr{O}
-quasi-coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-submodule
\mathscr{N}
of a coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module
\mathscr{M}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-coherent. The next lemma follows by an easy adaptation of
[
Kas03
, Prop. 4.23]
.

Lemma 2.4
.

1.

The category of coherent,
resp.

\mathscr{O}
-good,
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules, is abelian and stable by extensions in
\mathrm{Mod}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
, and
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
,
resp.

\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{good}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
, is a triangulated full subcategory of
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
.

2.

Let
\mathscr{M}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}
be a bounded complex of
\mathscr{O}
-good
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules. Then each cohomology
\mathscr{H}^{\ell}(\mathscr{M}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})
is
\mathscr{O}
-good on
X\times\penalty S
.

3.

On any relatively compact open subset
U\!\Subset\!X\times\penalty S
, each good
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module
\mathscr{N}
comes in an exact sequence

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{N}^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}}\mathscr{L}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{N}_{|U}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0

with
\mathscr{L}
being
\mathscr{O}_{U}
-coherent and
\mathscr{N}^{\prime}
being good on
U
. In particular,
\mathscr{N}_{|U}
has a coherent
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-filtration.

Remark 2.5
.

1.

With a slight adaptation of the proof of Lemma
2.3
(
1
) we conclude that if
\mathscr{M}
is good (
resp.

\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-coherent) then
\mathscr{M}(*Y)
is
\mathscr{O}
-good (
resp.

\mathscr{O}
-quasi-coherent).

2.

If a local section
m
of an
\mathscr{O}
-quasi-coherent module satisfying
\mathscr{M}=\mathscr{M}(*Y)
is zero when restricted to
X^{*}\times\penalty S
, then it is zero. Indeed if
m
is defined on
U\times U_{S}
,
m
generates an
\mathscr{O}
-coherent submodule of
\mathscr{M}(*Y)_{|U\times U_{S}}
which is supported on
Y\times\penalty S
; given a local defining equation
f=0
for
Y
, we have
f^{k}m=0
for some
k
, thus
m=0
.

Characteristic varieties

To any object
\mathscr{M}
of
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
is associated, by means of local coherent
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-filtrations, its characteristic variety
\Char(\mathscr{M})
, which is contained in
T^{*}X\times S
. A coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module is
holonomic
if its characteristic variety is contained in
\Lambda\times S
, with
\Lambda
closed complex analytic Lagrangian
\mathbb{C}^{*}
-homogeneous in
T^{*}X
. Correspondingly are defined the derived categories
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
and
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{hol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
and the characteristic variety
\Char(\mathscr{M})
for such objects
\mathscr{M}
.

The structure of the characteristic variety of a holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module
\mathscr{M}
is described in
[
FMF18
, Lem. 2.10]
: for each irreducible component
\Lambda_{i}
of
\Lambda
(
i\in I
) there exists a locally finite family
(T_{ij})_{j\in J_{i}}
of closed analytic subsets of
S
such that

\Char(\mathscr{M})=\mathop{\textstyle\bigcup}\displaylimits_{i\in I}\mathop{\textstyle\bigcup}\displaylimits_{j\in J_{i}}\Lambda_{i}\times T_{ij}=\mathop{\textstyle\bigcup}\displaylimits_{i\in I}\Lambda_{i}\times T_{i}\qquad\hbox{with }T_{i}=\mathop{\textstyle\bigcup}\displaylimits_{j}T_{ij}.

(2.6)

The projection to
X
of
\Lambda_{i}
is an irreducible closed analytic subset of
X
that we denote by
Z_{i}
. These subsets form a locally finite family of closed analytic subsets of
X
. We have

\Supp(\mathscr{M})=\mathop{\textstyle\bigcup}\displaylimits_{i\in I}Z_{i}\times T_{i},

(2.7)

and we set

\Supp_{X}(\mathscr{M})=\mathop{\textstyle\bigcup}\displaylimits_{i\in I}Z_{i},\quad\Supp_{S}(\mathscr{M})=\mathop{\textstyle\bigcup}\displaylimits_{i\in I}T_{i},

that we call respectively the
X
-support (which is a closed analytic subset of
X
) and the
S
-support of
\mathscr{M}
(which may be not closed analytic if
I
is infinite). Anyway, we set
\dim\Supp_{S}(\mathscr{M})=\max_{i,j}\dim T_{ij}\leqslant\dim S
.

Any
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-coherent submodule or quotient module of
\mathscr{M}
is holonomic and its characteristic variety is the union of some irreducible components of
\Char(\mathscr{M})
.

Lemma 2.8
.

The category
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{hol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
of holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules is closed under taking extensions in the category
\mathrm{Mod}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
, and under taking sub-quotients in the category
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
.

We say that a local section
m
of a
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module
\mathscr{M}
is an
S
-torsion section
if it is annihilated by some holomorphic function on
S
. The
S
-torsion submodule

\mathscr{M}_{\mathrm{t}}
of
\mathscr{M}
is the submodule consisting of
S
-torsion local sections. Note that if
\mathscr{M}
is a holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module then
the
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-submodule
\mathscr{M}_{\mathrm{t}}
is holonomic since it is an
\mathscr{O}
-quasi coherent submodule of
\mathscr{M}
. We say that
\mathscr{M}
is
S
-torsion-free
if
\mathscr{M}_{\mathrm{t}}=0
. We denote by
\mathscr{M}_{\mathrm{tf}}:\mathscr{M}/\mathscr{M}_{\mathrm{t}}
the torsion-free quotient.

We recall that the duality functor
\boldsymbol{D}
for
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules was considered in
[
MFS13
, Def. 3.4]
and that
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{hol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
is stable under duality which is an involution.

2.b
Behaviour with respect to pullback, pushforward and external product

Notation 2.9
.

1.

For a holomorphic map
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X^{\prime}
, we also denote by
f
the morphism of complex manifolds
f\times\id:X\times\penalty S\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X^{\prime}\times\penalty S
and by
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f^{*}
and
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}
the pullback and pushforward functors in the derived category of relative
\mathscr{D}
-modules.

2.

For a morphism
\pi:S\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S^{\prime}
between analytic spaces, we denote by
\pi^{*}
and
R\pi_{*}
the natural extension to the category of relative
\mathscr{D}
-modules of the similar functors defined on the categories of
\mathscr{O}
-modules

Definition 2.10
.

We say that a
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module
\mathscr{M}
is
f
-(
\mathscr{O}
-)good
(
resp.

\pi
-(
\mathscr{O}
-)good
) if it is (
\mathscr{O}
-)good in some neighborhood of each fiber of
f
(
resp.

\pi
).

We recall results concerning the behaviour with respect to a morphism of complex manifolds.

Proposition 2.11
.

Let
\mathscr{M}
be a coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module.

1.

If
f:X^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X
is a holomorphic map of complex manifolds then, for each
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\mathscr{H}^{\ell}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f^{*}\mathscr{M}
is an
f
-
\mathscr{O}
-good
\mathscr{D}_{X^{\prime}\times\penalty S/S}
-module.

2.

Let
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X^{\prime}
be a proper holomorphic map. If
\mathscr{M}
is
f
-good (
resp.

f
-good and holonomic), then for each
\ell
,
\mathscr{H}^{\ell}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\mathscr{M}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X^{\prime}\times\penalty S/S}
-coherent (
resp.
holonomic).

Proof.

We refer to
[
SS94
, Th. 4.2 & Cor. 4.3]
for the proof of (
2
) (or one can adapt the proof of
[
Kas03
, Th. 4.25 & 4.27]
), while for the proof of (
1
) we can easily adapt the argument of
[
LS87
, Prop. 2.1]
which gives the absolute case.
∎

Base pullback

Let
\pi:S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
be a morphism of complex analytic manifolds. We also denote by
\pi
the induced map
\id\times\pi:X\times\penalty S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X\times\penalty S
. We have

\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}=\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}\otimes_{\pi^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}}\pi^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}=:\pi^{*}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S},

(2.12)

hence
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
is a right
\pi^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module. One then defines in a natural way the functor
L\pi^{*}:\rD(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rD(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})
as
L\pi^{*}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})=\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}\otimes^{L}_{\pi^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})
.

The following is straightforward.

Lemma 2.13
.

Let
\mathscr{L}
be an
\mathscr{O}
-quasi-coherent
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-module. Then each cohomology sheaf
L^{j}\pi^{*}\mathscr{L}
is
\pi
-
\mathscr{O}
-good.

Lemma 2.14
.

The functor
L\pi^{*}
induces a functor
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{hol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{hol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})
. For a holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module
\mathscr{M}
and for each
j
, the characteristic variety
\Char L^{j}\pi^{*}\mathscr{M}
is contained in the pullback
\pi^{-1}\Char\mathscr{M}
(here,
\pi
denotes the map
T^{*}X\times S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}T^{*}X\times S
).

Proof.

By using that, locally, a coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module has a resolution of length
2\dim X
by free
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules of finite rank, one gets the first point for
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
. It suffices thus to prove the second point, which is a local question. One can find such a resolution which is strictly compatible with coherent filtrations relative to
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
. In such a way, one finds that for each
j
,
\Char L^{j}\pi^{*}\mathscr{M}
is contained in the support of
L\pi^{*}\mathrm{gr}^{F}\mathscr{M}
, hence the assertion.
∎

Base pushforward

We now consider pushforward by a proper morphism
\pi:S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
. Owing to (
2.12
), we have a natural morphism
\pi^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
which, by adjunction, entails a natural morphism
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\pi_{*}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
.

We recall
[
MFS19
, Prop. 1.6]
:

Proposition 2.15
.

Assume that
\pi
is proper and that
\mathscr{M}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
-holonomic and
\pi
-good. Then, for each
j
,
R^{j}\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-holonomic with characteristic variety contained in
\pi(\Char\mathscr{M})
.

An adjunction formula

Let
\pi:S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
be a morphism of complex manifolds. We will make use of the following adjunction formula.

Lemma 2.16
.

Let
\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N}
be objects of
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
. Then there is a bi-functorial isomorphism in

\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
:

R\pi_{*}R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}}(L\pi^{*}\mathscr{M},L\pi^{*}\mathscr{N})\simeq R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},R\pi_{*}L\pi^{*}\mathscr{N}).

(2.16
\,*
)

If
\mathscr{N}
is a coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module, there are functorial morphisms in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
:

\mathscr{N}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R\pi_{*}L\pi^{*}\mathscr{N}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R\pi_{*}\pi^{*}\mathscr{N},

(2.16
\,**
)

and if
\pi
is proper,
R\pi_{*}L\pi^{*}\mathscr{N}\simeq(R\pi_{*}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})\otimes^{L}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}\mathscr{N}
belongs to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
.

Proof.

We have (cf.
[
Kas03
, p. 241]
)

R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}}(L\pi^{*}\mathscr{M},L\pi^{*}\mathscr{N})\simeq R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\pi^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\pi^{-1}\mathscr{M},L\pi^{*}\mathscr{N}),

hence (
2.16

∗

) is obtained by adjunction (cf.
[
KS90
, (2.6.15)]
).
By setting
\mathscr{M}=\mathscr{N}
in (
2.16

∗

) we get
\Hom_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}}(L\pi^{*}\mathscr{N},L\pi^{*}\mathscr{N})\simeq\Hom_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{N},R\pi_{*}L\pi^{*}\mathscr{N})
. The image of
\id
by this isomorphism is the first morphism in (
2.16

∗
⁣
∗

) while the natural morphism
L\pi^{*}\mathscr{N}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\pi^{*}\mathscr{N}
in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})
provides the desired morphism
R\pi_{*}L\pi^{*}\mathscr{N}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R\pi_{*}\pi^{*}\mathscr{N}
. The last isomorphism is obtained by the projection formula (cf.
[
KS90
, Prop. 2.6.6]
).
∎

Remark 2.17

((External) tensor product)
.

If
\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N}\in\rD^{-}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
, the tensor product

\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}}^{L}\mathscr{N}\in\rD^{-}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})

is isomorphic to the pullback
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}\delta^{*}(\mathscr{M}\boxtimes^{L}_{\mathscr{D}}\mathscr{N})
of the
S
-external tensor product (recall that
\mathscr{D}_{X\times X\times\penalty S/S}
is flat over
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}\boxtimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
)

\mathscr{M}\boxtimes^{L}_{\mathscr{D}}\mathscr{N}:=\mathscr{D}_{X\times X\times\penalty S/S}\otimes_{(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}\boxtimes_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})}(\mathscr{M}\boxtimes^{L}_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{N})

by the diagonal embedding
\delta:X\times\penalty S\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}(X\times X)\times S
over
S
.
If
\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N}\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
,
\mathscr{M}\boxtimes^{L}_{\mathscr{D}}\mathscr{N}
belongs to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times X\times\penalty S/S})
and we have

C:=\Char(\mathscr{M}\boxtimes^{L}_{\mathscr{D}}\mathscr{N})\subset\Char(\mathscr{M})\times_{S}\Char(\mathscr{N}).

This is seen by considering local resolutions of
\mathscr{M}

resp.

\mathscr{N}
by free
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules, showing both inclusions
C\subset T^{*}X\times\Char(\mathscr{N})
and
C\subset\Char(\mathscr{M})\times T^{*}X
. Therefore, if
\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N}
are holonomic, so is
\mathscr{M}\boxtimes^{L}_{\mathscr{D}}\mathscr{N}
.

3
Regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules

In the special case of Lemma
2.14
where
\pi
is the inclusion
i_{s_{o}}:\{s_{o}\}\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}S
of a point
s_{o}
in
S
, we recall a consequence of
[
Kas03
, (A.10)]
:

Proposition 3.1

(cf.
[
MFS13
, Prop. 3.1]
)
.

For any
\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N}
in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
, for any
s_{o}\in S
, the natural morphism

Li^{*}_{s_{o}}R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{i^{*}_{s_{o}}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(Li^{*}_{s_{o}}\mathscr{M},Li^{*}_{s_{o}}\mathscr{N})

is an isomorphism in
\rD(\mathbb{C}_{X})
.

3.a
Characterization of relative regular holonomicity

The category of
regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules
was introduced in
[
MFS19
]
as well as the full subcategory
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
of
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{hol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
of bounded complexes of
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules having regular holonomic cohomology. According to
[
MFS19
]
, we say that an object
\mathscr{M}\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{hol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
is
regular
if it satisfies

(\mathrm{Reg}\,1)
For each
s_{o}\in S
and any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
,
Li^{*}_{s_{o}}\mathscr{H}^{j}(\mathscr{M})\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X})
.

An alternative and natural property of regularity would be the following:

(\mathrm{Reg}\,2)
For each
s_{o}\in S
,
Li^{*}_{s_{o}}\mathscr{M}\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X})
.

Regularity in either sense is the same property for objects of
\mathrm{Mod}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
. Property
(\mathrm{Reg}\,1)
is by definition compatible with the truncation functors while Property

(\mathrm{Reg}\,2)
is compatible with base change on
S
, meaning that, for any morphism
\pi:S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
of complex manifolds and any object
\mathscr{M}\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{hol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})

which satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg}\,2)
, the pullback
L\pi^{\ast}(\mathscr{M})\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{hol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X^{\prime}\times\penalty S/S})

satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg}\,2)
too. We enlarge the setting for further use since both conditions make sense for any complex in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
.

Proposition 3.2
.

Let
Y
be a hypersurface of
X
.
On any complex manifold
S
,

i.

for a complex in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
(
resp.
in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y))
), the condition
(\mathrm{Reg}\,1)
is equivalent to
(\mathrm{Reg}\,2)
, and we denote both by
(\mathrm{Reg})
,

ii.

the category of coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
(
resp.
coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)
)-modules satisfying
(\mathrm{Reg})
is closed under taking extensions in the category
\mathrm{Mod}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
(
resp.
in
\mathrm{Mod}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y))
) and sub-quotients in the category
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
(
resp.

\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y))
),

iii.

the category of regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules is closed under taking extensions in
\mathrm{Mod}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
and sub-quotients in
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
.

We note that (
iii
) follows from (
i
) and (
ii
) together with Lemma
2.8
, so that we will focus on the latter properties. The proof in both cases is the same because it is based on the coherence of the rings involved. We provide it in the localized case.

Lemma 3.3
.

Condition
(\mathrm{Reg}\,1)
implies
(\mathrm{Reg}\,2)
.

Proof.

We argue by induction on the amplitude of the complex
\mathscr{M}
.
We may assume that
\mathscr{M}\in\rD^{\geqslant 0}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y))

and we consider the following distinguished triangle

\mathscr{H}^{0}\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\tau^{\geqslant 1}\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt+1\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{},

(3.4)

where
\tau^{\geqslant 1}
is the truncation functor with respect to the natural
t
-structure on

\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y))
.
Let us assume that
\mathscr{M}
satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg}\,1)
, hence
by definition and induction, both
\mathscr{H}^{0}\mathscr{M}
and
\tau^{\geqslant 1}\mathscr{M}
satisfy
(\mathrm{Reg}\,1)
.
As remarked,
\mathscr{H}^{0}\mathscr{M}
satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg}\,2)
too and by induction on the amplitude of
\mathscr{M}
,

\tau^{\geqslant 1}\mathscr{M}
satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg}\,2)
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.2
.

For
d\geqslant 0
, we denote by
(\mathrm{Reg}\,1)_{d}
,
resp.

(\mathrm{Reg}\,2)_{d}
, the corresponding condition for
\dim S\leqslant d
.
If
\dim S=0
,
\eqref{Prop:21}_{0}
holds true, and
\eqref{Prop:22}_{0}
is proved e.g. in
[
Bjö93
, Th. 5.3.4]
. We thus assume from now on that
d\geqslant 1
and we proceed by induction on
d:=\dim S
, denoting by (
i
)
d
and
(
ii
)
d
the statements of the proposition restricted to
\dim S\leqslant d
. We will prove the following implications for
d\geqslant 1
:

a.

\eqref{Prop:21}_{d-1}\wedge\eqref{Prop:22}_{d-1}\Longrightarrow\eqref{Prop:21}_{d}
;

b.

\eqref{Prop:21}_{d}\wedge\eqref{Prop:22}_{d-1}\Longrightarrow\eqref{Prop:22}_{d}
.

Let us start with (
a
). Assuming that both
\eqref{Prop:21}_{d-1}
and
\eqref{Prop:22}_{d-1}
hold, we have to prove that
(\mathrm{Reg}\,2)_{d}\Rightarrow(\mathrm{Reg}\,1)_{d}
.
Due to the induction hypothesis
\eqref{Prop:21}_{d-1}
we simply write
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d-1}
for either

(\mathrm{Reg}\,1)_{d-1}
or
(\mathrm{Reg}\,2)_{d-1}
.

We note that
\mathscr{M}\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y))
satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg}\,2)_{d}
if and only if for each smooth codimension-one germ
(H,s_{o})\subset(S,s_{o})
,
Li_{H}^{*}\mathscr{M}
satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d-1}
. It is then enough to prove that, for such an
\mathscr{M}
,
Li_{H}^{*}\mathscr{H}^{j}\mathscr{M}
satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d-1}
for any
j
and
H
. We shall argue by induction on the amplitude of
\mathscr{M}
.
We may assume that
\mathscr{M}\in\rD^{\geqslant 0}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y))
and we consider the distinguished triangle (
3.4
).
We deduce an isomorphism

\mathscr{H}^{-1}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{H}^{0}\mathscr{M}\simeq\mathscr{H}^{-1}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}

(3.5)

and an exact sequence

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{H}^{0}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{H}^{0}\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{H}^{0}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{H}^{0}Li^{*}_{H}\tau^{\geqslant 1}\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0.

(Note that
\mathscr{H}^{k}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{H}^{0}\mathscr{M}=0
for
k\neq 0,-1
.)
Since
Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}
satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d-1}
, so does
\mathscr{H}^{k}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}
(
k=-1,0
), and so does
\mathscr{H}^{-1}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{H}^{0}\mathscr{M}
by (
3.5
). Since
(
ii
)
d-1
is assumed to hold,
any coherent sub-quotient of
\mathscr{H}^{0}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}

satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d-1}
, hence so does
\mathscr{H}^{0}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{H}^{0}\mathscr{M}
,
which proves, following Lemma
3.3
, that
Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{H}^{0}\mathscr{M}
satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d-1}
.
Thus
\mathscr{H}^{0}\mathscr{M}
satisfies

(\mathrm{Reg}\,2)_{d}

and, by the distinguished triangle (
3.4
),

\tau^{\geqslant 1}\mathscr{M}
satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg}\,2)_{d}
.
Induction on the cohomological length applied to
\tau^{\geqslant 1}\mathscr{M}
implies that
Li_{H}^{*}\mathscr{H}^{j}\mathscr{M}
satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d-1}
for any
j\geqslant 1
, which concludes the proof of (
a
) and we now simply write
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d}
.

Let us now prove (
b
). The extension property in
\eqref{Prop:22}_{d}
is clear. Let us consider stability by sub-quotients in
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y))
. Let
\mathscr{M}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y))
satisfy
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d}
. Given any short exact sequence

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{M}_{1}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{M}_{2}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0

(3.6)

of coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)
-modules, we wish to prove that
\mathscr{M}_{1}
and
\mathscr{M}_{2}
satisfy
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d}
. Owing to our assumption on
\mathscr{M}
,
Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}
satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d-1}
for any smooth codimension-one germ
(H,s_{o})\subset(S,s_{o})
, and it is enough to prove that either
Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}_{1}
or
Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}_{2}
satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d-1}
. From (
3.6
) we obtain the long exact sequence:

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{H}^{-1}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}_{1}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{H}^{-1}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{H}^{-1}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}_{2}\\
\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{H}^{0}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}_{1}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{H}^{0}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{H}^{0}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}_{2}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0.

(3.7)

Since
Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}
satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d-1}
then so do
\mathscr{H}^{-1}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}_{1}
and
\mathscr{H}^{0}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}_{2}
owing to
\eqref{Prop:22}_{d-1}
, and it remains to be proved that either
\mathscr{H}^{-1}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}_{2}
, or
\mathscr{H}^{0}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}_{1}
, satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d-1}
. Let
s
be a local coordinate on
S
vanishing on
H
.

Let us denote by
\mathscr{M}^{\prime}
the pullback of
\mathscr{M}^{\prime}_{2}:=\mathrm{tors}_{H}(\mathscr{M}_{2})
in
\mathscr{M}
and by
\mathscr{M}_{2,\mathrm{tf}}
the quotient
\mathscr{M}_{2}/\mathscr{M}^{\prime}_{2}
. The commutative diagram below is Cartesian and its columns and rows are short exact sequences:

\begin{array}[]{c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern (3.8)

Since
\mathscr{M}_{2,\mathrm{tf}}
is
i^{*}_{H}
-acyclic, the exact sequence (
3.7
) for the middle column splits as

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{-1}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathscr{H}^{-1}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M},

\displaystyle 0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{H}^{0}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{H}^{0}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{H}^{0}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}_{2,\mathrm{tf}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0,

which implies that
Li_{H}^{*}\mathscr{M}^{\prime}
and
Li_{H}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{2,\mathrm{tf}}
satisfy
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d-1}
by
\eqref{Prop:22}_{d-1}
, hence
\mathscr{M}^{\prime}
and
\mathscr{M}_{2,\mathrm{tf}}
satisfy
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d}
. We now prove that
Li_{H}^{*}\mathscr{M}^{\prime}_{2}
satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d-1}
, that will conclude the proof. Since
\mathscr{M}^{\prime}_{2}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)
-coherent, there exists locally an integer
k\geqslant 1
such that
s^{k}\mathscr{M}^{\prime}_{2}=\penalty 0
.
We will prove by induction on
k
that any
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)
-coherent torsion quotient of a
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)
-coherent module satisfying
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d}
,
satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d}
too.

If
k=1
, we have
\mathscr{H}^{-1}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}^{\prime}_{2}\simeq\mathscr{H}^{0}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}^{\prime}_{2}
, and, by
\eqref{Prop:22}_{d-1}
the latter satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d-1}
, being a quotient of
\mathscr{H}^{0}Li^{*}_{H}\mathscr{M}^{\prime}
.

If
k>1
, we argue with the following Cartesian commutative diagram, analogous to (
3.8
):

78.83682pt\raise-22.24419pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 87.82294pt\raise-36.47055pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 131.3368pt\raise-22.24419pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 179.83334pt\raise-22.24419pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise
