141.16321pt\raise-33.36627pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle{s}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 141.16321pt\raise-36.47055pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 179.83334pt\raise-22.24419pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-44.48837pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 36.3264pt\raise-44.48837pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 73.1528pt\raise-44.48837pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{s\cdot\mathscr{M}^{\prime}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\kern 126.49307pt\raise-44.48837pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{s\cdot\mathscr{M}^{\prime}_{2}}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 182.33334pt\raise-44.48837pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}

By the induction hypothesis on
k
,
Li_{H}^{*}(s\cdot\mathscr{M}^{\prime}_{2})
satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d-1}
since
s\cdot\mathscr{M}^{\prime}_{2}
is a
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)
-coherent quotient of
\mathscr{M}^{\prime}
which is annihilated by
s^{k-1}
. It follows that, by considering the middle vertical sequence, so does
Li_{H}^{*}\mathscr{M}^{\prime\prime}
. Since
\mathscr{M}^{\prime\prime}_{2}
is a
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)
-coherent quotient of
\mathscr{M}^{\prime\prime}
annihilated by
s
,
Li_{H}^{*}(\mathscr{M}^{\prime\prime}_{2})
satisfies
(\mathrm{Reg})_{d-1}
. It follows that, by considering the first horizontal sequence, so does
Li_{H}^{*}\mathscr{M}_{1}
, hence finally, by considering the middle horizontal sequence, so does
Li_{H}^{*}\mathscr{M}^{\prime}_{2}
, as wanted.
∎

Corollary 3.9
.

The category
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
is a full triangulated subcategory of
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
stable by duality.

Proof.

We note that
(\mathrm{Reg}\,2)
implies that
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
is a full triangulated subcategory of
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{hol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
. Since the latter is a full triangulated subcategory of
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
, the first assertion follows. Stability by duality follows from the same property in the absolute case (cf. e.g.
[
Bjö93
, Th. 5.4.15 (4)]
), together with the isomorphism
Li^{*}_{s_{o}}\boldsymbol{D}\mathscr{M}\simeq\boldsymbol{D}Li^{*}_{s_{o}}\mathscr{M}
, which follows from Proposition
3.1
.
∎

3.b
Stability of regular holonomicity under base pullback and base pushforward

For a proper morphism
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y
, it has been shown in
[
MFS19
, Cor. 2.4]
that if
\mathscr{M}
is an object of
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
with
f
-good cohomology, then
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\mathscr{M}
belongs to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S/S})
. On the other hand, stability of regular holonomicity (and hence its coherence) by pullback
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f^{*}
has been shown in
[
FMFS21
]
only if
\dim S=1
as a consequence of the Riemann-Hilbert correspondence proved there. This will be obtained in general by the proof of Theorem
1
in Section
4.e
. In this section, we consider on the other hand the behaviour with respect to base pullback and pushforward.

Proposition 3.10

(Stability under base pullback)
.

Let
\pi:S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
be a morphism of complex manifolds and let
\mathscr{M}
be an object of
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
. Then
L\pi^{*}\mathscr{M}
belongs to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})
.

Proof.

We already know that
L\pi^{*}\mathscr{M}
belongs to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{hol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})
by Lemma
2.14
. Regularity follows from the isomorphism of functors
Li_{s^{\prime}_{o}}^{*}L\pi^{*}\simeq Li_{\pi(s^{\prime}_{o})}^{*}
for any
s^{\prime}_{o}\in\penalty S^{\prime}
.
∎

Let
t_{o}
be a
fat point
of
S
, that is, a complex subspace of
S
supported on a reduced point
|t_{o}|\in S
. In other words, the ideal
\mathscr{I}_{t_{o}}\subset\mathscr{O}_{S}
, which satisfies
\mathscr{I}_{t_{o}}\subset\mathfrak{m}_{|t_{o}|}
(which is the ideal associated to

|t_{o}|
such that its fiber in
|t_{o}|
is the maximal ideal of
\mathscr{O}_{S,|t_{o}|}
),
contains some power
\mathfrak{m}_{|t_{o}|}^{k}
.
By abuse of notation we will still denote by
\mathscr{I}_{t_{o}}
(
resp.

\mathfrak{m}_{|t_{o}|}
) the ideal
\mathscr{I}_{t_{o},|t_{o}|}
(
resp.

\mathfrak{m}_{|t_{o}|,|t_{o}|}
) and also the sheaf
p^{-1}\mathscr{I}_{t_{o}}

(
resp.

p^{-1}\mathfrak{m}_{|t_{o}|}
).
Let
i_{t_{o}}:t_{o}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
denote the natural morphism of complex spaces defined by the surjective morphism
\mathscr{O}_{S}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{O}_{S}/\mathscr{I}_{t_{o}}
. For an
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-module,
resp.
a
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module
\mathscr{M}
, we set

i_{t_{o}}^{*}\mathscr{M}:=(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}/p^{-1}\mathscr{I}_{t_{o}})\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{M},

that we regard in a natural way as a
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module since
\mathscr{O}_{S,|t_{o}|}/\mathscr{I}_{t_{o}}
is a finite-dimensional vector space. We define thereby the pullback functor

Li_{t_{o}}^{*}:\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{D}_{X}).

We note that, endowed with its natural structure of
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module,
i_{t_{o}}^{*}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
is coherent. As a consequence, if
\mathscr{M}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-coherent, then
Li_{t_{o}}^{*}\mathscr{M}
has
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-coherent cohomology. In other words,
Li_{t_{o}}^{*}
induces a functor
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X})
.

Corollary 3.11
.

Let
\mathscr{M}
be an object of
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
. Then, for any fat point
t_{o}
of
S
,
Li_{t_{o}}^{*}\mathscr{M}
belongs to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X})
if and only if
Li_{|t_{o}|}^{*}\mathscr{M}
does so.

Proof.

For
k\geqslant 0
we set
\mathscr{I}_{k}=\mathscr{I}_{t_{o}}\cap\mathfrak{m}_{|t_{o}|}^{k}
(with
\mathfrak{m}_{|t_{o}|}^{0}:=\mathscr{O}_{S}
), so that
\mathscr{I}_{1}=\mathscr{I}_{t_{o}}
and
\mathscr{I}_{k}=\mathfrak{m}_{|t_{o}|}^{k}
for
k
large enough. It is enough to prove that
p^{-1}(\mathscr{I}_{k}/\mathscr{I}_{k+1})\otimes^{L}_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{M}
belongs to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X})
for any
k
if and only if
Li_{|t_{o}|}^{*}\mathscr{M}
does so. Since the sheaf
\mathscr{I}_{k}/\mathscr{I}_{k+1}
is an
\mathscr{O}_{S}/\mathfrak{m}_{|t_{o}|}
-module
supported on
|t_{o}|
whose fiber is a finite dimensional vector space, we have

\displaystyle p^{-1}(\mathscr{I}_{k}/\mathscr{I}_{k+1})\otimes^{L}_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{M}

\displaystyle\simeq p^{-1}(\mathscr{I}_{k}/\mathscr{I}_{k+1})\otimes^{L}_{p^{-1}(\mathscr{O}_{S}/\mathfrak{m}_{|t_{o}|})}(p^{-1}(\mathscr{O}_{S}/\mathfrak{m}_{|t_{o}|})\otimes^{L}_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{M})

\displaystyle=(\mathscr{I}_{k}/\mathscr{I}_{k+1})\otimes Li^{*}_{|t_{o}|}\mathscr{M},

and the conclusion follows.
∎

Setting 3.12
.

We consider a local setting where
S
is a polydisc
\Delta^{d}
written as
\Delta^{d-1}\!\times\!\penalty\Delta=S^{\prime}\times\Delta
, and we denote by
q:S\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S^{\prime}
the projection
(s^{\prime},t)\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}s^{\prime}
, and we keep the same notation after taking the product with
X
. Recall (cf. e.g.
[
Kas03
, Prop. A.14]
) that the sheaves of rings
q^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}
,
q^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}
and
q^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
are Noetherian.

Let
h(s^{\prime},t)=t^{k}+\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}h_{i}(s^{\prime})t^{i}
be a Weierstrass polynomial, with
h_{i}
holomorphic on
S^{\prime}
and let
T=h^{-1}(0)
. The equivalence between the categories of

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

coherent
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-modules supported on
T
,

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

h
-nilpotent coherent
q^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}[t]
-modules,

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

h
-nilpotent
q^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}[t]
-modules which are
q^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}
-coherent,

extends in a natural way to
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
, to
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
and to
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)
for a given hypersurface
Y
of
X
. For example, in one direction, if
\mathscr{M}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-coherent and supported on
X\times\penalty T
, then
\mathscr{M}
is
q^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
-coherent and each local section is annihilated by some power of
h
. By taking local
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-generators of
\mathscr{M}
, we conclude that there exists locally an integer
\ell\geqslant 1
such that
h^{\ell}\mathscr{M}=0
. Conversely, if
\mathscr{M}
satisfies the latter property, we can regard it as an
h
-nilpotent coherent
q^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}[t]
-module and the associated
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}\otimes_{q^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}[t]}\mathscr{M}
.

By definition, the characteristic variety of a coherent
q^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
-module is (locally) the support in
(T^{*}X)\times S
of the graded coherent
q^{-1}\mathrm{gr}^{F}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
-module with respect to any local coherent
q^{-1}F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
-filtration. Such a module is said to be holonomic if this support is contained in
\Lambda\times S
for some Lagrangian variety
\Lambda\subset T^{*}X
.

Remark 3.13

(Holonomic and regular holonomic
q^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
-modules)
.

Given a coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module
\mathscr{M}
supported on
T
as above, one checks that a coherent
F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-filtration is also a coherent
q^{-1}F_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
-filtration by the above correspondence. As a consequence, such a module is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-holonomic if and only if it is
q^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
-holonomic.

On the other hand, we say that a holonomic
q^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
-module
\mathscr{M}
is regular if, for any
s^{\prime}_{o}\in S^{\prime}
and any
s_{o}\in q^{-1}(s^{\prime}_{o})
, the holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module
i_{s_{o}}^{-1}(Li_{q^{-1}(s^{\prime}_{o})}^{*}\mathscr{M})
is regular.

Corollary 3.14
.

A coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module
\mathscr{M}
supported on
X\times T
is regular holonomic if and only if, when regarded as a
q^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
-module, it is regular holonomic.

Proof.

By Remark
3.13
, we only need to check regularity, and we can suppose from the start that
\mathscr{M}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-holonomic supported on
X\times T
. We assume first that
\mathscr{M}
is
q^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
-regular. We wish to prove that
Li_{s_{o}}^{*}\mathscr{M}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-regular holonomic for any
s_{o}\in S
. It is enough to prove this for
s_{o}\in T
. Let us choose coordinates
(s^{\prime}_{1},\dots,s^{\prime}_{d-1},t)
of
S^{\prime}\times\Delta
centered at
s_{o}
. There exists
k\geqslant 1
such that
\mathscr{O}_{S}/(h^{k})\otimes^{L}_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{M}\simeq\mathscr{M}[1]\oplus\mathscr{M}
, since
h^{k}\mathscr{M}=0
for some
k\geqslant 1
, so, by the assumption,

\mathscr{O}_{S}/(s^{\prime}_{1},\dots,s^{\prime}_{d-1})\otimes^{L}_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\bigl[\mathscr{O}_{S}/(h^{k})\otimes^{L}_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{M}\bigr]=\mathscr{O}_{S}/(s^{\prime}_{1},\dots,s^{\prime}_{d-1},h^{k})\otimes^{L}_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{M}

is a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module. Since the support of
\mathscr{O}_{S}/(s^{\prime}_{1},\dots,s^{\prime}_{d-1},h^{k})
is a finite union of fat points in
S
(defined by the ideal generated by
h^{k}(0,t)
), one of which is supported at
s_{o}
, we conclude that
Li_{s_{o}}^{*}\mathscr{M}
is a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module by applying Corollary
3.11
. The converse is proved similarly.
∎

Theorem 3.15

(Stability under projective base pushforward)
.

Let
\pi:S\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S^{\prime}
be a projective morphism of complex manifolds and let
\mathscr{M}
be an object of
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
. Assume that the cohomology of
\mathscr{M}
is
\pi
-good. Then
R\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}
belongs to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})
.

Proof.

A standard consequence of Proposition
2.15
is that
R\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}
belongs to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{hol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}})
. Furthermore, we note that the question is local with respect to
X
and to
S^{\prime}
.

Step 1: Reduction to the case where
S^{\prime}
is a point

Since
\pi
is projective, we can regard
\pi
(locally with respect to
S^{\prime}
) as the composition of the inclusion
S\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}\mathbb{P}^{m}\times S^{\prime}
and the projection
\mathbb{P}^{m}\times S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S^{\prime}
for a suitable
m
. Moreover, we note that the result is easy if
\pi
is a closed embedding. We can thus assume that
\pi
is a projection
S=\mathbb{P}^{m}\times S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S^{\prime}
. For a complex
\mathscr{M}
in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
, proving the
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
-regularity of
R\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}
amounts to proving the
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-regularity of
Li_{s^{\prime}_{o}}^{*}R\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}
for any
s^{\prime}_{o}\in S^{\prime}
. Let
\mathfrak{m}_{s^{\prime}_{o}}
be the maximal ideal sheaf of
\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}
at
s^{\prime}_{o}
. Let us set
\mathbb{P}^{m}_{s^{\prime}_{o}}=\mathbb{P}^{m}\times\{s^{\prime}_{o}\}
and consider the following Cartesian square

\textstyle{X\times\mathbb{P}^{m}_{s^{\prime}_{o}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{i^{\prime}}
\textstyle{X\times\{s^{\prime}_{o}\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{i_{s^{\prime}_{o}}}
\textstyle{X\times\penalty S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}

Then we have

\displaystyle Li_{s^{\prime}_{o}}^{*}R\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}

\displaystyle=i_{s^{\prime}_{0}}^{-1}((\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}/p^{-1}(\mathfrak{m}_{s^{\prime}_{o}})\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}}^{L}R\pi_{*}\mathscr{M})

\displaystyle\simeq i^{-1}_{s^{\prime}_{o}}R\pi_{*}\bigl(\pi^{-1}(\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}/p^{-1}(\mathfrak{m}_{s^{\prime}_{o}})\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}})\otimes^{L}_{\pi^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}}\mathscr{M}\bigr)

\displaystyle\simeq R\pi^{\prime}_{*}{i^{\prime}}^{-1}\bigl(\pi^{-1}(\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}/p^{-1}(\mathfrak{m}_{s^{\prime}_{o}})\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}})\otimes^{L}_{\pi^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}}\mathscr{M}\bigr)

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{\simeq}R\pi^{\prime}_{*}\bigl(\mathscr{O}_{X\times\mathbb{P}^{m}_{s^{\prime}_{o}}}\otimes^{L}_{i^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}}i^{\prime-1}\mathscr{M}\bigr)

\displaystyle=R\pi^{\prime}_{*}(Li^{\prime*}\mathscr{M}),

where the isomorphism
(*)
follows by extension of scalars since
\mathscr{M}
is a complex of
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-modules. By Proposition
3.10
,
Li^{\prime*}\mathscr{M}
is a complex with regular holonomic cohomologies, so that if we know the theorem for
\pi^{\prime}
, we deduce it for
\pi
.

From now on, we assume that
S^{\prime}
is a point. In such a case,
S
is a projective space
\mathbb{P}^{m}
. Since the question is local with respect to
X
and since
S
is compact, we can assume that the sets
I
and
J_{i}
occurring in (
2.6
) are finite, so the
S
-support
T
of
\mathscr{M}
is a closed analytic subset of
S
. We argue by induction on the dimension of the
S
-support of
\mathscr{M}
.

Step 2: Case
\dim\Supp_{S}\mathscr{M}=0

If the
S
-support of
\mathscr{M}
has dimension zero, it consists of a finite number of points, and it is enough to consider the case where the support consists of one point
s_{o}\in S
. By a standard argument we may assume that
\mathscr{M}
is concentrated in degree zero and locally we can assume that there exists
k\geqslant 1
such that, denoting by
\mathfrak{m}_{s_{o}}
the maximal ideal sheaf of
s_{o}
in
S
, we have
\mathfrak{m}_{s_{o}}^{k}\mathscr{M}=0
. If we denote by
t_{o}
the fat point supported by
s_{o}
with ring
\mathscr{O}_{S}/\mathfrak{m}_{s_{o}}^{k}\mathscr{O}_{S}
, we conclude that
\mathscr{M}=i_{s_{o}*}i_{t_{o}}^{*}\mathscr{M}
. Therefore,
R\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}=R\pi_{*}i_{s_{o}*}i_{t_{o}}^{*}\mathscr{M}=i_{t_{o}}^{*}\mathscr{M}
. In this case, the theorem follows from Corollary
3.11
.

Step 3: Case
\dim\Supp_{S}\mathscr{M}\geqslant 1

We recall that, in this step,
\pi
is the constant map on
S=\mathbb{P}^{m}
.

(i) Proof of the regularity of
\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}

Let
d\geqslant 1
. We assume now that the statement holds true for any complex
\mathscr{M}^{\prime}
whose
S
-support has dimension
<d
and we aim at proving the same property for any complex
\mathscr{M}
with
S
-support
T
of dimension
d
. We may then reduce again to the case where
\mathscr{M}
is a single module.

We first prove that
\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}
is regular holonomic (instead of all modules
R^{k}\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}
).

One checks that the
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-submodule
\mathscr{M}^{\prime}
of
\mathscr{M}
consisting of local sections
m
such that the
S
-support of
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}\cdot m
has dimension
<d
is holonomic (denoting by
T_{<d}
the union of irreducible components of
T
of dimension
<d
,
\mathscr{M}^{\prime}
is locally defined as the sheaf of local sections annihilated by some power of the ideal
\mathscr{I}_{T_{<d}}
of
T_{<d}
in
\mathscr{O}_{S}
). By Proposition
3.2
(
iii
),
\mathscr{M}^{\prime}
is regular holonomic and, by the induction hypothesis,
\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}^{\prime}
is regular holonomic. It is thus enough to prove regularity of
\pi_{*}(\mathscr{M}/\mathscr{M}^{\prime})
, and we can likewise assume that

(3.16)

\mathscr{M}
has no nonzero coherent submodule with
S
-support of dimension
<d
.

Let us denote by
\Lambda
a Lagrangian subvariety of
T^{*}X
such that the characteristic variety of
\mathscr{M}
is contained in
\Lambda\times S
.
Since
T
is compact, it has a finite number of irreducible components and we index by
I_{d}
those which are of dimension
d
. We choose a point
s_{i}
on the smooth part of each
T_{i}
(
i\in I_{d}
). For the sake of simplicity, we denote by
\boldsymbol{s}_{o}
the finite set
\{s_{i}\mid i\in I_{d}\}
.

There is a natural morphism of
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-modules

\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}i_{\boldsymbol{s}_{o}}^{-1}\mathscr{M}

(3.17)

which associates to a section
m\in\Gamma(U;\pi_{*}\mathscr{M})=\Gamma(U\times S;\mathscr{M})
(
U
open in
X
) its germ along
U\times\boldsymbol{s}_{o}
. Since
\mathscr{M}
has no
S
-torsion supported in dimension
<d
, this morphism is
injective
. Indeed, let
x_{o}\in U\subseteq X
and let
m\in\Gamma(U\times S;\mathscr{M})
belong to the kernel, that is, such that its germ at
(x_{o},\boldsymbol{s}_{o})
vanishes, i.e.,
m
is zero in some neighborhood of
(x_{o},\boldsymbol{s}_{o})
that we write
U\times V
, up to shrinking
U
. Let us suppose by contradiction that
m\not=0
. Let us consider the coherent
\mathscr{D}_{U\times S/S}
-submodule
\mathscr{D}_{U\times S/S}\cdot m\subset\mathscr{M}_{|U\times S}
which is holonomic with
S
-support contained in
T
. By (
(3.16)
)
\mathscr{D}_{U\times S/S}\cdot m
has
S
-support of dimension
d
. Hence any irreducible
d
-dimensional component of its
S
-support is equal to some
T_{i}
for a suitable
i\in I_{d}
, but since
m
is zero on
U\times V
this is not possible.

Let
\mathfrak{m}_{\boldsymbol{s}_{o}}
denote the ideal sheaf of
\boldsymbol{s}_{o}
in
\mathscr{O}_{S}
. For each
k\geqslant 1
, we consider the induced morphism

\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}i_{\boldsymbol{s}_{o}}^{-1}\mathscr{M}/i_{\boldsymbol{s}_{o}}^{-1}\mathfrak{m}_{\boldsymbol{s}_{o}}^{k}\mathscr{M}.

The proposition will be proved if we prove that this morphism is injective for
k
large enough, since the right-hand side is regular holonomic by Corollary
3.11
.

Its kernel
\mathscr{N}_{k}
is a coherent, hence holonomic,
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-submodule of
\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}
and the sequence
(\mathscr{N}_{k})
is decreasing with characteristic variety contained in
\Lambda
. It is thus stationary. Let
\mathscr{N}\subset\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}
denote this constant value. We conclude that the map

\mathscr{N}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}i_{\boldsymbol{s}_{o}}^{-1}\mathscr{M}/i_{\boldsymbol{s}_{o}}^{-1}\mathfrak{m}_{\boldsymbol{s}_{o}}^{k}\mathscr{M}

is zero for any
k
. Since
\mathscr{N}=\mathscr{N}_{k}
for
k
large enough, we aim at proving that
\mathscr{N}=0
.

The image of
\mathscr{N}
by (
3.17
) is contained in
\mathop{\textstyle\bigcap}\displaylimits_{k}i_{\boldsymbol{s}_{o}}^{-1}\mathfrak{m}_{\boldsymbol{s}_{o}}^{k}\mathscr{M}
, and since (
3.17
) is injective, it suffices to prove that
\mathscr{M}^{\prime}:=\mathop{\textstyle\bigcap}\displaylimits_{k}i_{\boldsymbol{s}_{o}}^{-1}\mathfrak{m}_{\boldsymbol{s}_{o}}^{k}\mathscr{M}=0
. For
x_{o}\in X
, let us denote by
\mathscr{D}_{(x_{o},\boldsymbol{s}_{o})}
the germ at
(x_{o},\boldsymbol{s}_{o})
of
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
and similarly by
\mathscr{M}_{(x_{o},\boldsymbol{s}_{o})}
that of
\mathscr{M}
. It suffices thus to prove that, for all
x_{o}\in X
, the germ
\mathscr{M}^{\prime}_{(x_{o},\boldsymbol{s}_{o})}=\mathop{\textstyle\bigcap}\displaylimits_{k}\mathfrak{m}_{\boldsymbol{s}_{o}}^{k}\mathscr{M}_{(x_{o},\boldsymbol{s}_{o})}
is zero. We note that
\mathscr{M}^{\prime}_{(x_{o},\boldsymbol{s}_{o})}
is of finite type over
\mathscr{D}_{(x_{o},\boldsymbol{s}_{o})}
since the latter ring is Noetherian, hence
\mathscr{M}^{\prime}_{(x_{o},\boldsymbol{s}_{o})}
is holonomic with characteristic variety contained in
\Lambda_{x_{o}}\times(S,\boldsymbol{s}_{o})
, where
\Lambda_{x_{o}}
is the germ of
\Lambda
along
T^{*}_{x_{o}}X
. Furthermore, it satisfies
\mathscr{M}^{\prime}_{(x_{o},\boldsymbol{s}_{o})}=\mathfrak{m}_{\boldsymbol{s}_{o}}\mathscr{M}^{\prime}_{(x_{o},\boldsymbol{s}_{o})}
. The Nakayama-type argument given in the proof of
[
MFS19
, Prop. 1.9(1)]
shows that
\mathscr{M}^{\prime}_{(x_{o},\boldsymbol{s}_{o})}=0
for each
x_{o}
. This concludes the proof of Step 3(i).

(ii) Regularity of
R^{k}\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}

We use the result of (i) in order to prove holonomicity and regularity of
R^{k}\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}
for all
k
.

For any
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}
, we consider the line bundle
\mathscr{O}_{S}(\ell)
that we can realize as
\mathscr{O}_{S}(\ell H)
for any hyperplane
H
of
S
. For a
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module or an
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-module
\mathscr{N}
, we set
\mathscr{N}(\ell)=p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}(\ell)\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\penalty\mathscr{N}
.

We apply the technical lemma
3.18
below to
\mathscr{M}
. We note that
\mathscr{M}(\ell)
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
regular holonomic: indeed, this is a local property on
S
, and locally
\mathscr{O}_{S}(\ell)\simeq\mathscr{O}_{S}
. Also,
\mathscr{M}(\ell)
is
\pi
-good. Furthermore, we choose a hyperplane
H\subset S
such that
\dim H\cap T<\dim T
, and we realize
\mathscr{O}_{S}(\ell)
as
\mathscr{O}_{S}(\ell H)
. We also consider
\mathscr{M}(\ell H)
with such a choice of
H
. From Step 3(i), we know that
\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}(\ell H)
is
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-regular. Let us consider the sheaf
\mathscr{O}_{\ell H}
defined by the exact sequence

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{O}_{S}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{O}_{S}(\ell H)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{O}_{\ell H}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0

yielding to the distinguished triangle

\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{M}(\ell H)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{N}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt+1\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}

where
\mathscr{N}
is a complex in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
with
S
-support contained in
T\cap H
. We deduce the long exact sequence

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}H^{-1}R\pi_{*}\mathscr{N}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}(\ell H)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}H^{0}R\pi_{*}\mathscr{N}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0

and the isomorphisms
R^{k+1}\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}\simeq H^{k}R\pi_{*}\mathscr{N}
for
k\geqslant 1
. By the induction hypothesis,
R\pi_{*}\mathscr{N}
belongs to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X})
. The latter isomorphism implies the regularity of
R^{k+1}\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}
for
k\geqslant 1
, and, together with Corollary
3.9
, it implies that of
\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}
and
R^{1}\pi_{*}\mathscr{M}
.
∎

Lemma 3.18
.

Let us assume
S=\mathbb{P}^{m}
. Let
\mathscr{N}
be a coherent
\pi
-good
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module. Then, for any
x_{o}\in X
, there exists a neighborhood
\mathrm{nb}(x_{o})\subset X
and an integer
\ell\geqslant 0
such that, for any
k\geqslant 1
,
R^{k}\pi_{*}\mathscr{N}(\ell)_{|\mathrm{nb}(x_{o})}=0
.

A similar result is well-known to hold for a coherent
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-module, as a consequence of Grauert-Remmert’s Theorems A and B (cf.
[
GR58
]
and
[
BS76
, Th. IV.2.1]
).

Proof.

For any
N\geqslant 1
, by iteration of Lemma
2.4
(
3
) we obtain a distinguished triangle on
\mathrm{nb}(x_{o})\times S

\mathscr{N}^{\prime}[N]\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}}\mathscr{L}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{N}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt+1\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}.

Let us choose
N=2m+1
and let
\ell
be an integer given by the Grauert-Remmert theorems for
\mathscr{L}_{i}
(
i=-N,\dots,0
) in
\mathrm{nb}(x_{o})
. We consider the same triangle obtained after tensoring with
p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}(\ell)
. Since
\pi
has cohomological dimension
2m
, we find that
R^{k}\pi_{*}\mathscr{N}^{\prime}(\ell)[N]=0
for any
k\geqslant 0
. On the other hand, on
\mathrm{nb}(x_{o})
(cf. (
2.1
)),

\displaystyle R\pi_{*}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}}\mathscr{L}_{i}(\ell))

\displaystyle\simeq R\pi_{*}(\pi^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X}\otimes_{\pi^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{X}}\mathscr{L}_{i}(\ell))

\displaystyle\simeq\mathscr{D}_{X}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}R\pi_{*}\mathscr{L}_{i}(\ell)=\mathscr{D}_{X}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\pi_{*}\mathscr{L}_{i}(\ell).

Since
R\pi_{*}\mathscr{N}(\ell)_{|\mathrm{nb}(x_{o})}
is in non negative degrees it follows that
R\pi_{*}\mathscr{N}(\ell)_{|\mathrm{nb}(x_{o})}
is isomorphic to the complex
\tau^{\geqslant 0}(\mathscr{D}_{X}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X}}\pi_{*}\mathscr{L}_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}(\ell))
and since the latter is in non-positive degrees, we conclude that
R\pi_{*}\mathscr{N}(\ell)_{|\mathrm{nb}(x_{o})}\simeq\pi_{*}\mathscr{N}(\ell)_{|\mathrm{nb}(x_{o})}
.
∎

3.c
Integrable regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules

The following proposition, answering a question of Lei Wu, shows that, under a suitable condition on the characteristic variety, a coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-submodule of a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S}
-module is relatively regular holonomic. We call such
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules
integrable
since their relative connection can be lifted as an integrable connection, i.e., a
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S}
-module structure.The condition on the characteristic variety is due to the restrictive definition of a holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module (cf. after Lemma
2.4
).

Proposition 3.19
.

Let
X
and
S
be complex manifolds and let
\mathscr{N}
be a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S}
-module. Assume that the characteristic variety of
\mathscr{N}
is contained in
\Lambda\!\times\!T^{*}S
for some conic Lagrangian closed analytic subset
\Lambda
in
T^{*}X
. Let
\mathscr{M}
be a coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-submodule of
\mathscr{N}
. Then
\mathscr{M}
is a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module.

Let us first recall two properties ((
(3.20)
) and (
(3.21)
) below) of regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S}
-modules that will be useful for the proof and do not depend on the assumption on
\Char\mathscr{N}
made in the proposition.

Let
\mathscr{N}
be any regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S}
-module and let
\mathscr{M}
be a coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-submodule of
\mathscr{N}
. Any irreducible component of
\Char\mathscr{N}
projects to
X\times\penalty S
as an irreducible closed analytic subset
Z
and, denoting by
Z^{\circ}
the smooth part of
Z
, this irreducible component is the closure of the conormal bundle
T^{*}_{Z^{\circ}}(X\times\penalty S)
, that we denote by
T^{*}_{Z}(X\times\penalty S)
. We also denote by
Z^{\circ\circ}
the open set of the smooth locus
Z^{\circ}
on which
p_{Z^{\circ}}:Z^{\circ}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
has maximal rank. The closure in the relative cotangent space
T^{*}(X\times\penalty S/S)
of the relative conormal bundle
T^{*}_{p|Z^{\circ\circ}}(X\times\penalty S/S)
is denoted by
T^{*}_{p|Z}(X\times\penalty S/S)
, and is a conic analytic subset of
T^{*}(X\times\penalty S/S)
.

(3.20)

According to
[
Sab87
, Th. 3.2]
any irreducible component of
\Char\mathscr{M}
is equal to
T^{*}_{p|Z}(X\times\penalty S/S)
for some
Z
such that
T^{*}_{Z}(X\times\penalty S)
is an irreducible component of
\Char\mathscr{N}
.

Assume furthermore that
\dim S=1
. For each
s_{o}\in S
, we denote by
s
a local coordinate on
S
vanishing at
s_{o}
. We postpone after the proof of Proposition
3.19
that of the following classical result.

(3.21)

Under the above assumptions, the kernel and cokernel of
s:\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{M}
are regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-modules.

Proof of Proposition
3.19
, Step 1.

We now add the assumption on
\Char\mathscr{N}
. We first show that
\mathscr{M}
is relative holonomic with characteristic variety contained in
\Lambda\times S
. We note that
T^{*}_{Z}(X\times\penalty S)
is contained in
\Lambda\times T^{*}S
if and only if
Z
decomposes as the product
Y\times T
for some irreducible closed analytic subsets
Y\subset X
and
T\subset S
and
T^{*}_{Y}X
is an irreducible component of
\Lambda
(this seen by considering first the smooth part
Z^{\circ}
). It is then easily seen that

T^{*}_{p|Z}(X\times\penalty S/S)=(T^{*}_{Y}X)\times T,

hence is contained in
\Lambda\times S
. The conclusion follows from (
(3.20)
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.19
, Step 2.

It remains to show the relative regularity of
\mathscr{M}
. We argue by induction on
d=\dim S
. If
d=1
, relative regularity is provided by (
(3.21)
), since we already have relative holonomicity by Step 1. We thus assume that the statement of the proposition holds whenever
\dim S\leqslant d-1
and we assume
\dim S=d\geqslant 2
. The question being local, we fix a local coordinate
s
in
S
, defining a smooth hypersurface
H:=\{s=0\}
. Locally, we can assume that
S=H\times\mathbb{C}
. According to Proposition
3.2
, we are reduced to proving that

(3.22)

the kernel and cokernel of
s\colon\mathscr{M}\!\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\!\mathscr{M}
are regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times H/H}
-modules.

For the sake of clarity we set

X^{\prime}=X\times\penalty H,\ S^{\prime}=\mathbb{C},\ p^{\prime}:X^{\prime}\times\penalty S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S^{\prime}\quad\text{so that}\quad X=X^{\prime}\times\penalty S^{\prime},\quad S=H\times S^{\prime},

We denote by
\mathscr{M}_{1}
the
\mathscr{D}_{X^{\prime}\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
-submodule of the regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X^{\prime}\times\penalty S^{\prime}}
-module
\mathscr{N}
generated by
\mathscr{M}
. It is
\mathscr{D}_{X^{\prime}\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
–coherent, so by (
(3.21)
), the kernel
\mathscr{N}_{1}
and the cokernel
\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{1}
of
s:\mathscr{M}_{1}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{M}_{1}
are
\mathscr{D}_{X^{\prime}}
-regular holonomic.

Claim
.

The characteristic varieties
\Char\mathscr{N}_{1}
and
\Char\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{1}
are contained in
\Lambda\times T^{*}H
.

Proof.

We apply (
(3.20)
) to
\mathscr{M}_{1}\!\subset\!\mathscr{N}
and to the map
X^{\prime}\times\penalty S^{\prime}\!\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\!S^{\prime}
. Since any irreducible component of
\Char\mathscr{N}
takes the form
T^{*}_{Y}X\times T^{*}_{T}S=T^{*}_{Z}(X^{\prime}\times\penalty S^{\prime})
with
Z=Y\times T
, any irreducible component of
\Char\mathscr{M}_{1}
takes the form
T^{*}_{p^{\prime}_{|Z}}(X^{\prime}\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime})
for some such
Z
. Denoting by
q:S\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S^{\prime}
the projection
H\times S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S^{\prime}
, the latter space reads

(T^{*}_{Y}X)\times T^{*}_{q_{|T}}(H\times S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}).

The fiber of the composition
T^{*}_{q|T}(H\times S^{\prime}/S^{\prime})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}T\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S^{\prime}
above
s=0
(
s
is the coordinate on
S^{\prime}
) is contained in
T^{*}H
and is Lagrangian: indeed, it has dimension
\dim H
since
T^{*}_{s|T}(H\times S^{\prime}/S^{\prime})
has dimension
\dim H+1
, and it is easily seen to be isotropic. We denote it by
\Lambda_{T}
. The characteristic varieties of
\mathscr{N}_{1}
and
\mathscr{N}^{\prime}_{1}
are contained in the fiber above
s=0
of
\Char\mathscr{M}_{1}
, hence in the union, over
Y,T
occurring in
\Char\mathscr{N}
, of the Lagrangian subsets
T^{*}_{Y}X\times\Lambda_{T}
, as claimed, since
\Lambda
is nothing but
\mathop{\textstyle\bigcup}\displaylimits_{Y}T^{*}_{Y}X
.
∎

As a consequence,
\mathscr{N}_{1}
is a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty H}
-module satisfying the assumption of the proposition, and
\ker(s:\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{M})
is a coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times H/H}
-submodule of it. We can thus apply the induction hypothesis to conclude that (
(3.22)
) holds for
\ker s
.

On the other hand, for each
k\geqslant 0
, by applying (
(3.21)
) to
s^{k}\mathscr{M}_{1}
, we obtain, according to the claim, that
s^{k}\mathscr{M}_{1}/s^{k+1}\mathscr{M}_{1}
is a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty H}
-module with characteristic variety contained in
\Lambda\times T^{*}H
. Then

\mathscr{M}^{(k)}:=(\mathscr{M}\cap s^{k}\mathscr{M}_{1})/(\mathscr{M}\cap s^{k+1}\mathscr{M}_{1})

is a coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times H/H}
-submodule of
s^{k}\mathscr{M}_{1}/s^{k+1}\mathscr{M}_{1}
. The induction hypothesis implies that
\mathscr{M}^{(k)}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times H/H}
-regular holonomic. It follows by Proposition
3.2
(iii) that
\mathscr{M}/(\mathscr{M}\cap s^{k+1}\mathscr{M}_{1})
is also a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times H/H}
-module. It remains to notice that coherence implies that, locally on
X
, there exists
k
such that
\mathscr{M}\cap s^{k+1}\mathscr{M}_{1}\subset s\mathscr{M}
, so that
\mathscr{M}/s\mathscr{M}
is a coherent quotient of a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times H/H}
-module.
Again by Proposition
3.2
(iii) it is also a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times H/H}
-module, concluding the proof of (
(3.22)
).
∎

Proof of (
(3.21)
).

We consider the Kashiwara-Malgrange
V
-filtration
V_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\mathscr{N}
of
\mathscr{N}
relative to the function
s
(cf. e.g.
[
MS89
,
MM04
]
). This is an increasing filtration indexed by
\mathbb{Z}
which satisfies, owing to the regularity of
\mathscr{N}
, the following properties:

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

V_{k}\mathscr{N}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-coherent for any
k\in\mathbb{Z}
(
[
MS89
, Th. (4.12.1)]
),

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,
s(V_{k}\mathscr{N})\subset V_{k-1}\mathscr{N}
and for
k\leqslant-1
, the multiplication by
s
on
V_{k}\mathscr{N}
is injective with image
V_{k-1}\mathscr{N}
(cf. e.g.
[
MS89
, Prop. (4.5.2)]
),

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

each
\mathrm{gr}_{k}^{V}\mathscr{N}
is a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module (cf. e.g.
[
MM04
, Cor. 4.7-5]
).

From the second point we deduce that, for
k\leqslant-1
,
Li_{s_{o}}^{*}V_{k}\mathscr{N}=i_{s_{o}}^{*}V_{k}\mathscr{N}=\mathrm{gr}_{k}^{V}\mathscr{N}
, and by the third point the latter is
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-regular holonomic. For
k\geqslant 0
,
V_{k}\mathscr{N}
is a successive extension of
V_{-1}\mathscr{N}
by regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-modules
\mathrm{gr}_{\ell}^{V}\mathscr{N}
. Therefore, for each
k
, the kernel and cokernel of
s:V_{k}\mathscr{N}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}V_{k}\mathscr{N}
are
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-regular holonomic: this is proved by induction on
k

since it is clear for
k<0
, and the inductive step follows by considering the snake lemma applied to the following commutative diagram of exact sequences

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V_{k-1}\mathscr{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{s}
\textstyle{V_{k}\mathscr{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{s}
\textstyle{\mathrm{gr}_{k}^{V}\mathscr{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V_{k-1}\mathscr{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V_{k}\mathscr{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathrm{gr}_{k}^{V}\mathscr{N}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

We deduce that
sV_{k}\mathscr{N}/s^{\ell}V_{k}
is regular holonomic for any
\ell\geqslant 1
. As any
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-coherent submodule of
\mathscr{N}
, locally on
X\times\penalty S
,
\mathscr{M}
is contained in
V_{k}\mathscr{N}
for some
k\gg 0
and, by the Artin-Rees lemma,
s\mathscr{M}
contains
\mathscr{M}\cap s^{\ell}V_{k}\mathscr{N}
for some
\ell\geqslant 1
. Since
\ker(s:\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{M})
is contained in
\ker(s:V_{k}\mathscr{N}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}V_{k}\mathscr{N})
, the former is regular holonomic since the latter is so. On the other hand, by considering the inclusion and the quotient maps

sV_{k}\mathscr{N}/s^{\ell}V_{k}\mathscr{N}\mathrel{\mathchoice{\longleftarrow}{\leftarrow}{\leftarrow}{\leftarrow}\joinrel\rhook}(\mathscr{M}\cap sV_{k}\mathscr{N})/(\mathscr{M}\cap s^{\ell}V_{k}\mathscr{N})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\hskip-14.22636pt\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\mathscr{M}\cap sV_{k}\mathscr{N})/s\mathscr{M}

we conclude that
(\mathscr{M}\cap sV_{k}\mathscr{N})/s\mathscr{M}
is also
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-regular holonomic. Furthermore, by considering the exact sequence

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\mathscr{M}\cap sV_{k}\mathscr{N})/s\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{M}/s\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}V_{k}\mathscr{N}/sV_{k}\mathscr{N},

we conclude that
\mathscr{M}/s\mathscr{M}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-regular holonomic.
∎

4
Holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules of D-type and applications

4.a
S
-locally constant sheaves and their associated relative connections

Let
X
be a connected complex manifold and let
L
be an
S
-locally constant sheaf of
p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
-modules on
X\times\penalty S
(cf.
[
MFS19
, App.]
). For any
x_{o}\in X
,
L
is uniquely determined from a monodromy representation
\pi_{1}(X,x_{o})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\Aut_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}(G)
with
G=i_{x_{o}}^{-1}L
. As a consequence, there exists an
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module
G
such that, for any
1
-connected open subset
U
of
X\times\penalty S
, there exists an isomorphism
L_{|U\times S}\simeq p_{U}^{-1}G
. Two choices of
G
are isomorphic, but non-canonically in general. Furthermore,
L
is
p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
-coherent if and only if
G
is
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-coherent.

To any
S
-locally constant sheaf
L
one can associate an exact sequence of sheaves of
p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
-modules

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}L_{\mathrm{t}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}L\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}L_{\mathrm{tf}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0

(4.1)

where
L_{\mathrm{t}}
denotes the subsheaf of
p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
-torsion and
L_{\mathrm{tf}}
the maximal
p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
-torsion-free quotient. Then
L_{\mathrm{t}}
and
L_{\mathrm{tf}}
are
S
-locally constant and the previous exact sequence yields (for any choice of
G
) to the exact sequence of
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-modules

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}G_{\mathrm{t}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}G\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}G_{\mathrm{tf}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0,

with
G_{\mathrm{t}}
and
G_{\mathrm{tf}}
defined similarly. The following is straightforward.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
\pi:S\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S^{\prime}
be a holomorphic map between complex manifolds.

1.

If
L
is an
S
-locally constant sheaf on
X\times\penalty S
with associated
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module
G
, then
\pi_{!}L
is
S^{\prime}
-locally constant on
X\times\penalty S^{\prime}
and
\pi_{!}G
is an associated
\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}
-module to
\pi_{!}L
.

2.

If
L^{\prime}
is an
S^{\prime}
-locally constant sheaf on
X\times\penalty S^{\prime}
with associated
\mathscr{O}_{S}^{\prime}
-module
G^{\prime}
, then
\pi^{*}L^{\prime}
is
S
-locally constant on
X\times\penalty S
and
\pi^{*}G^{\prime}
is an associated
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module to
\pi^{*}L^{\prime}
.

We shall denote by
\mathrm{d}_{X\times\penalty S/S}:\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\Omega^{1}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
the relative differential associated to
p
.
Let us recall the Riemann-Hilbert correspondence for coherent
S
-local systems proved in
[
Del70
, Th. 2.23 p. 14]
, in the particular case of a projection
X\times\penalty S\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
, where
X
and
S
are complex manifolds.

The functor
L\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}(E_{L},\nabla)=(\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}L,\mathrm{d}_{X\times\penalty S/S}\otimes\id)
induces an equivalence between the category of coherent
S
-locally constant sheaves of
p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
-modules and the category of coherent
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-modules
E
equipped with an integrable relative connection
\nabla:E\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\Omega^{1}_{X\times\penalty S/S}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}}E
. A quasi-inverse is given by
(E,\nabla)\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}E^{\nabla}=\ker\nabla
. The monodromy representation attached to the coherent
S
-locally constant sheaf
E^{\nabla}
is also called the monodromy representation of
\nabla
on
E
. Let us emphasize a direct consequence:

Corollary 4.3
.

Let
(E,\nabla)
be a coherent
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-module equipped with an integrable relative connection. Then the natural
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-linear morphism

\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}E^{\nabla}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}E

is an isomorphism compatible with the integrable connections
\mathrm{d}_{X\times\penalty S/S}\otimes\id
and
\nabla
.

Proposition 4.4
.

Notation as in Lemma
4.2
.

1.

Let
L
be a coherent
S
-locally constant sheaf. If
\pi
is proper, then
\pi_{*}L
is
S^{\prime}
-coherent and there exists a natural morphism

E_{\pi_{*}L}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\pi_{*}E_{L}.

2.

Let
L^{\prime}
be a coherent
S^{\prime}
-locally constant sheaf. Then
E_{\pi^{*}L^{\prime}}\simeq\pi^{*}(E_{L^{\prime}})
.

Proof.

1.

Since
\pi
is proper,
\pi_{*}L
is a coherent
S^{\prime}
-locally constant sheaf on
X\times\penalty S^{\prime}
(Lemma
4.2
(
1
) and Grauert’s theorem). The natural morphism
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\pi_{*}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
induces a composed morphism

\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}\otimes_{p^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}}\pi_{*}L\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\pi_{*}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}\otimes_{p^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}}\pi_{*}L\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\pi_{*}(\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}L),

which is clearly compatible with the relative differential
\mathrm{d}\otimes\id
. We note that if
\pi
is surjective with connected fibers, the first morphism is an isomorphism since
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\pi_{*}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
is then an isomorphism. This is the case if for example
S^{\prime}
is a complex manifold and
\pi
is a proper modification of
S^{\prime}
.

2.

The second point is straightforward.∎

4.b
The Deligne extension of an
S
-locally constant sheaf

We recall Theorem 2.6 and extend Corollary 2.8 of
[
MFS19
]
to the case where
\dim S>1
.

Notation 4.5
.

Let
Y
be a hypersurface in
X
(assumed to be connected) and let us denote the inclusion by
j:X^{*}:=X\smallsetminus Y\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}X
. Let
L
be a coherent
S
-locally constant sheaf on
X^{*}\times\penalty S
. Let
(E_{L},\nabla)=(\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\penalty L,\mathrm{d}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S/S}\otimes\id)
be the associated coherent
\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}
-module with flat relative connection
\nabla=\mathrm{d}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S/S}\otimes\id
, so that
L=E_{L}^{\nabla}
. We simply set
E=E_{L}
when the context is clear, that we consider as a left
\mathscr{D}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S/S}
-module. We sometimes call
L
a coherent
S
-local system.

Lemma 4.6
.

The functor
j_{*}E_{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}:L\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}j_{*}E_{L}
with values in
\mathrm{Mod}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
is exact.

Proof.

Any
(x_{o},s_{o})\!\in\!Y\times S
has a fundamental system of open neighborhood
U\times\penalty U(s_{o})
such that
(U\smallsetminus D)\times U(s_{o})
is Stein. Since
E_{L}
is
\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}
-coherent, the result follows.
∎

We assume from now on that
Y=D
is a divisor with normal crossings in
X
. Let
\varpi:\widetilde{X}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X
denote the real blowing up of
X
along the components of
D
. We denote by
\widetilde{\jmath}:X^{*}\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}\widetilde{X}
the inclusion, so that
j=\varpi\circ\widetilde{\jmath}
. Let
x_{o}\in D
,
\widetilde{x}_{o}\in\varpi^{-1}(x_{o})
and let
s_{o}\in S
. Choose local coordinates
(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})
at
x_{o}
such that
D=\{x_{1}\cdots x_{\ell}=0\}
and consider the associated polar coordinates
(\boldsymbol{\rho},\boldsymbol{\theta},\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}):=(\rho_{1},\theta_{1},\dots,\rho_{\ell},\theta_{\ell},x_{\ell+1},\dots,x_{n})
so that
\widetilde{x}_{o}
has coordinates
\boldsymbol{\rho}_{o}=0
,
\boldsymbol{\theta}_{o}
,
\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}_{o}=0
. We also denote by
\varpi
the induced map
\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X\times\penalty S
and by
\widetilde{p}:\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
the projection.

Definition 4.7
.

The subsheaf
\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S}
of
\widetilde{\jmath}_{*}\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}
of holomorphic functions having
moderate growth
along
\varpi^{-1}(D)
is defined by the following two requirements:

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

\widetilde{\jmath}^{-1}\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S}=\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}
.

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

For any
x_{o}\in D
,
\widetilde{x}_{o}\in\varpi^{-1}(x_{o})
and
s_{o}\in S
, a germ
\widetilde{h}\in(\widetilde{\jmath}_{*}\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S})_{(\widetilde{x}_{o},s_{o})}
is said to have
moderate growth
, i.e., to belong to
\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S,(\widetilde{x}_{o},s_{o})}
, if there exist open sets

\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon}:=\{\|\boldsymbol{\rho}\|<\varepsilon,\|\boldsymbol{x}^{\prime}\|<\varepsilon,\|\boldsymbol{\theta}-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{o}\|<\varepsilon\}\quad\text{and}\quad U(s_{o})\ni s_{o}

(4.8)

(
\varepsilon
small enough) such that, setting
U^{*}_{\varepsilon}:=\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon}\smallsetminus\{\rho_{1}\cdots\rho_{\ell}=0\}
,
\widetilde{h}
is defined on
U^{*}_{\varepsilon}\times U(s_{o})
and
|\widetilde{h}|
is bounded on this open set by
C\|\boldsymbol{\rho}\|^{-N}
, for some
C,N>0
.

We recall the following properties of the sheaf
\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S}
:

1.

\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S}
is a
\varpi^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*D)
-module (cf.
[
Sab00
, §1.1.6]
) which is
\varpi^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-flat (cf.
[
Moc14
, Th. 4.6.1]
),

2.

for any coherent
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-module
M
, the natural morphism

\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S}\otimes_{\varpi^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}}\varpi^{-1}M\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\widetilde{\jmath}_{*}j^{-1}M

is injective (cf. loc. cit.),

3.

R\varpi_{*}\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S}=\varpi_{*}\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S}=\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)
(cf.
[
Sab00
, Cor. II.1.1.18]
).

Let us already notice for later use that these properties imply, for any
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module
G
,

\varpi_{*}(\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S}\otimes_{\widetilde{p}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\widetilde{p}^{-1}G)\simeq\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}p^{-1}G.

(4.9)

Indeed, we have

\displaystyle R\varpi_{*}(\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S}\otimes_{\widetilde{p}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\widetilde{p}^{-1}G)

\displaystyle\simeq R\varpi_{*}(\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S}\otimes^{L}_{\widetilde{p}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\widetilde{p}^{-1}G)\quad\text{by \eqref{enum:Amod1}}

\displaystyle\simeq R\varpi_{*}(\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S})\otimes^{L}_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}p^{-1}G

\displaystyle\simeq\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)\otimes^{L}_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}p^{-1}G\quad\text{by \eqref{enum:Amod2}}

\displaystyle\simeq\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}p^{-1}G,

where the latter isomorphism follows from flatness of
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)
over
p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
. Furthermore, (
2
) implies that, for any coherent
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module
G
, the natural morphism

\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S}\otimes_{\widetilde{p}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\widetilde{p}^{-1}G\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\widetilde{\jmath}_{*}(\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}p^{-1}G)

is injective.

Note that,
U^{*}_{\varepsilon}
being contractible, we have
L_{|U^{*}_{\varepsilon}\times U(s_{o})}\simeq p^{-1}_{U^{*}_{\varepsilon}}G_{|U(s_{o})}
(cf.
[
MFS19
, Prop. A.12]
). We thus have an identification

(\widetilde{\jmath}_{*}E)_{|\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon}\times U(s_{o})}\simeq\widetilde{\jmath}_{*}\Bigl(\mathscr{O}_{U^{*}_{\varepsilon}\times U(s_{o})}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{U(s_{o})}}p^{-1}_{U^{*}_{\varepsilon}}G_{|U(s_{o})},\mathrm{d}_{U^{*}_{\varepsilon}\times U(s_{o})/U(s_{o})}\otimes\id\Bigr)\\
\overset{\sim}{\longleftarrow}\Bigl((\widetilde{\jmath}_{*}\mathscr{O}_{U^{*}_{\varepsilon}\times U(s_{o})})\otimes_{\widetilde{p}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{U(s_{o})}}\widetilde{p}^{-1}_{\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon}}G_{|U(s_{o})},\mathrm{d}_{\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon}\times U(s_{o})/U(s_{o})}\otimes\id\Bigr).

(4.10)

Indeed, for a polysector
\widetilde{V}_{\varepsilon}\subset\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon}
and
V\subset U(s_{o})
, the natural morphism

\Gamma(V_{\varepsilon}^{*}\times V;\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}(V)}\Gamma(\widetilde{V}_{\varepsilon}\times V;\widetilde{p}^{-1}G)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\Gamma(V_{\varepsilon}^{*}\times V;\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S})\otimes_{\mathscr{O}(V)}\Gamma(V_{\varepsilon}^{*}\times V;p^{-1}G)

is an isomorphism, since the adjunction morphism
p_{W*}p_{W}^{-1}G\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}G
is an isomorphism for both
W=V_{\varepsilon}^{*}
and
W=\widetilde{V}_{\varepsilon}
(both being connected, cf. e.g.
[
MFS19
, Prop. A.1(2)]
). The assertion is obtained by passing from the pre-sheaf isomorphism to a sheaf isomorphism.

Definition 4.11

(The Deligne extension)
.

Let
x_{o}\in X
,
\widetilde{x}_{o}\in\varpi^{-1}(x_{o})
and
s_{o}\in S
.

1.

A germ
\widetilde{v}
of
(\widetilde{\jmath}_{*}E_{L})_{(\widetilde{x}_{o},s_{o})}
is said to have
moderate growth
if for some open set
\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon}\times U(s_{o})
as above, and for some (equivalently, any) identification
L_{|U^{*}_{\varepsilon}\times U(s_{o})}\simeq p^{-1}_{U^{*}_{\varepsilon}}G_{|U(s_{o})}
,
\widetilde{v}
belongs to the image of the
injective
morphism

\Gamma\bigl(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon}\times U(s_{o});\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S}\otimes_{\widetilde{p}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\widetilde{p}^{-1}G\bigr)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\Gamma(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon}\times U(s_{o});\widetilde{\jmath}_{*}E_{L}).

2.

A germ
v
of
(j_{*}E_{L})_{(x_{o},s_{o})}
is said to have
moderate growth
if for each
\widetilde{x}_{o}
in
\varpi^{-1}(x_{o})
, the corresponding germ in
(\widetilde{\jmath}_{*}E_{L})_{(\widetilde{x}_{o},s_{o})}
has moderate growth. In particular, this holds for any local section of
E_{L}
at
(x_{o},s_{o})
if
x_{o}\notin D
.

3.

The subsheaf of
j_{*}E_{L}
consisting of local sections whose germs have moderate growth is denoted by
\widetilde{E}_{L}
. It satisfies
j^{*}\widetilde{E}_{L}=E_{L}
. It is called
the Deligne extension
of
E_{L}
.

Remark 4.12
.

By definition, with the previous notations,
v
has moderate growth if and only if, on any such polysector
U_{\varepsilon}\times U(s_{o})
, for any isomorphism
L|_{U_{\varepsilon}^{*}\times U(s_{o})}\simeq p_{U_{\varepsilon}^{*}}^{-1}G_{|U(s_{o})}
, for any family of local generators
(g_{j})
of
G
on
U(s_{o})
,
v
can be written as
\sum_{j}v_{j}\otimes g_{j}
with
v_{j}
being holomorphic functions on the corresponding polysector in
X^{*}\times\penalty S
with moderate growth with respect to
D
.

Since
\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S}
is stable by derivations with respect to
X
,
\widetilde{E}_{L}
is a
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*D)
-submodule of
j_{*}E_{L}
.

Proposition 4.13

(First properties of the Deligne extension
\widetilde{E}_{L}
)
.

Let
L
be a coherent
S
-locally constant sheaf on
X^{*}\times\penalty S
and let
(E_{L},\nabla)=(\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}L,\mathrm{d}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S/S}\otimes\id)
be the associated
\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}
-module with flat relative connection.

1.

The assignment
L\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}(\widetilde{E}_{L},\nabla)
with values in
\mathrm{Mod}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
is functorial.

2.

Let
\pi:S\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S^{\prime}
be a proper holomorphic map. Then the natural morphism
j_{*}(E_{\pi_{*}L},\nabla)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\pi_{*}j_{*}(E_{L},\nabla)
sends the subsheaf
\widetilde{E}_{\pi_{*}L}
to
\pi_{*}\widetilde{E}_{L}
.

3.

Let
\pi:S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
be a holomorphic map. Then the natural morphism
\pi^{*}j_{*}(E_{L},\nabla)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}j_{*}(E_{\pi^{*}L},\nabla)
sends isomorphically
\pi^{*}(\widetilde{E}_{L},\nabla)
to
(\widetilde{E}_{\pi^{*}L},\nabla)
.

Proof.

We only prove (
1
) and (
2
), and (
3
) will be a consequence of Theorem
4.15
below.

1.

A morphism of
S
-locally constant sheaves
\varphi:L\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}L^{\prime}
defines a morphism
\varphi:E_{L}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}E_{L^{\prime}}
compatible with
\nabla
, hence
j_{*}E_{L}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}j_{*}E_{L^{\prime}}
compatible with
\nabla
, and we only need to check that it sends
\widetilde{E}_{L}
to
\widetilde{E}_{L^{\prime}}
. This is straightforward from the definition.

2.

From the commutative diagram

\begin{array}[]{c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\pi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-23.68402pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.99295pt\raise-15.85092pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\pi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.99295pt\raise-23.68402pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise
