3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}

(4.14)

together with the natural morphism of Proposition
4.2
(
1
), we obtain the morphism
j_{*}(E_{\pi_{*}L},\nabla)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\pi_{*}j_{*}(E_{L},\nabla)
and similarly
\widetilde{\jmath}_{*}(E_{\pi_{*}L},\nabla)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\pi_{*}\widetilde{\jmath}_{*}(E_{L},\nabla)
. Let us consider an open subset
\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon}\subset\widetilde{X}
as in (
4.8
). Then
U_{\varepsilon}^{*}
is contractible and for any
s^{\prime}_{o}\in S^{\prime}
,
L_{|U_{\varepsilon}^{*}\times\pi^{-1}(U(s^{\prime}_{o}))}\simeq p_{U_{\varepsilon}^{*}}^{-1}G_{|\pi^{-1}(U(s^{\prime}_{o}))}
. In order to simplify the notation, we set
S^{\prime}=U(s^{\prime}_{o})
and
S=\pi^{-1}(U(s^{\prime}_{o}))
. We also set
p=p_{\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon}}
, so that
p_{U_{\varepsilon}^{*}}=p\circ\widetilde{\jmath}
. According to (
4.10
), we are led to proving that the natural morphism

(\widetilde{\jmath}_{*}\mathscr{O}_{U_{\varepsilon}^{*}\times S^{\prime}})\otimes_{\widetilde{p}^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}}\widetilde{p}^{\prime-1}\pi_{*}G\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\pi_{*}\bigl((\widetilde{\jmath}_{*}\mathscr{O}_{U_{\varepsilon}^{*}\times S})\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}p^{-1}G\bigr)

sends
\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon}\times S^{\prime}}\otimes_{\widetilde{p}^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}}\widetilde{p}^{\prime-1}\pi_{*}G
to
\pi_{*}(\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon}\times S}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}p^{-1}G)
.
Following the proof of Proposition
4.4
(
1
), we are led to proving that the natural morphism
\widetilde{\jmath}_{*}\mathscr{O}_{U_{\varepsilon}^{*}\times S^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\pi_{*}(\widetilde{\jmath}_{*}\mathscr{O}_{U_{\varepsilon}^{*}\times S})
sends
\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon}\times S^{\prime}}
to
\pi_{*}\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon}\times S}
. This follows from the definition of moderate growth, owing to the properness of
\pi
.∎

4.c
Regular holonomicity of the Deligne extension of an
S
-locally constant sheaf

We continue to refer to Notation
4.5
and assume that
Y=D
has normal crossings.

Theorem 4.15
.

Assume that
L
is a coherent
S
-locally constant sheaf on
X^{*}\times\penalty S
. Then

1.

the subsheaf
\widetilde{E}_{L}
of
j_{*}E_{L}
is
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)
-coherent;

2.

the functor
L\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\widetilde{E}_{L}
from the category of coherent
S
-locally constant sheaves on
X^{*}\times\penalty S
to that of
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules is fully faithful;

3.

as a
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module,
(\widetilde{E}_{L},\nabla)
is regular holonomic.

We will make use of the following flattening result for a coherent
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module (here, we only need a local version, but the corresponding global one also holds true).

Proposition 4.16
.

Near each
s_{o}\!\in\!S
, there exists a projective modification
\pi:S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
with
S^{\prime}
smooth such that the torsion-free quotient of
\pi^{*}G
is
\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}
-locally free.

Proof.

We first apply the flattening theorem
[
Ros68
, Th. 3.5]
to the coherent sheaf
G
. There exists thus a projective modification
\pi^{\prime\prime}:S^{\prime\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
such that
\pi^{\prime\prime*}G
, when quotiented by its
\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime\prime}}
-torsion, is
\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime\prime}}
-locally free. We then apply resolution of singularities
\pi^{\prime}:S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S^{\prime\prime}
of
S^{\prime\prime}
in the neighborhood of the projective subset
\pi^{\prime\prime-1}(s_{o})
(cf.
[
Hir64
, §7, Main Th. I’]
) and denote by
\pi
the morphism
\pi^{\prime\prime}\circ\pi^{\prime}
, which answers the question.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.15
(
1
).

We recall the proof of
[
MFS19
, Th. 2.6]
for the
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)
-coherence. The problem is local on
X\times\penalty S
. We thus assume that
X\times\penalty S
is a small neighborhood of
(x_{o},s_{o})
as above. In such a neighborhood, giving the local system is equivalent to giving
T_{1},\dots,T_{\ell}\in\Aut(G)
which pairwise commute. Let
U(s_{o})
be an open neighborhood of
s_{o}
isomorphic to an open polydisc. The formula
[
Wan08
, (2.11)]
defining a logarithm of
T_{i}
can be used to show that there exist
A_{1}(s),\dots,A_{\ell}(s)\in\End_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}(G_{|U(s_{o})})
which pairwise commute, such that
T_{i}=\exp(-2\pi iA_{i}(s))
for each
i
on
U(s_{o})
(cf.
[
Wan08
, Cor. 2.3.10 & Chap. 3]
). Set
\widehat{E}_{G}:=\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}p^{-1}G
, equipped with the connection
\widehat{\nabla}
such that
\widehat{\nabla}_{x_{i}\partial_{x_{i}}}
acts on
1\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}p^{-1}G
by
\id\otimes A_{i}(s)
if
i=1,\dots,\ell
, and zero otherwise. Then the monodromy representation of
\widehat{\nabla}
on
\widehat{E}_{G|X^{*}\times\penalty S}
is given by
T_{1},\dots,T_{\ell}
, hence an isomorphism
L\simeq(\widehat{E}_{G|X^{*}\times\penalty S})^{\widehat{\nabla}}
, from which one deduces, according to Corollary
4.3
, an isomorphism

\displaystyle(E_{L},\nabla)

\displaystyle=(\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}L,\mathrm{d}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S/S}\otimes\id)

(4.17)

\displaystyle\simeq(\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(\widehat{E}_{G|X^{*}\times\penalty S})^{\widehat{\nabla}},\mathrm{d}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S/S}\otimes\id)\simeq(\widehat{E}_{G|X^{*}\times\penalty S},\widehat{\nabla}).

It follows that
\widetilde{E}_{L}\!\simeq\!\widetilde{\widehat{E}\,}_{\!G|X^{*}\times\penalty S}
and we are thus reduced to proving that
\widetilde{\widehat{E}\,}_{\!G|X^{*}\times\penalty S}\!=\!\widehat{E}_{G}
(we have trivialized the locally constant sheaf but the connection is not trivial anymore).

Remark 4.18
.

Let
p_{X^{*}}^{-1}G
be the constant
S
-local system on
X^{*}\times\penalty S
. Thus, locally on
X\times\penalty S
, there exist
\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}
-linear isomorphisms

E_{L}\simeq\widehat{E}_{G|X^{*}\times\penalty S}\simeq E_{p_{X^{*}}^{-1}G},

but in general we do not have
(E_{L},\nabla)\simeq(E_{p_{X^{*}}^{-1}G},\widehat{\nabla})
, i.e., this isomorphism is not
\mathscr{D}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S/S}
-linear.

Let us prove the inclusion
\widetilde{\widehat{E}\,}_{\!G|X^{*}\times\penalty S}\subset\widehat{E}_{G}
. Let
v\in(j_{*}\widehat{E}_{G})_{(x_{o},s_{o})}
be locally defined on
U\times\penalty U(s_{o})
. On any polysector
U^{*}_{\varepsilon}\times U(s_{o})
, we can choose logarithms of
x_{i}

(
i=1,\dots,\ell
)
and the automorphism
\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{\ell}x_{i}^{A_{i}(s)}
of
\widehat{E}_{G|X^{*}\times\penalty S}=\mathscr{O}_{U^{*}_{\varepsilon}\times U(s_{o})}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{U(s_{o})}}p^{-1}G
is well-defined by setting
x_{i}^{A_{i}(s)}=\sum_{k}[(\log x_{i})^{k}/k!]\otimes A_{i}(s)^{k}
. By definition,
v
has moderate growth, i.e., is a local section of
\widetilde{\widehat{E}\,}_{\!G|X^{*}\times\penalty S}
, if and only if, on any such polysector
\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon}\times U(s_{o})
, the section
w:=(\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{\ell}x_{i}^{-A_{i}(s)})\cdot v
is a section of
\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S}\otimes_{\widetilde{p}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\widetilde{p}^{-1}G
.

Let
(g_{j})
be a family of local generators of
G
on
U(s_{o})
. The entries of the matrices of
\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{\ell}x_{i}^{A_{i}(s)}
and of
\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{\ell}x_{i}^{-A_{i}(s)}
with respect to this family have moderate growth. If, on such a polysector,
w
writes
\sum_{j}w_{j}\otimes g_{j}
, where
w_{j}
are sections of
\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S}
, then
v=\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{\ell}x_{i}^{A_{i}(s)}\cdot w
writes similarly
\sum_{j}v_{j}\otimes g_{j}
, where
v_{j}
are sections of
\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S}
. Conversely, if
v
has the previous expression, then so does
w=\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{\ell}x_{i}^{-A_{i}(s)}\cdot v
. We conclude that, for any such polysector, the moderate growth condition on
v
is equivalent to
v\in\Gamma(\widetilde{U}_{\varepsilon}\times U(s_{o});\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S}\otimes_{\widetilde{p}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\widetilde{p}^{-1}G)
. In other words, if
v
has moderate growth we have by (
4.9
) that

\displaystyle v\in\Gamma(U\times U(s_{o});\varpi_{*}\mathscr{A}^{\mathrm{mod}}_{\widetilde{X}\times\penalty S}\otimes_{\widetilde{p}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\widetilde{p}^{-1}G)

\displaystyle=\Gamma(U\times U(s_{o});\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}p^{-1}G)

\displaystyle=\Gamma(U\times U(s_{o});\widehat{E}_{G}),

hence
\widetilde{\widehat{E}\,}_{\!G|X^{*}\times\penalty S}\subset\widehat{E}_{G}
. The reverse inclusion also follows from the moderate growth of the entries of the matrices of
\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{\ell}x_{i}^{A_{i}(s)}
and of
\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i=1}^{\ell}x_{i}^{-A_{i}(s)}
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.15
(
2
).

We already know, by the Riemann-Hilbert correspondence of
[
Del70
, Th. 2.23]
, that the functor
L\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}E_{L}
is fully faithful, and it is clearly exact, so it remains to prove that
E_{L}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\widetilde{E}_{L}
is so. Faithfulness is clear. Let us check fullness. Let
\varphi:\widetilde{E}_{L}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\widetilde{E}_{L^{\prime}}
be a morphism and set
\widetilde{\varphi}=\widetilde{j^{*}\varphi}:\widetilde{E}_{L}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\widetilde{E}_{L^{\prime}}
. The kernel and cokernel of
\varphi-\widetilde{\varphi}
are coherent
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)
-modules by
4.15
(
1
), and are zero when restricted to
X^{*}\times\penalty S
. Therefore they are zero, and
\varphi=\widetilde{\varphi}
, proving the assertion.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.15
(
3
).

The question is local at a point
(x_{o},s_{o})\in X\times\penalty S
. By the proof of
4.15
(
1
), we can work with

(\widehat{E}_{G},\widehat{\nabla})=(\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}p^{-1}G,\widehat{\nabla})

(with
\widehat{\nabla}=\mathrm{d}\otimes\id+\sum_{i}\mathrm{d}x_{i}/x_{i}\otimes\penalty A_{i}
and
A_{i}\in\End_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}(G)
). Our method is to reduce to the case where
G
is locally free of rank one and then prove
4.15
(
3
) for it.

We argue by induction on the lexicographically ordered pair
(\dim S,\rk G)
, where
\rk G
is the rank of
G
at a general point of
S
. The case
\dim S=0
and
\rk G
arbitrary is well-known. We thus assume that
d=\dim S\geqslant 1
and
r=\rk G\geqslant 0
, and that
4.15
(
3
) holds for
(\widehat{E}_{G^{\prime}},\widehat{\nabla})
for any
p^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}
-module
G^{\prime}
with
(\dim S^{\prime},\rk G^{\prime})<(d,r)
.

Step 1: The case
(d,0)

If
\rk G=0
,
G
is an
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-torsion module. Since the question is local, we can assume that the support of
G
is contained in a hypersurface
T\subset S
locally presented as in the local setting
3.12
. Then
G
is
q^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}
-coherent and is endowed with an endomorphism
t
(i.e., multiplication by
t
), so that the natural
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-linear morphism
\mathscr{O}_{S}\otimes_{q^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}[t]}G\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}G
is an isomorphism. Furthermore, the endomorphisms
A_{i}
are
q^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}
-linear (via the natural inclusion
q^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}\mathscr{O}_{S}
. Let us set
(\widehat{E}^{\prime}_{G},\nabla)=(\widehat{E}_{G},\widehat{\nabla})
when regarded as a
q^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
-module. By the induction hypothesis, it is regular holonomic. By Corollary
3.14
we conclude that
(\widehat{E}_{G},\widehat{\nabla})
is regular holonomic, so that
4.15
(
3
) holds for
(\widehat{E}_{G},\widehat{\nabla})
.

Step 2: Reduction after proper surjective generically finite base change

Let
\pi\,{:}\,S^{\prime}\!\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\!S
be a proper surjective generically finite morphism of complex manifolds and let
p^{\prime}:X\times\penalty S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S^{\prime}
denote the projection.
Let us assume that
4.15
(
3
) holds for
(\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}(*D)\otimes_{p^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}}p^{\prime-1}\pi^{*}G,\pi^{*}\widehat{\nabla})
.
The purpose of Step 2 is to show that, under such an assumption,
4.15
(
3
) holds for
(\widehat{E}_{G},\widehat{\nabla})
.

From now on, we assume
\dim S=d
and
\rk G\geqslant 1
. Let
G^{\prime}
be the
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-torsion subsheaf of
G
. We note that the endomorphisms
A_{i}
of
G
preserve
G^{\prime}
, so the exact sequence
0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}G^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}G\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}G^{\prime\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0
with
G^{\prime\prime}
being torsion-free gives rise to an exact sequence

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\widehat{E}_{G^{\prime}},\widehat{\nabla})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\widehat{E}_{G},\widehat{\nabla})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\widehat{E}_{G^{\prime\prime}},\widehat{\nabla})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0.

Since
4.15
(
3
) holds for
(\widehat{E}_{G^{\prime}},\widehat{\nabla})
by Step 1, it is enough to prove
4.15
(
3
) for
(\widehat{E}_{G^{\prime\prime}},\widehat{\nabla})
. In other words, we may assume that
G
is torsion-free.

By assumption, there exists a closed analytic subset
T
of codimension
\geqslant 1
in
S
such that, setting
T^{\prime}=\pi^{-1}(T)
, the morphism
\pi:S^{\prime}\smallsetminus T^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S\smallsetminus T
is finite étale. We have
\dim S^{\prime}=\dim S
and
\dim T^{\prime}<\dim S
.

Remark that we have, for any holomorphic map
\pi:S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
, and denoting for clarity by
\widetilde{\pi}
the map
\id\times\pi:X\times\penalty S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X\times\penalty S
, the following list of canonical isomorphisms:

\displaystyle L\widetilde{\pi}^{*}(\mathscr{O}

\displaystyle{}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}p^{-1}G)

(4.19)

\displaystyle=\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}(*D)\otimes^{L}_{\widetilde{\pi}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)}\widetilde{\pi}^{-1}(\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}p^{-1}G)

\displaystyle\overset{(*)}{=}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}(*D)\otimes^{L}_{\widetilde{\pi}^{-1}p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\widetilde{\pi}^{-1}p^{-1}G

\displaystyle=\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}(*D)\otimes^{L}_{p^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}}(p^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}\otimes^{L}_{\widetilde{\pi}^{-1}p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\widetilde{\pi}^{-1}p^{-1}G)

\displaystyle=\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}(*D)\otimes^{L}_{p^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}}p^{\prime-1}L\pi^{*}G=\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}(*D)\otimes_{p^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}}p^{\prime-1}L\pi^{*}G,

which reads

L\pi^{*}\widehat{E}_{G}\simeq\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}(*D)\otimes_{p^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}}p^{\prime-1}L\pi^{*}G.

(4.20)

We conclude that, for each
j
, we have
L^{j}\pi^{*}\widehat{\nabla}_{x_{k}\partial_{x_{k}}}=\mathrm{d}\otimes\id+\id\otimes L^{j}\pi^{*}A_{k}
.

Furthermore, under the assumption on
\pi
for this step, according to the projection formula for
R\widetilde{\pi}_{*}
applied to
(*)
, we have

R\pi_{*}(L\pi^{*}\widehat{E}_{G})\simeq R\pi_{*}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}(*D)\otimes^{L}_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}p^{-1}G.

By induction, if
j\neq 0
,
4.15
(
3
) holds for
(\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}(*D)\otimes_{p^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}}p^{\prime-1}L^{j}\pi^{*}G,L^{j}\pi^{*}\widehat{\nabla})
, according to the argument given in Step 1, since
L^{j}\pi^{*}G
is supported on
T^{\prime}
, hence is a torsion module. Thus
4.15
(
3
) holds for
L\pi^{*}\widehat{E}_{G}
(i.e., for each of its cohomology modules
L^{j}\pi^{*}\widehat{E}_{G}
) since for
j=0
it is the initial assumption.

Since
\widehat{E}_{G}
is
\mathscr{O}
-quasi-coherent (cf. Remark
2.5
(
1
)), one deduces from Lemma
2.13
that each
L^{j}\pi^{*}\widehat{E}_{G}
is
\pi
-good. Then, by Theorem
3.15
,
4.15
(
3
)
holds for
R\pi_{*}(L\pi^{*}\widehat{E}_{G})
, hence for
\mathscr{H}^{0}(R\pi_{*}(L\pi^{*}\widehat{E}_{G}))
.

The natural morphism
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R\pi_{*}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}(*D)
yields a morphism

\widehat{E}_{G}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{H}^{0}(R\pi_{*}(L\pi^{*}\widehat{E}_{G})).

Both modules are
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)
-coherent, hence so is the kernel of this morphism.

On
S\smallsetminus T
, we claim that this morphism is injective: indeed, since
\pi:S^{\prime}\smallsetminus T^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S\smallsetminus T
is finite étale, the trace morphism
\mathrm{tr}_{\pi}:\pi_{*}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}\smallsetminus T^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{O}_{S\smallsetminus T}
defined by
\mathrm{tr}_{\pi}(\varphi)(s)=(1/\deg\pi)\sum_{s^{\prime}\in\pi^{-1}(s)}\varphi(s^{\prime})
satisfies
\mathrm{tr}_{\pi}\circ\iota=\id
, if
\iota
denotes the natural morphism
\mathscr{O}_{S\smallsetminus T}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\pi_{*}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}\smallsetminus T^{\prime}}
; hence
\widehat{E}_{G|S\smallsetminus T}
is locally a direct summand of the right-hand side. As
G
, hence
\widehat{E}_{G}
, is assumed to be
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-torsion-free, the kernel is
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-torsion-free,
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)
-coherent and
S
-supported on
T
. It is thus zero and this morphism is injective.

Furthermore,
\widehat{E}_{G}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-coherent: indeed, as
4.15
(
3
) holds for
\mathscr{H}^{0}(R\pi_{*}(L\pi^{*}\widehat{E}_{G}))
and
\widehat{E}_{G}
is
\mathscr{O}
-good, it follows that
\widehat{E}_{G}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-coherent, and then regular holonomic by Proposition
3.2
(
iii
), so that
4.15
(
3
) holds for
\widehat{E}_{G}
.

Step 3: Reduction to the case where
G
is
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-locally free

We choose
\pi
as in Proposition
4.16
and by Step 2 we can assume from the start that the torsion-free quotient
G^{\prime\prime}
of
G
is
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-locally free. By Step 1, it is enough to prove
4.15
(
3
) for
(\widehat{E}_{G^{\prime\prime}},\widehat{\nabla})
, i.e., we can assume (and we do assume from now on) that
G
is
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-locally free.

Step 4: The case where
G
is
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-locally free

We still work locally on
S
and we assume that
G
is
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-free of rank
r\geqslant 1
. By the induction hypothesis,
4.15
(
3
) holds for any
(\widehat{E}_{G^{\prime}},\widehat{\nabla})
with
\rk G^{\prime}<r
. Locally let us fix an
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-basis of
G
and let
\mathsf{A}_{1}(s)
denote the matrix of
A_{1}
in this basis. Let
\Sigma\subset S\times\mathbb{C}
be the zero locus of
P:=\det(\alpha_{1}\id-\mathsf{A}_{1})
and let
\sigma:\Sigma\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
denote the projection. Since
P
is a Weierstrass polynomial with respect to the variable
\alpha_{1}
(considered as a coordinate on
\mathbb{C}
) with coefficients in
\mathscr{O}_{S}
,
\sigma
is a finite morphism of degree
\deg\sigma
and
\Sigma
is defined by the corresponding reduced Weierstrass polynomial. We note the following two properties related to
\sigma
and similarly to
\id_{X}\times\sigma
:

(4.21)

The sheaf
\sigma_{*}\mathscr{O}_{\Sigma}
is
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-free of degree
\deg\sigma
(with basis
1,\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{1}^{\deg\sigma-1}
).

(4.22)

There exist dense analytic open subsets
\Sigma^{\circ}\!\subset\!\Sigma
and
S^{\circ}\!\subset\!S
such that
\sigma:\Sigma^{\circ}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S^{\circ}
is a finite covering of degree
\deg\sigma
. The corresponding trace morphism
\mathrm{tr}_{\sigma}:\sigma_{*}\mathscr{O}_{\Sigma^{\circ}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\circ}}
has been defined above. If
\varphi
is a section of
\sigma_{*}\mathscr{O}_{\Sigma}
, its trace on
\Sigma^{\circ}
is bounded on
S^{\circ}
, hence extends holomorphically to
S
, so that
\mathrm{tr}_{\sigma}
extends as a morphism
\sigma_{*}\mathscr{O}_{\Sigma}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{O}_{S}
. If
\iota
denotes the natural morphism
\mathscr{O}_{S}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\sigma_{*}\mathscr{O}_{\Sigma}
, we clearly have
\mathrm{tr}_{\sigma}\circ\iota=\id
, making
\mathscr{O}_{S}
a direct summand of
\sigma_{*}\mathscr{O}_{\Sigma}
.

Let
\pi:S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\Sigma
be a resolution of singularities of
\Sigma
, so that the natural composed map
(\sigma\circ\pi):S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
is projective and generically finite. Let us set
G^{\prime}=\ker(\alpha_{1}\id-\mathsf{A}_{1}\circ\sigma\circ\pi)\subset(\sigma\circ\pi)^{*}G
, where we regard
\alpha_{1}
as a function
S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{C}
. Noting that
\sigma\circ\pi
is generically a local isomorphism, it follows by construction that
\rk G^{\prime}\geqslant 1
. We consider the exact sequence
0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}G^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\sigma\circ\pi)^{*}G\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}G^{\prime\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0
which satisfies
0<\rk G^{\prime}
and
\rk G^{\prime\prime}<\rk G
, and which is preserved by the endomorphisms
(\sigma\circ\pi)^{*}A_{i}
, so that it induces an exact sequence of
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\widehat{E}_{G^{\prime}},\widehat{\nabla})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\sigma\circ\pi)^{*}(\widehat{E}_{G},\widehat{\nabla})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\widehat{E}_{G^{\prime\prime}},\widehat{\nabla})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0.

If
\rk G^{\prime}<\rk G
, we can apply induction to
(\widehat{E}_{G^{\prime}},\widehat{\nabla}),(\widehat{E}_{G^{\prime\prime}},\widehat{\nabla})
and conclude that
4.15
(
3
) holds for
(\sigma\circ\pi)^{*}(\widehat{E}_{G},\widehat{\nabla})
, hence for
(\widehat{E}_{G},\widehat{\nabla})
according to Step 2.

If
\rk G^{\prime}=\rk G
, then
\rk G^{\prime\prime}=0
, so Step 1 applies to
(\widehat{E}_{G^{\prime\prime}},\widehat{\nabla})
, and we are reduced to proving
4.15
(
3
) for
(\widehat{E}_{G^{\prime}},\widehat{\nabla})
, i.e., we can assume that
A_{1}=\alpha_{1}\id
. Iterating the argument, we are reduced to the case where
A_{i}=\alpha_{i}\id
for
i=1,\dots,\ell
, where
\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{\ell}
are holomorphic functions on
S
, and
G
is
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-locally free. By considering a local basis of
G
, it suffices to consider the case where
\rk G=1
.

Step 5: The case where
G
is
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-locally free of rank one

We now consider
(\widehat{E}_{G},\widehat{\nabla})=(\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D),\mathrm{d}_{X\times\penalty S/S}+\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\alpha_{i}(s)\mathrm{d}x_{i}/x_{i})
. The argument for obtaining
4.15
(
3
) is then similar to that used in the proof of
[
MFS19
, Cor. 2.8]
. One can assume that, in the neighborhood of
s_{o}
and for any
i
,
\alpha_{i}(s)\in\mathbb{Z}\mathchoice{\Longrightarrow}{\Rightarrow}{\Rightarrow}{\Rightarrow}\alpha_{i}(s)=0
. Then there exists a surjective morphism

\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}/\bigl((x_{i}\partial_{x_{i}}-\alpha_{i}(s)+1)_{i=1,\dots,\ell},(\partial_{x_{j}})_{j=\ell+1,\dots,n}\bigr)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\widehat{E}_{G},\widehat{\nabla})

sending
1
to
1/x_{1}\cdots x_{\ell}
, which is easily seen to be an isomorphism by the condition on
(\alpha_{i})_{i=1,\dots,\ell}
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.13
(
3
).

The statement is local, so, as a consequence of (
4.17
) in the proof of Theorem
4.15
(
1
) and keeping the same notation, we are reduced to proving
\pi^{*}(\widehat{E}_{G},\widehat{\nabla})\simeq(\widehat{E}_{\pi^{*}G},\widehat{\nabla})
, that is,

\pi^{*}(\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}p^{-1}G,\widehat{\nabla})\simeq(\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}(*D)\otimes_{p^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}}p^{-1}\pi^{*}G,\pi^{*}{\widehat{\nabla}}),

which follows by taking the
0
-cohomology in (
4.19
).
∎

4.d
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules of D-type

Recall Notation
4.5
. In this section, we exhibit a family of regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules, that we call
of D-type
and we prove in Proposition
4.26
a special case of the analogue in the relative setting of
[
KK81
, Th. 2.3.2]
asserting that the restriction functor to the complement of the divisor is an equivalence of categories. The general case will be obtained in Theorem
6.17
below.

Let
Y
be a closed hypersurface of
X
.

Definition 4.23
.

We say that
a coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)
-module
\mathscr{L}
is of D-type
if

a.

there exists a coherent
S
-locally constant sheaf
L
on
X^{*}\times\penalty S
such that
\mathscr{L}_{|X^{*}\times\penalty S}\simeq E_{L}=(\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}L,\mathrm{d}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S/S}\otimes\id)
, equivalently,
\mathscr{L}_{|X^{*}\times\penalty S}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S/S}
-holonomic with characteristic variety contained in the zero section,

b.

for each
s\in S
, the cohomology of
Li_{s}^{*}\mathscr{L}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-regular holonomic (in particular,
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-coherent). In other words
\mathscr{L}
satisfies condition
(\mathrm{Reg}\,2)
and thus
(\mathrm{Reg}\,1)
(cf. Proposition
3.2
).

We say that
\mathscr{L}
is
strict
if
L
is
p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
-locally free.

We denote by
\mathrm{Mod}_{Y}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y))
the full subcategory of
\mathrm{Mod}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y))
whose objects are coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)
-modules of D-type.

Lemma 4.24
.

For
\pi:S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
and
\mathscr{L}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{Y}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y))
,
L\pi^{*}\mathscr{L}
has cohomology in
\mathrm{Mod}_{Y}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}(*Y))
.

Proof.

The good behaviour of
4.23
(
a
) by base pullback is clear. Let us check that of
4.23
(
b
). Arguing as in Lemma
2.14
, we see that
L\pi^{*}\mathscr{L}
is an object of
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}(*D))
. For any
s^{\prime}\in S^{\prime}
we have
Li_{s^{\prime}}^{*}L\pi^{*}\mathscr{L}\simeq Li_{\pi(s^{\prime})}^{*}\mathscr{L}
, so the complex
Li_{s^{\prime}}^{*}L\pi^{*}\mathscr{L}
has
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-regular holonomic cohomology. By Proposition
3.2
(
i
), each
Li_{s^{\prime}}^{*}L^{j}\pi^{*}\mathscr{L}
also has
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-regular holonomic cohomology.
∎

Proposition 4.25
.

Assume that
Y=D
is a divisor with normal crossings in
X
.

1.

If
\mathscr{L}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*D)
-coherent of D-type and strict, then the natural morphism
\psi:\mathscr{L}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}j_{*}E_{L}
sends
\mathscr{L}
isomorphically to
\widetilde{E}_{L}
.

2.

If
\mathscr{L}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*D)
-coherent of D-type, then
\mathscr{L}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-regular holonomic.

Proof.

We note that the question is local on
X\times\penalty S
, so we may replace
E_{L}
with
\widehat{E}_{G|X^{*}\times\penalty S}
as in (
4.17
). Namely, we have

\mathscr{L}_{|X^{*}\times\penalty S}\simeq(\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}p^{-1}G,\widehat{\nabla}).

We note that:

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

If
\mathscr{L}
is strict, the second assertion follows from the first one, because
\widetilde{E}_{L}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-regular holonomic (Theorem
4.15
(
3
)).

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

For any coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*D)
-module of D-type, the morphism
\psi
is injective. Indeed, according to the first point of Definition
4.23
, the restriction of
\psi
to
X^{*}\times\penalty S
is an isomorphism. The assertion follows from the
\mathscr{O}
-quasi-coherence of
\mathscr{L}
(cf. Remark
2.5
(
2
)). We thus identify
\mathscr{L}
with a
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*D)
-submodule of
j_{*}E_{L}
.

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

Due to
4.23
(
b
),
4.25
(
2
) amounts to holonomicity of
\mathscr{L}
(in particular,
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-coherence).

Proof of Proposition
4.25
(
1
)

We assume that
G
is
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-locally free of finite rank.
We can mimic the end of the proof of
[
MFS19
, Prop. 2.11]
to directly show that
\psi
is an isomorphism
\mathscr{L}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\widetilde{E}_{L}
because, although in loc. cit. we assumed the
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-coherence of
\mathscr{L}
, that proof works under the weaker assumption of its
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)
-coherence.
This shows
4.25
(
1
).
In particular this implies the
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-regular holonomicity of
\mathscr{L}
.

Proof of Proposition
4.25
(
2
)

We now prove the holonomicity of
\mathscr{L}
by assuming only that
G
is
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-coherent. As in the proof of Theorem
4.15
(
3
), we argue by induction on the dimension of
S
. The case where
S
is a point is well-known (
[
KK81
, Th. 2.3.2]
). We assume that the result holds if
\dim S\leqslant d-1
(
d\geqslant 1
) and that
\dim S=d
.

Step 1: The case where
G
is a torsion
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module

Since we work locally, we can assume that the support of
G
is contained in a hypersurface
T
of
S
, having equation
h=0
, endowed with a finite morphism
q
to
S^{\prime}
of dimension
d-1
, and that
h^{k}G=0
for some
k\geqslant 1
. We claim that any local section
m
of
\mathscr{L}
is annihilated by
h^{k}
. Indeed, for any such
m
,
h^{k}m
is zero on
X^{*}\times\penalty S
and we can apply the result of Remark
2.5
(
2
).

Therefore,
\Supp_{S}\mathscr{L}
is contained in
T
. By the induction hypothesis and the equivalence recalled in Setting
3.12
, we deduce that
\mathscr{L}
is
q^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
-holonomic, hence, arguing as in Remark
3.13
, we conclude that
\mathscr{L}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-holonomic, as desired.

Step 2: The case where
G_{\mathrm{tf}}
is
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-locally free

We consider the exact sequence (
4.1
) and we assume that
G_{\mathrm{tf}}
is
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-locally free. We also consider the similar exact sequence
0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{t}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{L}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{tf}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0
. Let us prove that
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{tf}}
is of D-type. First,
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{t}}
is easily seen to be
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*D)
-coherent, hence so is
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{tf}}
. Next,
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{tf}}
satisfies the first point of Definition
4.23
, with local system
p^{-1}G_{\mathrm{tf}}
.

For the second point, we note that the image of
i^{*}_{s}\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{t}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}i^{*}_{s}\mathscr{L}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-coherent since
i^{*}_{s}\mathscr{L}
is so and
i^{*}_{s}\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{t}}
is
\mathscr{O}
-quasi-coherent. As a consequence, it is
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-regular holonomic since
i^{*}_{s}\mathscr{L}
is so, and thus
i^{*}_{s}\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{tf}}
is regular holonomic. On the other hand, for
j<0
,
L^{j}i^{*}_{s}\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{tf}}
is
\mathscr{O}
-quasi-coherent and supported on
D
by our assumption on
G_{\mathrm{tf}}
, so
L^{j}i^{*}_{s}\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{tf}}=0
by Lemma
2.3
(
2
).

In conclusion,
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{tf}}
is of D-type, and we also deduce that
\mathscr{L}_{\mathrm{t}}
is of D-type. By Step 1 and
4.25
(
1
),
\mathscr{L}
is holonomic if
G_{\mathrm{tf}}
is
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-locally free.

Step 3: The general case

Let
\pi:S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
be a projective modification as in Proposition
4.16
such that the
S^{\prime}
-torsion-free quotient of
\pi^{*}G_{\mathrm{tf}}
is
\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}
-locally free. The
S^{\prime}
-torsion-free quotient of
\pi^{*}G
, being equal to it, is then
\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}
-locally free.

By Lemma
4.24
,
L\pi^{*}\mathscr{L}
has cohomology in
\mathrm{Mod}_{D}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}(*D))
. We can therefore apply Steps 1 and 2 to deduce that
\pi^{*}\mathscr{L}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}
-holonomic, and it is regular by Definition
4.23
(
b
). It is moreover
\pi
-good (cf. Lemma
2.13
). Hence,
\pi_{*}\pi^{*}\mathscr{L}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-regular holonomic (Theorem
3.15
).

Since
\mathscr{L}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*D)
-coherent, the image
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}
of the adjunction morphism
\mathscr{L}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\pi_{*}\pi^{*}\mathscr{L}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-coherent, hence regular holonomic (Proposition
3.2
(
iii
)) and its kernel is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*D)
-coherent. It follows that the latter, which clearly satisfies the first point of Definition
4.23
, also satisfies the second point since
\mathscr{L}
and
\mathscr{L}^{\prime}
do so (cf. Proposition
3.2
). Since
\pi
is biholomorphic above
S\smallsetminus T
for some closed analytic subset
T
of codimension
\geqslant 2
, this kernel satisfies the assumption of Step 1. It is thus
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-holonomic, hence
\mathscr{L}
is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-holonomic, as desired.
∎

Note that the assignment
\mathscr{L}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}L=\mathscr{H}^{0}\DR(\mathscr{L}_{|X^{*}\times\penalty S})
is a functor which takes values in the category of coherent
S
-locally constant sheaves, since the characteristic variety of
\mathscr{L}_{|X^{*}\times\penalty S}
is contained in the zero section.

Proposition 4.26
.

The category of strict regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules of D-type with singularities along
D
is equivalent to the category of
S
-locally constant sheaves on
X^{*}\times\penalty S
which are
p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
-locally free of finite rank, under the correspondences
\mathscr{L}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}L=\mathscr{H}^{0}\DR(\mathscr{L}_{|X^{*}\times\penalty S})
and
L\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\mathscr{L}=\widetilde{E}_{L}
.

Proof.

Owing to the Riemann-Hilbert correspondence on
X^{*}\times\penalty S
(cf.
[
MFS19
, Rem. A.10]
), we have
\mathscr{L}_{|X^{*}\times\penalty S}\simeq(\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}L,\mathrm{d}_{X\times\penalty S/S}\otimes\id)=E_{L}
. Since the natural morphism
\mathscr{L}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}j_{*}E_{L}
sends isomorphically
\mathscr{L}
to
\widetilde{E}_{L}
(Proposition
4.25
(
1
)), the functor
L\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}\widetilde{E}_{L}
from the category of
S
-locally constant sheaves on
X^{*}\times\penalty S
which are
p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
-locally free of finite rank to that of regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules of D-type is essentially surjective. That it is fully faithful has been proved in Theorem
4.15
(
2
).
∎

4.e
Proof of Theorem
1

Although the next proposition is not general enough to prove Theorem
1
, it will be one of the main tools for its proof.

Let
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y
be a morphism of real or complex analytic manifolds, we denote by
\mathscr{D}_{{Y\leftarrow X}/S}
and by
\mathscr{D}_{{X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y}/S}
the relative transfer bi-modules.

Proposition 4.27
.

Let
\mathscr{M}
be a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module with
X
-support
Z=\mathop{\textstyle\bigcup}\displaylimits_{i}Z_{i}
(see (
2.7
)). Let
Y\subset X
be a hypersurface such that
Y\supset Z_{i}
if
\dim Z_{i}<\dim Z
, and
Z_{o}:=Z\smallsetminus(Y\cap Z)
is smooth of pure dimension
\dim Z
. Then the localized
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module
\mathscr{M}(*Y)
is regular holonomic and locally isomorphic to the projective pushforward of a relative
\mathscr{D}
-module of D-type.

Proof.

The question is local. The assumption on
Y
implies that the characteristic variety of
\mathscr{M}_{|(X\smallsetminus Y)\times S}
is contained in
(T^{*}_{Z_{o}}X)\times S
. By Kashiwara’s equivalence,
\mathscr{M}_{|(X\smallsetminus Y)\times S}
is the pushforward by the inclusion map of a coherent
\mathscr{O}_{Z_{o}\times S}
-module with flat relative connection, which is thus of the form
(\mathscr{O}_{Z_{o}\times S}\otimes_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}L,\mathrm{d}_{Z_{o}\times S/S})
for some coherent locally constant
p^{-1}_{Z_{o}}\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module
L
.

One can find a complex manifold
X^{\prime}
together with a divisor with normal crossings
Y^{\prime}\subset\penalty X^{\prime}
and a projective morphism
f:X^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X
which induces a biholomorphism
X^{\prime}\smallsetminus Y^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}Z_{o}
(cf. e.g.
[
AHV18
, Prologue, Th. 4]
). We set
\delta=\dim Z-\dim X=\dim X^{\prime}-\dim X\leqslant 0
. For each
\ell
, we consider the
\mathscr{D}_{X^{\prime}\times\penalty S/S}
-module
\mathscr{M}^{\prime\ell}:=\mathscr{H}^{\ell}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f^{*}\mathscr{M}
. Although it is not yet known to be coherent, it is
f
-
\mathscr{O}
-good in the sense of Definition
2.2
(Proposition
2.11
(
1
)). By considering the filtration by the order of the pole along
Y^{\prime}
, one checks that
\mathscr{M}^{\prime\ell}(*Y^{\prime})
is also
f
-
\mathscr{O}
-good (cf. Remark
2.5
(
1
)).

If
\ell\neq\delta
, the sheaf-theoretic restriction of
\mathscr{M}^{\prime\ell}
to
(X^{\prime}\smallsetminus Y^{\prime})\times S
is zero, therefore
\mathscr{M}^{\prime\ell}(*Y^{\prime})=0
owing to
\mathscr{O}
-quasi-coherence (cf. Lemma
2.3
(
2
)). Since
\mathscr{O}_{X^{\prime}\times\penalty S}(*Y^{\prime})
is flat over
\mathscr{O}_{X^{\prime}\times\penalty S}
, we conclude that

{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f^{*}\bigl(\mathscr{M}(*Y)\bigr)[\delta]\simeq\bigl({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f^{*}\mathscr{M}\bigr)(*Y^{\prime})[\delta]\simeq\mathscr{M}^{\prime\delta}(*Y^{\prime}).

(4.28)

We can interpret
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f^{*}\bigl(\mathscr{M}(*Y)\bigr)
as the pullback of
\mathscr{M}(*Y)
as a
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)
-module. Since
f
is a modification and
Y^{\prime}=f^{-1}(Y)
, we have

\begin{split}\mathscr{D}_{X^{\prime}\times\penalty S/S}(*Y^{\prime})&\simeq\mathscr{O}_{X^{\prime}\times\penalty S}(*Y^{\prime})\otimes_{f^{-1}(\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*Y))}f^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)\\[-3.0pt]
&=\mathscr{D}_{X^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X/S}(*Y^{\prime})\end{split}

(4.29)

(in local coordinates
x^{\prime}
in
X^{\prime}
and
x
in
X
, the determinant of the matrix expressing
\partial_{x^{\prime}_{i}}
in terms of
\partial_{x_{j}}
is invertible in
\mathscr{O}_{X^{\prime}\times\penalty S}(*Y^{\prime})
). It follows that
\mathscr{M}^{\prime\delta}(*Y^{\prime})=\mathscr{D}_{X^{\prime}\times\penalty S/S}(*Y^{\prime})\otimes_{f^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)}f^{-1}\mathscr{M}(*Y)
is
\mathscr{D}_{X^{\prime}\times\penalty S/S}(*Y^{\prime})
-coherent.

Since
Li_{s}^{*}
commutes with
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f^{*}
,
\mathscr{M}^{\prime\delta}(*Y^{\prime})
is
\mathscr{D}_{X^{\prime}\times\penalty S/S}(*Y^{\prime})
-coherent of D-type (Definition
4.23
) so, by Proposition
4.25
(
2
), it is
\mathscr{D}_{X^{\prime}\times\penalty S/S}
-regular holonomic. Since
\mathscr{M}^{\prime\delta}(*Y^{\prime})
is
f
-
\mathscr{O}
-good and
\mathscr{D}_{X^{\prime}\times\penalty S/S}
-coherent, it is
f
-good.

According to
[
MFS19
, Cor. 2.4]
,
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}(\mathscr{M}^{\prime\delta}(*Y^{\prime}))
has regular holonomic cohomology. Furthermore, since
\mathscr{H}^{j}({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\mathscr{M}^{\prime\delta}(*Y^{\prime}))
is supported on
Y\times\penalty S
for
j\neq 0
, and since
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}(\mathscr{M}^{\prime\delta}(*Y^{\prime}))\simeq({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\mathscr{M}^{\prime\delta})(*Y)
, we have

{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}(\mathscr{M}^{\prime\delta}(*Y^{\prime}))\simeq\mathscr{H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}(\mathscr{M}^{\prime\delta}(*Y^{\prime}))\simeq\mathscr{H}^{0}({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\mathscr{M}^{\prime\delta})(*Y).

On the other hand, there is a natural adjunction morphism (cf.
[
Kas03
, Lem. 4.28 & Prop. 4.34]
)

{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f^{*}\mathscr{M}[\delta]\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{M},

which induces a morphism of coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)
-modules

\mathscr{H}^{0}({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\mathscr{M}^{\prime\delta}(*Y^{\prime}))\simeq(\mathscr{H}^{0}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\mathscr{M}^{\prime\delta})(*Y)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{M}(*Y),

(4.30)

where the left-hand side is
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-coherent and regular holonomic. Its cokernel is zero on
(X\smallsetminus Y)\times S
and
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)
-coherent, hence it is zero according to Lemma
2.3
(
2
), so that this morphism is an isomorphism. In conclusion,
\mathscr{M}(*Y)
is regular holonomic.
∎

Corollary 4.31
.

Let
\mathscr{M}
be a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module and let
Y
be any hypersurface in
X
. Then the localized
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module
\mathscr{M}(*Y)
is regular holonomic (hence
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-coherent).

Proof.

The question is local on
X\times\penalty S
. Let
Z
denote the
X
-support of
\mathscr{M}
. We argue by induction on the dimension of
Z
. The case where
\dim Z=0
is clear because either
Z\subset Y
and then
\mathscr{M}(*Y)=0
, or
Z\not\subset Y
and then
\mathscr{M}(*Y)=\mathscr{M}
.

Let
Y^{\prime}
be a hypersurface satisfying the properties as in Proposition
4.27
. Then
Y\cup Y^{\prime}
satisfies the same properties. We consider the following commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{[Y^{\prime}]}\mathscr{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{M}(*Y^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R^{1}\Gamma_{[Y^{\prime}]}\mathscr{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\Gamma_{[Y^{\prime}]}\mathscr{M})(*Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{M}(*Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathscr{M}(*(Y^{\prime}\cup Y))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(R^{1}\Gamma_{[Y^{\prime}]}\mathscr{M})(*Y)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

By Proposition
4.27
, the terms of the top horizontal line, together with
\mathscr{M}(*(Y^{\prime}\cup Y))
, are regular holonomic. On the other hand, the support of the regular holonomic modules
\Gamma_{[Y^{\prime}]}\mathscr{M}
and
R^{1}\Gamma_{[Y^{\prime}]}\mathscr{M}
is of dimension
<\dim Z
, hence the assertion holds for these modules by the induction hypothesis, and the extreme terms of the lower horizontal line are regular holonomic. It follows that the remaining term
\mathscr{M}(*Y)
is regular holonomic.
∎

Corollary 4.32
.

Let
\mathscr{M}
be a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module and let
Y
be any closed analytic subset in
X
. Then
R\Gamma_{[Y]}\mathscr{M}
belongs to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
.

Proof.

The question is local. The case of a hypersurface follows from Corollary
4.31
. The general case follows by writing locally
Y
as the intersection of hypersurfaces.
∎

Corollary 4.33
.

Let
Y
be a closed hypersurface of
X
and let
\mathscr{M}
be a coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)
-module
\mathscr{L}
of D-type.
Then
\mathscr{M}
belongs to
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
.

Proof.

This is obtained by the arguments used in the proof of Proposition
4.27
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1
.

We regard the morphism
f
as the composition of a closed inclusion and a projection. The latter case is clear, so we only consider the case where
f:Y\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}X
is the inclusion of a closed submanifold. Then Corollary
4.32
implies that
R\Gamma_{[Y]}\mathscr{M}
is regular holonomic. By Kashiwara’s equivalence, we conclude that
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f^{*}\mathscr{M}
belongs to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S/S})
.
∎

Proposition 4.34
.

If
\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N}
are objects of
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
, then so is
\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}}^{L}\mathscr{N}
.

Proof.

Recall that the tensor product has been defined in Remark
2.17
. According to Theorem
1
applied to the diagonal embedding
\delta
, it is enough to prove that the
S
-external tensor product
\mathscr{M}\boxtimes^{L}_{\mathscr{D}}\mathscr{N}
is an object of
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{(X\times X)\times S/S})
. Holonomicity has been observed in Remark
2.17
. Regularity follows from the isomorphism
Li_{s}^{*}(\mathscr{M}\boxtimes^{L}_{\mathscr{D}}\penalty\mathscr{N})\simeq\mathscr{D}_{X\times X}\otimes_{\mathscr{D}_{X}\boxtimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathscr{D}_{X}}(Li_{s}^{*}\mathscr{M}\boxtimes_{\mathbb{C}}Li_{s}^{*}\mathscr{N})
and from the regular holonomicity of the latter as a complex in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times X})
.
∎

Let
Y_{i}
(
i=1,\dots,p
) be hypersurfaces of
X
defined as the zero set of holomorphic functions
h_{i}:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{C}
, set
Y=\mathop{\textstyle\bigcup}\displaylimits_{i}Y_{i}
and let
\mathscr{N}
be a
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)
-module. We regard
\mathscr{N}
as an
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*Y)
-module with flat relative connection
\nabla
, and for a
tuple

\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{p})
of holomorphic functions
\alpha_{i}:S\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{C}
, we denote by
\mathscr{N}h^{\alpha}
the
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-module
\mathscr{N}
, endowed with the flat relative connection
\nabla+\sum_{i}\alpha_{i}\id\otimes\mathrm{d}h_{i}/h_{i}
. The functor
\mathscr{N}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{N}h^{\alpha}
is an auto-equivalence of the category
\mathrm{Mod}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y))
, as well as of
\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y))
. We have a functorial isomorphism
\mathscr{N}h^{\alpha}\simeq\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*Y)h^{\alpha}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}}\mathscr{N}\simeq\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*Y)h^{\alpha}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}}^{L}\mathscr{N}
.

Corollary 4.35
.

Assume that
\mathscr{M}
is a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module. Then so is
\mathscr{M}(*Y)h^{\alpha}
.

Sketch of proof.

One checks that the coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)
-module
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*Y)h^{\alpha}
is of D-type along
Y
by using that
\mathscr{O}_{X}(*Y)h^{\alpha(s)}
is regular holonomic for each
s\!\in\!S
. Hence
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*Y)h^{\alpha}
is regular holonomic by Corollary
4.33
. Then, on noting that
\mathscr{M}(*Y)h^{\alpha}\simeq\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*Y)h^{\alpha}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}}\mathscr{M}
, we conclude by applying Proposition
4.34
.
∎

Example 4.36

(Generalized Mellin transform)
.

Let
f_{1},\dots,f_{p}
be meromorphic functions on
X
, i.e., locally each
f_{i}
is the quotient of two holomorphic functions
h_{i},g_{i}
without common factor. Let
Y
be the union of the divisors of zeros and poles of the functions
f_{i}
, i.e., locally the divisors of zeros of
h_{i},g_{i}
. We also set
S=\mathbb{C}^{p}
with its analytic topology. It is usual to denote by
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*Y)f^{s}
the
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-module
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*Y)
equipped with the twisted connection
\mathrm{d}+\sum_{i}s_{i}\mathrm{d}f_{i}/f_{i}
. The same argument as in Corollary
4.35
shows that
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*Y)f^{s}
is regular holonomic and that, if
\mathscr{M}
is a regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X}
-module, then the
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module
q^{*}(\mathscr{M}(*Y))f^{s}:=q^{*}\mathscr{M}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*Y)f^{s}
(where
q:X\times\penalty S\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X
denotes the projection) is also regular holonomic.

Furthermore, the
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-submodule of
q^{*}(\mathscr{M}(*Y))f^{s}
generated by the image of
q^{*}\mathscr{M}\otimes 1\cdot f^{s}
is also regular holonomic, according to Proposition
3.2
(
iii
), as it is clearly coherent (being locally of finite type in a coherent
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module). This property is the
S
-analytic variant of
[
Mai23
, Prop. 13]
, with the regularity assumption however.

Since
\mathscr{M}
is regular holonomic, it is good, and so is
q^{*}(\mathscr{M}(*Y))f^{s}
. Therefore, if
X
is compact, the
\mathscr{D}
-module pushforward
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}p_{*}\bigl[q^{*}(\mathscr{M}(*Y))f^{s}\bigr]
is an object of
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{O}_{S})
. This is the generalized Mellin transform of
\mathscr{M}
with respect to
(f_{1},\dots,f_{p})
.

4.f
Another characterization of regular holonomicity

For a closed analytic subset
Y
of
X
, we denote by
\mathscr{O}_{\widehat{Y\times\penalty S}}=\varprojlim_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}/\mathscr{J}^{k}
the formal completion of
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
along
Y\times\penalty S
, where
\mathscr{J}
denotes the defining ideal of
Y\times\penalty S
in
X\times\penalty S
. Let
i:Y\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}X
denote the inclusion. We consider the exact sequence of sheaves supported on
Y\times\penalty S
:

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}i_{*}i^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{O}_{\widehat{Y\times\penalty S}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{Q}_{Y\times\penalty S}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0.

Corollary 4.37
.

If
\mathscr{M}\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
, then the complexes

R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{O}_{\widehat{Y\times\penalty S}})\quad\text{and}\quad R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{Q}_{Y\times\penalty S})

belong to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
.

Proof.

If follows from Corollary
4.32
that we have
R\Gamma_{[Y]}\mathscr{M}\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
, so that the complex
R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(R\Gamma_{[Y]}\mathscr{M},\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S})
belongs to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
, according to
[
MFS13
, Th. 3.7]
. On the one hand, by mimicking the proof when
S
is reduced to a point (cf. e.g.
[
Meb04
, Cor. 2.7-2]
), one finds a natural isomorphism

R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(R\Gamma_{[Y]}\mathscr{M};\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{O}_{\widehat{Y\times\penalty S}}),

hence the
S
-
\mathbb{C}
-constructibility of the latter complex. On the other hand, we have natural isomorphisms

\displaystyle Ri_{*}i^{-1}R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}Ri_{*}R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(i^{-1}\mathscr{M},i^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S})

\displaystyle\simeq R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},Ri_{*}i^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}),

showing
S
-
\mathbb{C}
-constructibility of the latter complex, and therefore that of
R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{Q}_{Y\times\penalty S})
.
∎

Theorem 4.38
.

Let
\mathscr{M}
belong to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{hol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
. Then
\mathscr{M}
is regular holonomic if and only if for any germ of closed analytic subset
Y\subset X
,
R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{Q}_{Y\times\penalty S})
is isomorphic to zero.

Lemma 4.39
.

For any closed analytic subset
Y\subset X
we have

Li^{*}_{s}\mathscr{O}_{\widehat{Y\times\penalty S}}\simeq\mathscr{O}_{\widehat{Y}}\quad\text{and}\quad Li^{*}_{s}\mathscr{Q}_{Y\times\penalty S}\simeq\mathscr{Q}_{Y}.

Proof.

Thanks to the properties of
Li^{*}_{s}
(cf. Proposition
3.1
) the result follows from Mittag-Leffler’s condition since the morphisms
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}/\mathscr{J}^{k+1}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}/\mathscr{J}^{k}
are surjective.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4.38
.

We first remark that the theorem holds if
S
is reduced to a point, according to
[
KK81
, (6.4.6) & (6.4.7)]
.

If
R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{Q}_{Y\times\penalty S})=0
for all germ
Y\subset X
, then for any
s\in S

\begin{split}R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X}}(Li^{*}_{s}\mathscr{M},\mathscr{Q}_{Y})&\simeq R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X}}(Li^{*}_{s}\mathscr{M},Li^{*}_{s}\mathscr{Q}_{Y\times\penalty S})\\
&\simeq Li_{s}^{*}R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{Q}_{Y\times\penalty S})=0.\end{split}

(4.40)

From the preliminary remark we conclude that
Li^{*}_{s}\mathscr{M}\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X})
for any
s\in S
, hence
\mathscr{M}\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
.

Conversely, if
\mathscr{M}
is regular holonomic, then
R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{Q}_{Y\times\penalty S})
is
S
-
\mathbb{C}
-constructible, and by the variant of Nakayama’s lemma (cf.
[
MFS13
, Prop. 2.2]
), it is zero if (and only if)
Li_{s}^{*}R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{Q}_{Y\times\penalty S})=0
for any
s\in S
. Reading (
4.40
) backwards and according to the preliminary remark, we find that the latter property is satisfied, hence

R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{Q}_{Y\times\penalty S})=0.\qed

5
Construction of the relative Riemann-Hilbert functor
\RH_{X}^{S}

In this section, we extend the definition of the functor
\RH_{X}^{S}
introduced in
[
MFS19
]
when
\dim S=\penalty 1
to the case
\dim S\geqslant 2
. We will check that it satisfies properties similar to those explained in
[
MFS19
,
FMFS21
]
.

5.a
Reminder on the subanalytic site and complements

We first recall the main results in
[
MFP21
]
. We consider the site in the real analytic manifold
S_{\mathbb{R}}
given by the usual topology, that is, where the family
\Op(S)
consisting of all open sets. On the other hand, we have the subanalytic site
X_{\mathrm{sa}}
underlying the real analytic manifold
X_{\mathbb{R}}
for which the family of open subsets
\Op(X_{\mathrm{sa}})
consists of subanalytic open subsets in
X_{\mathbb{R}}
. Lastly, we let
X_{\mathrm{sa}}\times S
be the subanalytic site underlying the real analytic manifold
X_{\mathbb{R}}\times S_{\mathbb{R}}
, for which the family of open sets
\Op(X_{\mathrm{sa}}\times S)
consists of those which are finite unions of products
U\times V
with
U\in\Op(X_{\mathrm{sa}})
and
V
is open in
S
. A subset
T\subset\Op(X_{\mathrm{sa}}\times S)
is a covering of
W=U\times V\in\Op(X_{\mathrm{sa}}\times S)
if and only if it admits a refinement
\{U_{i}\times V_{j}\}_{i\in I,j\in J}
such that
\{U_{i}\}_{i\in\ I}
is a covering of
U
(in
X_{\mathrm{sa}}
) and
\{V_{j}\}_{j\in J}
is a covering of
V
(in
S
). In particular, when
U
is relatively compact
I
is finite but
J
needs not to be so even if
V
is relatively compact.

We have the following commutative diagram, where the arrows are natural morphisms of sites induced by the inclusion of families of open subsets.

\begin{array}[]{c}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 16.99994pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 45.99992pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{X_{\mathrm{sa}}\times S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 98.64919pt\raise-10.51389pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.15277pt\hbox{$\textstyle{a}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 121.0739pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 134.97488pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-16.99994pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\end{array}

(5.1)

We recall that

(5.2)

\rho_{S}^{-1}
commutes with tensor products (cf.
[
KS06
, Lem. 18.3.1(ii)(c)]
) and
\rho_{S}^{-1}\rho_{S*}\!\simeq\!\id
. Furthermore,
\rho_{S}^{-1}
admits a left adjoint
\rho_{S!}
which is exact and commutes with tensor products (cf.
[
MFP21
, §3]
).

The following proposition generalizes
[
MFS19
, Prop. 3.3]
. Its proof is completely similar.

Proposition 5.3
.

The category
\mathrm{Mod}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
is acyclic for
\rho_{S}
:

\forall\,F\in\mathrm{Mod}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}),\;\forall\,k\geqslant 1,\quad\mathscr{H}^{k}R\rho_{S*}F=0.

In particular
\rho_{S*}
is exact on
\mathrm{Mod}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
.

The next statement corrects
[
MFS19
, Prop. 3.5]
. Its proof is given in the appendix.

Proposition 5.4
.

Let
F^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}
be a bounded complex of
p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
-modules with
S
-
\mathbb{R}
-constructible cohomology. Then there exists an isomorphism
K^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}F^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}
in
\rD^{-}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
, where
K^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}
is a complex in
\rC^{-}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
whose terms are locally finite sums
\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{\alpha\in A}\mathbb{C}_{U_{\alpha}\times V_{\alpha}}\otimes p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
, where the
U_{\alpha}
are open subanalytic relatively compact in
X
and the
V_{\alpha}
are open relatively compact in
S
.

Remark 5.5
.

Recall the diagram (
5.1
).
From the fact that
a_{*}
is fully faithful and that
a^{-1}a_{*}=\id
, we have
\rho_{S*}=a^{-1}\rho^{\prime}_{*}
(thus
R\rho_{S*}=a^{-1}R\rho^{\prime}_{*}
) as explained in
[
MFP21
]
before Prop. 3.1, we deduce that, for any open subset
U\times V\in\Op^{\mathrm{c}}(X_{\mathrm{sa}}\times S)
, the constant sheaf
\mathbb{C}_{U\times V}
on the site
X_{\mathrm{sa}}\times S
coincides with
\rho_{S*}\mathbb{C}_{U\times V}
. Similarly,

R\rho_{S*}(\mathbb{C}_{U\times V}\otimes p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})\simeq\rho_{S*}(\mathbb{C}_{U\times V})\otimes\rho_{S*}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})\simeq\rho_{S*}(\mathbb{C}_{U\times V}\otimes p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})

since the isomorphisms hold true with
\rho^{\prime}
instead of
\rho_{S}
(cf.
[
MFP14
, Lem. 3.6(2)]
).

Remark 5.6
.

For any
S
, the site
X_{\mathrm{sa}}\times S
is a ringed site both relatively to the sheaf
\rho_{S*}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
and to the sheaf
\rho_{S!}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
(cf.
[
KS06
, p. 449]
), and
\rho_{S}
is a morphism of ringed sites in both cases. Thus, according to
[
KS06
, Lem. 18.3.1, Th. 18.6.9]
,
\rho_{S}^{-1}
commutes with
\otimes
,
\otimes_{\rho_{S*}p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}
,
\otimes_{\rho_{S!}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}}
,
\otimes^{L}_{\rho_{S*}p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}
and
\otimes^{L}_{\rho_{S!}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}}
.
We recall that
R\rho_{S*}p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}\simeq\rho_{S!}p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
(cf.
[
MFP21
, Prop. 3.16]
).
Let
\pi:S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
be a morphism of complex manifolds. Since
\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}
is a
\pi^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module,
\rho_{S^{\prime}*}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}})
is a
\rho_{S^{\prime}*}(p_{X}^{-1}\pi^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
-module hence a
\pi^{-1}\rho_{S*}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
-module. Similarly,
\rho_{S^{\prime}!}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}}
is a
\pi^{-1}\rho_{S!}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-module. In other words,
\pi
induces a morphism of ringed sites with respect to both sheaves of rings. Consequently, according to
[
KS06
, Th. 18.6.9(i)]
, the derived functors
L\pi^{*}:\rD(\rho_{S*}p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rD(\rho_{S^{\prime}*}p^{-1}_{X}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}})

resp.
(keeping the same notation
\pi
for the morphism
\id\times\pi
),
L\pi^{*}:\rD(\rho_{S!}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rD(\rho_{S^{\prime}!}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}})
are well-defined.

We recall that
\rho_{S}^{-1}R\rho_{S*}\simeq\id
, that
R\rho_{S*}
commutes with
R\pi_{*}
and
\rho_{S}^{-1}
commutes with
\pi^{-1}
according to
[
KS06
, Prop. 17.5.3]
. Furthermore
(\pi^{-1},\pi_{*})
is a pair of adjoint functors according to
[
KS06
, Th. 17.5.2(i)]
and
\rho_{S}^{-1}
commutes with
R\pi_{*}
,
which follows by copying the proof of
[
Pre08
, Prop. 2.2.1(ii)]
since
\rho_{S}^{-1}
admits an exact left adjoint (
\rho_{S!}
) hence takes injective sheaves to injective sheaves. (Note that, in general, we do not have
\pi^{-1}R\rho_{S*}=R\rho_{S^{\prime}*}\pi^{-1}
while we have
\pi^{-1}\rho_{S!}=\rho_{S^{\prime}!}\pi^{-1}
.) We thus have isomorphisms of functors

\rho_{S^{\prime}}^{-1}\pi^{-1}R\rho_{S*}\simeq\pi^{-1},\quad R\rho_{S^{\prime}*}\rho_{S^{\prime}}^{-1}\pi^{-1}R\rho_{S*}\simeq R\rho_{S^{\prime}*}\pi^{-1}

and, composing with the natural morphism
\id\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R\rho_{S^{\prime}*}\rho_{S^{\prime}}^{-1}
, we deduce a natural morphism

\pi^{-1}R\rho_{S*}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R\rho_{S^{\prime}*}\pi^{-1}.

(5.6
\,*
)

We have

\pi^{-1}\rho_{S*}(\mathbb{C}_{U\times V})=\pi^{-1}(\mathbb{C}_{U\times V})=\mathbb{C}_{U\times\pi^{-1}V}=\rho_{S^{\prime}*}(\mathbb{C}_{U\times\pi^{-1}V})=\rho_{S^{\prime}*}\pi^{-1}(\mathbb{C}_{U\times V}).

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
\pi:S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
be a morphism. Then we have an isomorphism of functors on
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
with values in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}}(\rho_{S^{\prime}*}p^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}})
:

L\pi^{*}R\rho_{S*}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})\simeq R\rho_{S^{\prime}*}L\pi^{*}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}).

Proof.

Let
F\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
.
By (
5.6

∗

) we have a morphism (recalling that
R\rho_{S*}p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}\simeq\rho_{S*}p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
)

\rho_{S^{\prime}*}p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}\otimes^{L}_{\pi^{-1}\rho_{S*}p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\pi^{-1}R\rho_{S*}F\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rho_{S^{\prime}*}p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}\otimes^{L}_{\pi^{-1}\rho_{S*}p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}R\rho_{S^{\prime}*}\pi^{-1}F\\
\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R\rho_{S^{\prime}*}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}\otimes^{L}_{\pi^{-1}p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\pi^{-1}F).

This gives the desired morphism
\tau_{F}:L\pi^{*}R\rho_{S*}(F)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R\rho_{S^{\prime}*}L\pi^{*}(F)
.
By Proposition
A.6
,
[
FMFS21
, Rem. 1.8]
and Remark
A.8
we may assume that
F=\mathbb{C}_{U\times V}\otimes p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
, for some relatively compact open subsets respectively of
X
and
S
, with
U
subanalytic.
We have

\displaystyle L\pi^{*}\rho_{S*}(F)

\displaystyle=\rho_{S^{\prime}*}(p^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}})\otimes^{L}_{\pi^{-1}\rho_{S*}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})}\pi^{-1}\rho_{S*}(\mathbb{C}_{U\times V}\otimes p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})

\displaystyle\simeq\rho_{S^{\prime}*}(p^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}})\otimes^{L}_{\pi^{-1}\rho_{S*}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})}\pi^{-1}\rho_{S*}(\mathbb{C}_{U\times V})\otimes\pi^{-1}\rho_{S*}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})

\displaystyle\simeq\rho_{S^{\prime}*}(p^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}})\otimes\pi^{-1}\rho_{S*}(\mathbb{C}_{U\times V})\simeq\rho_{S^{\prime}*}(p^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}})\otimes\rho_{S^{\prime}*}\pi^{-1}(\mathbb{C}_{U\times V})

\displaystyle\simeq\rho_{S^{\prime}*}(p^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}\otimes\mathbb{C}_{U\times\pi^{-1}V})\simeq\rho_{S^{\prime}*}(L\pi^{*}F).

∎

5.b
The construction of
\RH_{X}^{S}
and behaviour under pushforward

For this section, we refer to the notation introduced in
[
FMFS21
, §2.2]
. However, instead of making use of the morphism
\rho^{\prime}
of (
5.1
) as in loc. cit., we replace it with
\rho_{S}
. Most proofs do not need any change.

We define the triangulated functor
\THH_{X}^{S}:\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})^{\mathrm{op}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
by

\displaystyle\THH_{X}^{S}(F)

\displaystyle:=\rho_{S}^{-1}\rh_{\rho_{S*}p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(R\rho_{S*}F,\Db^{t,S}_{X\times\penalty S}),

\displaystyle\RH_{X}^{S}(F)

\displaystyle:=\rho_{S}^{-1}\rh_{\rho_{S*}p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(R\rho_{S*}F,\mathscr{O}^{t,S}_{X\times\penalty S})[d_{X}].

If
\dim S=1
we recover the definitions of
[
MFS19
]
(where we restricted to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})^{\mathrm{op}}
):

\displaystyle\THH_{X}^{S}(F)

\displaystyle:=\rho^{\prime-1}\rh_{\rho^{\prime}_{*}p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(\rho^{\prime}_{*}F,\Db^{t,S,\sharp}_{X\times\penalty S}),

\displaystyle\RH_{X}^{S}(F)

\displaystyle:=\rho^{\prime-1}\rh_{\rho^{\prime}_{*}p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(\rho^{\prime}_{*}F,\mathscr{O}^{t,S,\sharp}_{X\times\penalty S})[d_{X}].

This can be seen as follows:
noting that (cf. (
5.1
))
\rho^{\prime}_{*}=a_{*}\circ\rho_{S*}
,
a^{-1}a_{*}\!=\!\id
and
a^{-1}\mathscr{O}^{t,S,\sharp}_{X\times\penalty S}=\mathscr{O}^{t,S}_{X\times\penalty S}
(cf.
[
MFP21
, §§3.1 &  3.2]
), for
F\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
,
one finds a natural morphism

\rho^{\prime-1}\rh_{\rho^{\prime}_{*}p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(\rho^{\prime}_{*}F,\mathscr{O}^{t,S,\sharp}_{X\times\penalty S})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rho_{S}^{-1}\rh_{\rho_{S*}p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(\rho_{S*}F,\mathscr{O}^{t,S}_{X\times\penalty S}).

Let us check that is an isomorphism. The question being local, we can reduce, according to Proposition
5.4
, to proving the isomorphism for sheaves of the form
F=\mathbb{C}_{U\times V}\otimes p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
, for some open subanalytic relatively compact subset
U
of
X
and
V
open in
S
. Both objects become then isomorphic to
R\Gamma_{X\times V}T\mathscr{H}om(\mathbb{C}_{U\times S},\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S})
, as follows from
[
MFP14
, Props. 4.1 & 4.7]
and
[
MFP21
, Prop. 3.24]
.

Let
U
be a subanalytic open subset in
X
and let us denote by
j:U\times S\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X\times\penalty S
the inclusion.

Lemma 5.8

(Extension in the case of an open subanalytic set)
.

Let
F\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}(p^{-1}_{U}\mathscr{O}_{S})
. Then there are natural isomorphisms in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})

\displaystyle\THH_{X}^{S}(j_{!}F)

\displaystyle\simeq\rho_{S}^{-1}Rj_{*}R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\rho_{S*}p_{U}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(R\rho_{S*}F,j^{-1}\Db^{t,S}_{X\times\penalty S}),

(
5.8
\,*
)

\displaystyle\RH_{X}^{S}(j_{!}F)[-d_{X}]

\displaystyle\simeq\rho_{S}^{-1}Rj_{*}R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\rho_{S*}p_{U}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(R\rho_{S*}F,j^{-1}\mathscr{O}^{t,S}_{X\times\penalty S}).

(
5.8
\,**
)

Proof.

This lemma is a variant of
[
MFS19
, Lem. 3.25]
and its proof is similar to that of loc. cit. ∎

We have the variant of
[
FMFS21
, Prop. 2.1]
using
[
MFP21
, Prop. 3.30]
:

Proposition 5.9
.

For each
s\in S
we have an isomorphism of functors

Li^{*}_{s}\RH^{S}_{X}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})[-d_{X}]\simeq T\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}(Li^{*}_{s}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}),\mathscr{O}_{X}).

We have the relative version of
[
Kas84
, Th. 4.1]
:

Theorem 5.10
.

Let
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y
be a morphism of real analytic manifolds, let
F\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}}(p_{Y}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
. Then we have a canonical morphism in
\rD(\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S/S})
, functorial with respect to
F
and compatible in a natural way with the composition of morphisms

{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{!}\THH_{X}^{S}(f^{-1}F)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\THH_{Y}^{S}(F).

The proof is stepwise similar to that with
\dim S=1
in
[
FMFS21
, Lem. 2.5]
using Proposition
A.6
.
We omit it here and the detailed proof can be found in the arXiv version of this paper. Proposition 7.1 of
[
Kas84
]
(see also
[
KS96
, Th. 5.7  (5.12)]
) has a relative version already used in
[
MFS19
]
, whose proof is given below.

Theorem 5.11
.

Let
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y
be a morphism of complex analytic manifolds, let
F\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
, and assume that
f
is proper on
\Supp F
. Then there is a canonical isomorphism in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
which is compatible in a natural way with the composition of morphisms

{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\RH_{X}^{S}(F)[d_{X}]{\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}\RH_{Y}^{S}(Rf_{*}F)[d_{Y}].

(5.11
\,*
)

Proof.

We recall (cf.
[
FMFS21
, (4)]
) that

\RH_{X}^{S}(F)\simeq\rh_{\mathscr{D}_{\overline{X}\times\overline{S}/\overline{S}}}(\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}\times\overline{S}},\THH^{S}(F))[d_{X}].

(5.12)

In view of Theorem
5.10
, where we replace
F
by
Rf_{*}F
, and by adjunction, we derive a natural morphism

\mu_{F}:{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\THH_{X}^{S}(F)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\THH_{Y}^{S}(Rf_{*}F).

(5.13)

Lemma 5.14
.

The morphism
\mu_{F}
is an isomorphism.

The proof of this lemma, which is a relative version of
[
KS96
, Th. 4.4]
, is given in the appendix and some more details are also given in the arXiv version of this article.

Applying
\rh_{\mathscr{D}_{\overline{Y}\times\overline{S}/\overline{S}}}(\mathscr{O}_{\overline{Y}\times\overline{S}},{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})[d_{Y}]
to both terms of (
5.13
) the right term becomes
\RH_{Y}^{S}(Rf_{*}F)
. For the left side of (
5.13
) we obtain

\displaystyle\rh_{\mathscr{D}_{\overline{Y}\times\overline{S}/\overline{S}}}(\mathscr{O}_{\overline{Y}\times\overline{S}},{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\THH_{X}^{S}(F))[d_{Y}]

\displaystyle\simeq\rh_{\mathscr{D}_{\overline{Y}\times\overline{S}/\overline{S}}}(\mathscr{O}_{\overline{Y}\times\overline{S}},Rf_{*}(\mathscr{D}_{Y_{\mathbb{R}}\leftarrow X_{\mathbb{R}}/S_{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes^{L}_{\mathscr{D}_{X_{\mathbb{R}}\times S_{\mathbb{R}}/S_{\mathbb{R}}}}\hskip-2.84526pt\THH_{X}^{S}(F)))[d_{Y}]

\displaystyle\simeq Rf_{*}(\rh_{f^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{\overline{Y}\times\overline{S}/\overline{S}}}(f^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{\overline{Y}\times\overline{S}},\mathscr{D}_{Y_{\mathbb{R}}\leftarrow X_{\mathbb{R}}/S_{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes^{L}_{\mathscr{D}_{X_{\mathbb{R}}\times S_{\mathbb{R}}/S_{\mathbb{R}}}}\hskip-2.84526pt\THH_{X}^{S}(F)))[d_{Y}]

\displaystyle\underset{\textup{(a)}}{\simeq}Rf_{*}(\rh_{f^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{\overline{Y}\times\overline{S}/\overline{S}}}(f^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{\overline{Y}\times\overline{S}},\mathscr{D}_{Y_{\mathbb{R}}\leftarrow X_{\mathbb{R}}/S_{\mathbb{R}}})\otimes^{L}_{\mathscr{D}_{Y_{\mathbb{R}}\times S_{\mathbb{R}}/S_{\mathbb{R}}}}\hskip-2.84526pt\THH_{X}^{S}(F))[d_{Y}]

\displaystyle\underset{\textup{(b)}}{\simeq}Rf_{*}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\leftarrow X/S}\otimes^{L}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\rh_{\mathscr{D}_{\overline{X}\times\overline{S}/\overline{S}}}(\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}\times\overline{S}},\mathscr{D}_{X_{\mathbb{R}}\times S_{\mathbb{R}}/S_{\mathbb{R}}})\otimes^{L}_{\mathscr{D}_{X_{\mathbb{R}}\times S_{\mathbb{R}}/S_{\mathbb{R}}}}\hskip-2.84526pt\THH_{X}^{S}(F)))[d_{Y}]

\displaystyle\simeq Rf_{*}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\leftarrow X/S}\otimes^{L}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\rh_{\mathscr{D}_{\overline{X}\times\overline{S}/\overline{S}}}(\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}\times\overline{S}},\THH_{X}^{S}(F)[d_{X}])).

Here (a) follows from
[
Kas03
, (A10)]
and (b) follows from the relative version of
[
Kas84
, Lem. 7.2]
which asserts that

\rh_{\mathscr{D}_{\overline{Y}\times\overline{S}/\overline{S}}}(\mathscr{O}_{\overline{Y}\times\overline{S}},\mathscr{D}_{Y_{\mathbb{R}}\leftarrow X_{\mathbb{R}}/S_{\mathbb{R}}})\\
\simeq\mathscr{D}_{Y\leftarrow X/S}\otimes^{L}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}\rh_{\mathscr{D}_{\overline{X}\times\overline{S}/\overline{S}}}(\mathscr{O}_{\overline{X}\times\overline{S}},\mathscr{D}_{X_{\mathbb{R}}\times S_{\mathbb{R}}/S_{\mathbb{R}}})[d_{X}-d_{Y}].

Therefore, by applying
\rh_{\mathscr{D}_{\overline{Y}\times\overline{S}/\overline{S}}}(\mathscr{O}_{\overline{Y}\times\overline{S}},{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})[d_{Y}]
to the left-hand side of (
5.13
) we obtain an isomorphism with
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{!}\RH^{S}_{X}(F)

which concludes the construction of the morphism (
5.11

∗

). Lemma
5.14
shows that it is an isomorphism.
∎

5.c
Riemann-Hilbert correspondence for Deligne’s extensions

We recall that, for
F\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
one defines
\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}F:=R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(F,p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
and
\boldsymbol{D}F:=R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(F,p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})[2d_{X}]
.

Let
L
be a coherent
S
-locally constant sheaf on
X^{*}\times\penalty S
.
We consider the setting of Notation
4.5
and assume that
Y=D
has normal crossings in
X
.

Proposition 5.15
.

Let
L
be a coherent
S
-locally constant sheaf on
X^{*}\times\penalty S
and let
\widetilde{E}_{L}
be the associated Deligne extension. Then

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

the complex of
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules
\RH_{X}^{S}(j_{!}\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}L)[-d_{X}]
is isomorphic to
\widetilde{E}_{L}
and thus it is regular holonomic,

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

\pSol\widetilde{E}_{L}\simeq j_{!}\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}L
.

Proof.

We adapt the idea of proof of
[
MFS19
, Lem. 4.2]
. Let us prove the second statement assuming the first one holds true. Firstly, the following lemma is similar to
[
MFS19
, Lem. 3.19]
:

Lemma 5.16
.

There exists an isomorphism of functors in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
:

\pDR_{X}(\RH_{X}^{S}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\boldsymbol{D}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}).

Since
\RH_{X}^{S}(j!\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}L)[-d_{X}]
is holonomic by the first point, we have
