\pSol_{X}\RH_{X}^{S}(j_{!}\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}L)[-d_{X}]\simeq\boldsymbol{D}\pDR\RH_{X}^{S}(j_{!}\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}L)[-d_{X}]\overset{(*)}{\simeq}\boldsymbol{D}\boldsymbol{D}(j_{!}\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}L)=j_{!}\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}L,

where
(*)
follows from Lemma
5.16
.

Let us now prove the first statement. We will set

\widetilde{E}^{S}_{L}=\RH_{X}^{S}(j_{!}\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}L)[-d_{X}].

Then, according to (

∗
⁣
∗

‣
5.8
) and to
[
MFS19
, Lem. 3.22]
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{E}^{S}_{L}

\displaystyle=\rho_{S}^{-1}Rj_{*}R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\rho_{S*}p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(\rho_{S*}\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}L,j^{-1}\mathscr{O}^{t,S}_{X\times\penalty S})

(5.17)

\displaystyle\simeq\rho_{S}^{-1}Rj_{*}(\rho_{S*}L\otimes^{L}_{\rho_{S*}p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}j^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}^{t,S}).

We make use of the following result of
[
MFP21
]
, the proof of which we recall with details as it is used in an essential way below.

Lemma 5.18

(
[
MFP21
, Prop. 3.32]
)
.

The complex
\widetilde{E}^{S}_{L}
is concentrated in degree zero.

Proof.

Assume first that
L
is the constant local system
p_{X^{*}}^{-1}G
with
G
being
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-coherent. Since the question is local on
X\times\penalty S
, we can assume that
G
admits a finite resolution
\mathscr{O}_{S}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}G
by free
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-modules of finite rank. Then we have

\rho_{S}^{-1}Rj_{*}(\rho_{S*}p_{X^{*}}^{-1}G\otimes_{p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}^{L}j^{-1}\mathscr{O}^{t,S}_{X\times\penalty S})\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longleftarrow}}\rho_{S}^{-1}Rj_{*}(\rho_{S*}p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\otimes_{p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}j^{-1}\mathscr{O}^{t,S}_{X\times\penalty S})\\
\simeq p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\otimes_{p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\rho_{S}^{-1}Rj_{*}j^{-1}\mathscr{O}^{t,S}_{X\times\penalty S}.

(5.19)

We recall that, according to
[
KS01
, Prop. 2.4.4 (2.4.4)]
, we have isomorphisms of functors on
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathbb{C}_{X_{\mathrm{sa}}\times S})

R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}(\rho_{S*}j_{!}\mathbb{C}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S},({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}Rj_{*}R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}(\rho_{S*}\mathbb{C}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S},j^{-1}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\simeq Rj_{*}j^{-1}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}).

As a consequence, we obtain an isomorphism

\rho^{-1}_{S}Rj_{*}j^{-1}\mathscr{O}^{t,S}_{X\times\penalty S}\simeq\rho_{S}^{-1}R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}(\mathbb{C}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S},\mathscr{O}^{t,S}_{X\times\penalty S}).

It follows then from
[
MFP21
, Prop. 3.24(1)]
applied to
\mathbb{C}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}=\mathbb{C}_{X^{*}}\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{S}
, that the latter complex is isomorphic to
\tho(\mathbb{C}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S},\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S})
, so that, according to
[
Kas84
, Lem. 7.5]

\rho^{-1}_{S}Rj_{*}j^{-1}\mathscr{O}^{t,S}_{X\times\penalty S}\simeq\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D).

(5.20)

We thus deduce from (
5.19
) and (
5.20
):

\displaystyle\rho_{S}^{-1}Rj_{*}(\rho_{S*}p_{X^{*}}^{-1}G\otimes_{p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}^{L}j^{-1}

\displaystyle\mathscr{O}^{t,S}_{X\times\penalty S})

(5.21)

\displaystyle\simeq\rho_{S}^{-1}Rj_{*}(\rho_{S*}p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\otimes_{p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}j^{-1}\mathscr{O}^{t,S}_{X\times\penalty S})

\displaystyle\simeq p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\otimes_{p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\rho_{S}^{-1}Rj_{*}j^{-1}\mathscr{O}^{t,S}_{X\times\penalty S}

\displaystyle\simeq p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\otimes_{p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)

\displaystyle\simeq p_{X}^{-1}G\otimes_{p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D),

where the latter isomorphism follows from the
p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
-flatness of
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)
. We conclude that
\widetilde{E}^{S}_{p_{X^{*}}^{-1}G}
is concentrated in degree zero.

Let us now consider the case of a possibly non constant
S
-local system
L
locally isomorphic to
p^{-1}G
, with
G
being
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-coherent. According to Remark
4.18
, locally on
X\times\penalty S
we have an isomorphism of
\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}
-modules

L\otimes_{p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}\simeq p_{X^{*}}^{-1}G\otimes_{p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S},

(5.22)

and by flatness of
\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}
over
p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
, the same equality holds with the derived tensor product
\otimes^{L}
. According to Proposition
5.3
,
the natural morphism
\rho_{S*}L\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R\rho_{S*}L
is an isomorphism and
[
MFP21
, Prop. 3.16]
implies that
\rho_{S!}L\simeq R\rho_{S*}L
. Recall also that, by
[
MFP21
, Prop. 3.13]
, the functor
\rho_{S!}
is exact and commutes with
\otimes
. From (
5.22
) we then derive
\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}
-linear isomorphisms (they are a priori not
\mathscr{D}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S/S}
-linear since we use a tensor product over
\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}
):

\displaystyle\rho_{S*}L\otimes^{L}_{\rho_{S*}p_{X*}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}j^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}^{t,S}

\displaystyle\simeq(\rho_{S!}L\otimes^{L}_{\rho_{S!}p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\rho_{S!}\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S})\otimes^{L}_{\rho_{S!}\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}}j^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}^{t,S}

\displaystyle\simeq(\rho_{S!}(L\otimes^{L}_{p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}))\otimes^{L}_{\rho_{S!}\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}}j^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}^{t,S}

\displaystyle\simeq(\rho_{S!}(p_{X^{*}}^{-1}G\otimes^{L}_{p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}))\otimes^{L}_{\rho_{S!}\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}}j^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}^{t,S}

\displaystyle\simeq(\rho_{S!}p_{X^{*}}^{-1}G\otimes^{L}_{\rho_{S!}p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\rho_{S!}\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S})\otimes^{L}_{\rho_{S!}\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}}j^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}^{t,S}

\displaystyle\simeq\rho_{S*}p_{{}_{X}^{*}}^{-1}G\otimes^{L}_{\rho_{S*}p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}j^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}^{t,S}.

Then, due to (
5.17
), we deduce an
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-linear isomorphism

\widetilde{E}^{S}_{L}\simeq\widetilde{E}^{S}_{p_{X^{*}}^{-1}G}\simeq p_{X}^{-1}G\otimes_{p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D).

(5.23)

By the first part of the proof, we deduce that
\widetilde{E}^{S}_{L}
is concentrated in degree zero. This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.18
.
∎

Lemma 5.24
.

Both
\widetilde{E}_{L}
and
\widetilde{E}^{S}_{L}
are naturally
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-submodules of
j_{*}E_{L}
.

Proof.

Firstly, applying the commutation of
\rho_{S}^{-1}
with
j^{-1}
together with the analogue of
[
MFS19
, Cor. 3.24]
entails that
\widetilde{E}^{S}_{L}
and
\widetilde{E}_{L}
coincide with
E_{L}
when restricted to
X^{*}\times\penalty S
.

On the one hand, by construction,
\widetilde{E}_{L}
is naturally a
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-submodule of
j_{*}E_{L}
. Let us check on the other hand that
\widetilde{E}^{S}_{L}
is also naturally a
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-submodule of
j_{*}E_{L}
. From the natural morphism of functors
\id\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R\rho_{S*}\rho_{S}^{-1}
on
\mathrm{Mod}(\mathbb{C}_{X_{\mathrm{sa}}\times S})
we derive a natural morphism
\rho_{S}^{-1}Rj_{*}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Rj_{*}\rho_{S}^{-1}
: denoting for a moment by
j_{*}^{S}
the morphism in the subanalytic site, we have an isomorphism of functors
Rj_{*}^{S}\circ R\rho_{S*}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}R\rho_{S*}\circ Rj_{*}
(cf.
[
Pre08
, Prop. 2.2.1(i)]
, by replacing there
\rho
by
\rho_{S}
and using the same argument), hence a morphism

Rj_{*}^{S}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Rj_{*}^{S}\circ R\rho_{S*}\rho_{S}^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}R\rho_{S*}Rj_{*}^{S}\rho_{S}^{-1},

and, by applying
\rho_{S}^{-1}
on the left, we obtain the desired morphism. Recall also, as already used, that
\rho_{S*}L\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}R\rho_{S*}L
. We then deduce a
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-linear morphism

\displaystyle\Psi:\widetilde{E}^{S}_{L}\simeq\rho_{S}^{-1}Rj_{*}(\rho_{S*}L

\displaystyle\otimes^{L}_{\rho_{S*}p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}j^{-1}\mathscr{O}^{t,S}_{X\times\penalty S})

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Rj_{*}\rho_{S}^{-1}(\rho_{S*}L\otimes^{L}_{\rho_{S*}p_{X*}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}j^{-1}\mathscr{O}^{t,S}_{X\times\penalty S})

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{(a)}}{\simeq}Rj_{*}(L\otimes^{L}_{p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\rho_{S}^{-1}j^{-1}\mathscr{O}^{t,S}_{X\times\penalty S})

\displaystyle\simeq Rj_{*}(L\otimes^{L}_{p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S})

\displaystyle\overset{\textup{(b)}}{\simeq}Rj_{*}(L\otimes_{p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S})=Rj_{*}E_{L}\overset{\textup{(c)}}{\simeq}j_{*}E_{L},

where (a) follows from (
(5.2)
), (b) follows from the
p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
-flatness of
\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}
and (c) from the Steinness of
j
.

In order to check that
\Psi
is injective, it is enough to consider it as an
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-linear morphism, and by (
5.23
), it is enough to check injectivity when
L=p_{X^{*}}^{-1}G
. Furthermore, the question is local on
S
.

In such a case, we consider a resolution
\mathscr{O}_{S}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}G
as in the proof of Proposition
5.18
. Then, locally on
S
, (
5.21
) identifies
\widetilde{E}^{S}_{p_{X^{*}}^{-1}G}
with
p_{X}^{-1}G\otimes_{p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)
and the morphism
\Psi
with the natural morphism

p_{X}^{-1}G\otimes_{p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}j_{*}(p_{X^{*}}^{-1}G\otimes_{p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times\penalty S}),

which is injective since
G
is
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-coherent, as wanted.
∎

End of the proof of Proposition
5.15

By Lemma
5.24
, we are reduced to showing that both
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-submodules
\widetilde{E}^{S}_{L}
and
\widetilde{E}_{L}
of
j_{*}E_{L}
coincide, and since the
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-structure is induced by that
j_{*}E_{L}
for both, it is enough to check that they coincide as
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-submodules. The question is local on
X\times\penalty S
. By Remark
4.18
, there exists a
local

\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-linear isomorphism
j_{*}E_{L}\simeq j_{*}\widehat{E}_{p_{X^{*}}^{-1}G}
under which
\widetilde{E}_{L}
is identified with
\widehat{E}_{G}=p_{X}^{-1}G\otimes_{p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)
. Besides, (
5.23
) also identifies
\widetilde{E}^{S}_{L}
with
p_{X}^{-1}G\otimes_{p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)
as an
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-module under the same local isomorphism, so the
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-submodules
\widetilde{E}_{L}
and
\widetilde{E}^{S}_{L}
of
j_{*}E_{L}
locally coincide, as desired.
∎

6
Proof of Theorem
2

The proof of Theorem
2
is similar to that of
[
FMFS21
, Th. 1]

where
\dim S=1
. However, various improvements are necessary in order to handle the case
\dim S\geqslant 2
.

6.a
First part of the proof of Theorem
2

We prove that
\RH_{X}^{S}
is a right quasi-inverse of the functor
\pSol_{X}:\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
by exhibiting an isomorphism of functors
\alpha:\id_{\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\pSol_{X}\circ\RH_{X}^{S}
. This is the analogue in possibly higher dimension for
S
of
[
MFS19
, Th. 3]
:

(6.1)

For
F
in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
,
\RH_{X}^{S}(F)
is an object of
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
and there exists a functorial isomorphism
\alpha_{F}:F\simeq\pSol_{X}(\RH_{X}^{S}(F))
in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
.

Proof of (
(6.1)
).

We recall the notation for the duality functor (cf.
[
MFS13
, Prop. 2.23]
): for
F
in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
, we set
\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}F=R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(F,p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
and
\boldsymbol{D}F=\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}F[2\dim X]
. Functoriality in (
(6.1)
) is obtained by means of Lemma
5.16
: once we know that, for
F
in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
,
\RH_{X}^{S}(F)
belongs to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
, we can apply
[
MFS13
, Cor. 3.9]
together with bi-duality in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
(cf.
[
MFS13
, Prop. 2.23]
) to obtain a functorial isomorphism

\alpha_{F}:F\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\pSol_{X}(\RH_{X}^{S}(F)).

For the first part of (
(6.1)
), the main step is provided by Proposition
5.15
, where we proved the case
F=j_{!}\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}L
in the setting of Section
4.b
(from which we keep the notation), that is,
D
is a normal crossing divisor in
X
and
L
is a coherent
S
-local system on
X^{*}\times\penalty S:=(X\smallsetminus D)\times S
.

We now conclude the proof of (
(6.1)
) by a standard induction on the dimension of the
X
-support of
F
, based on Theorem
5.11
, analogous to that of
[
Kas84
, §7.3]
(cf.
[
MFS19
, Th. 3]
for the case
\dim S=1
).

We thus assume that (
(6.1)
) holds if
\dim\Supp_{X}F<k
(
k\geqslant 1
) and we prove that it holds for any
F
with
\dim\Supp_{X}F\leqslant k
. By functoriality, it is enough to prove the first part of (
(6.1)
), so that the question is local. By induction on the amplitude of the complex
F
, we can also assume that
F
is an
S
-
\mathbb{C}
-constructible sheaf. We can find a projective morphism
f:X^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\penalty X
with
\dim X^{\prime}=k
, which is biholomorphic from the complement
X^{\prime*}
of a normal crossing divisor
D^{\prime}
in
X^{\prime}
to the smooth locus of dimension
k
of
\Supp_{X}F
, so that
f^{-1}F
is a coherent
S
-local system on
X^{\prime*}
. Let
j^{\prime}:X^{\prime*}\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}X^{\prime}
denote the inclusion. Proposition
5.15
implies the regular holonomicity of
\RH^{S}(j^{\prime}_{!}\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}j^{\prime-1}f^{-1}F)
and Theorem
5.11
, together with
[
MFS19
, Cor. 2.4]
, implies regular holonomicity of

\RH_{X}^{S}(Rf_{*}j^{\prime}_{!}\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}j^{\prime-1}f^{-1}F)\simeq\RH_{X}^{S}(j_{!}\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}j^{-1}F),

where
j
denotes the inclusion of
f(X^{\prime*})
in
X
. Hence, if
F
is an
S
-
\mathbb{C}
-constructible sheaf with
X
-support of dimension
\leqslant k
,
\RH_{X}^{S}(\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}F)
is regular holonomic since it fits in a distinguished triangle whose third term is regular holonomic by the induction hypothesis. The same holds for
\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}F
for any
F\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
with
\dim\Supp_{X}F\leqslant k
, and replacing
F
with
\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}F
, which has the same
X
-support, we conclude that
\RH_{X}^{S}(F)
is regular holonomic.
∎

6.b
Second part of the proof of Theorem
2

We prove that
\RH_{X}^{S}
is a left quasi-inverse of the functor
\pSol_{X}:\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
by exhibiting an isomorphism of functors
\beta:\id_{\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\RH_{X}^{S}\circ\pSol_{X}
.

(6.2)

For each object
\mathscr{M}
of
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
, there exists an isomorphism

\beta_{\mathscr{M}}:\mathscr{M}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\RH_{X}^{S}(\pSol_{X}(\mathscr{M})),

functorial in
\mathscr{M}
.

Proof of (
(6.2)
)

As in
[
FMFS21
, §3.b]
, we construct a bi-functorial isomorphism (with respect to
\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N}\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
) which, by applying
Li^{*}_{s}
for any
s\in S
, yields that of
[
Kas84
, Cor. 8.6]
:

\Hom_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\RH^{S}_{X}(\pSol_{X}(\mathscr{N})))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\Hom_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(\pSol_{X}(\mathscr{N}),\pSol_{X}(\mathscr{M})),

(6.3)

that we also denote as
\eqref{eq:HomRSol}_{\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N}}
. We then define the morphism

\beta_{\mathscr{M}}:\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\RH^{S}_{X}(\pSol_{X}(\mathscr{M}))

as the unique morphism such that
\eqref{eq:HomRSol}_{\mathscr{M},\mathscr{M}}(\beta_{\mathscr{M}})=\id_{\pSol(\mathscr{M})}
. One classically deduces from the full faithfulness of
\pSol
in the absolute case (a consequence of the Riemann-Hilbert correspondence of
[
Kas84
]
&
[
Meb84
]
) that, for each
s\in S
,
\beta_{Li^{*}_{s}\mathscr{M}}
is an isomorphism. Therefore, by a Nakayama-type argument
[
MFS19
, Prop. 1.9 & Cor. 1.10]
,
\beta_{\mathscr{M}}
is an isomorphism.

In order to check that
\beta_{\mathscr{M}}
is functorial with respect to
\mathscr{M}
, that is, it defines a morphism of functors
\id_{\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\RH^{S}_{X}\circ\pSol_{X}
, we consider as in
[
MFS19
, p. 668]
, for a morphism
\varphi:\mathscr{M}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{N}
, the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\Hom_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\RH^{S}_{X}(\pSol_{X}(\mathscr{M})))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\eqref{eq:HomRSol}_{\mathscr{M},\mathscr{M}}}
\textstyle{\RH^{S}(\pSol_{X}(\varphi))\circ{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}
\textstyle{\Hom_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(\pSol_{X}(\mathscr{M}),\pSol(\mathscr{M}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\circ\pSol_{X}(\varphi)}
\textstyle{\Hom_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\RH^{S}_{X}(\pSol_{X}(\mathscr{N})))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\eqref{eq:HomRSol}_{\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N}}}
\textstyle{\Hom_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(\pSol_{X}(\mathscr{N}),\pSol_{X}(\mathscr{M}))}
\textstyle{\Hom_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{N},\RH^{S}_{X}(\pSol_{X}(\mathscr{N})))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\eqref{eq:HomRSol}_{\mathscr{N},\mathscr{N}}}
\textstyle{{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\circ\varphi}
\textstyle{\Hom_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(\pSol_{X}(\mathscr{N}),\pSol_{X}(\mathscr{N}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\pSol_{X}(\varphi)\circ{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}

Since
\id_{\pSol_{X}(\mathscr{M})}\circ\pSol_{X}(\varphi)=\pSol_{X}(\varphi)\circ\id_{\pSol_{X}(\mathscr{N})}
, we obtain from the commutativity of the diagram that

\RH_{X}^{S}(\pSol_{X}(\varphi))\circ\beta_{\mathscr{M}}=\beta_{\mathscr{N}}\circ\varphi,

which is the desired functoriality.

In order to obtain (
6.3
), it is enough to construct a bi-functorial morphism in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})

R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\RH^{S}_{X}(F))\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(F,\pSol_{X}(\mathscr{M}))

(6.4)

for
\mathscr{M}\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
and
F\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
, and to show that it is an isomorphism. Then
\eqref{eq:HomRSol}_{\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N}}
is obtained by taking global sections of
\mathscr{H}^{0}\eqref{eq:HomRSolloc}
applied to
F=\pSol(\mathscr{N})
.

Such a morphism (
6.4
) is given by
[
FMFS21
, (14)]
. In view of Proposition
5.9
we can argue as in loc. cit., where it is also shown that, for each
s_{o}\in S
,
Li_{s_{o}}^{*}\eqref{eq:HomRSolloc}
can be identified with the morphism constructed in the absolute case by Kashiwara (
[
Kas84
, Cor. 8.6]
), hence it is an isomorphism. To conclude that (
6.4
) is an isomorphism, we apply a Nakayama-type argument
[
MFS13
, Prop. 2.2]
.

To construct the morphism (
6.4
) we need to check a finiteness property. The proof of (
(6.2)
) will thus be concluded with the proof of the following assertion.

(6.5)

For any
\mathscr{M}\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
and for any
F\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
,
R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\RH^{S}_{X}(F))
belongs to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
.

We also consider the statement:

(6.6)

For any
\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N}\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
, the complex
R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N})
belongs to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
.

We shall argue by induction on the pair
(\dim S,\dim\Supp_{X}\mathscr{M})
ordered lexicographically. For that purpose we introduce the following notations:

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

For
d\geqslant 0
, we denote by
\eqref{Tequivtf}_{d}
,
resp.

\eqref{Rhom}_{d}
the corresponding statement concerning any
S
satisfying
\dim S\leqslant d
.

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

For
k\geqslant 0
we introduce the assertion
\eqref{Rhom}_{d,k}
by requiring that the property holds for
\dim S\!\leqslant\!d-1
or
\dim S\!=\!d
and
\dim\Supp_{X}\mathscr{M}\!\leqslant\!k
.

Because of the first part of (
(6.1)
) already proved, we have

\eqref{Rhom}_{d}\implies\eqref{Tequivtf}_{d}.

Conversely, the reverse implication also holds:

\eqref{Tequivtf}_{d}\implies\eqref{Rhom}_{d}.

Indeed we deduce from
\eqref{Tequivtf}_{d}
an isomorphism
\beta_{\mathscr{N}}:\mathscr{N}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\RH^{S}_{X}(\pSol_{X}(\mathscr{N}))
, so applying
\eqref{Tequivtf}_{d}
with
F=\pSol_{X}(\mathscr{N})
gives
\eqref{Rhom}_{d}
. In the following, we will focus on
\eqref{Rhom}_{d}
. Since belonging to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
is a local property (cf.
[
MFS13
, §2.5]
), we will allow restriction to an open neighborhood of a point of
X\times\penalty S
when needed.

Proof of
\eqref{Rhom}_{d}
.

The proof of
\eqref{Rhom}_{d}
is done by induction on
d
, in order to reduce the assertion to the particular case of Lemma
6.15
below. Let us recall that
\eqref{Rhom}_{0}
holds, according to
[
Kas84
]
. So we assume that
\eqref{Rhom}_{d-1}
holds (
d\geqslant 1
) and we consider
S
with
\dim S=d
, together with any
\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N}\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
.

Step 1: The
S
-torsion case

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
\dim S=d
and let us assume that
\eqref{Rhom}_{d-1}
holds. Let
\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N}\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
and assume that the cohomology of
\mathscr{M}
or that of
\mathscr{N}
is of
S
-torsion. Then
\eqref{Rhom}_{d}
holds for
\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N}
, that is,

R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N})\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}).

Proof.

By the biduality isomorphism of
[
MFS13
, (3)]
, we have

R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N})\simeq R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\boldsymbol{D}\mathscr{N},\boldsymbol{D}\mathscr{M}),

(6.8)

and we recall that both
\boldsymbol{D}\mathscr{N}
and
\boldsymbol{D}\mathscr{M}
are regular holonomic according to Corollary
3.9
. It is thus enough to prove Lemma
6.7
when the cohomology of
\mathscr{N}
is of
S
-torsion. On the other hand, owing to the definition of
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
in terms of micro-support (cf.
[
MFS13
, §2.5]
), if two terms of a distinguished triangle in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
are objects of
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
, then so does the third one. This property allows us to assume that
\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N}
are regular holonomic

\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules instead of complexes. Lastly, the same argument implies that belonging to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
is a local question on
X\times\penalty S
.

The assumption on
\mathscr{N}
entails that
\Supp_{S}\mathscr{N}
is, locally with respect to
X\times\penalty S
, contained in a closed analytic subset
T
of
S
such that
\dim T<\dim S
. We will argue by induction on
\dim T
.

If
\dim T=0
, as we consider a local situation, we may assume
T=\{s_{o}\}
with maximal ideal sheaf
\mathfrak{m}_{s_{o}}
. By considering the (locally) finite filtration of
\mathscr{N}
by the
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-regular holonomic submodules
\mathfrak{m}_{s_{o}}^{k}\mathscr{N}
(
k\in\mathbb{N}
), we may reduce to assume
\mathfrak{m}_{s_{o}}\mathscr{N}=0
. We then have
\mathscr{N}\simeq i_{s_{0}*}i^{*}_{s_{0}}\mathscr{N}
and by adjunction

R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},i_{s_{o}*}i^{*}_{s_{0}}\mathscr{N})\simeq Ri_{s_{0}*}R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X}}(Li^{*}_{s_{0}}\mathscr{M},i^{*}_{s_{0}}\mathscr{N}),

so that the proof is reduced to applying
\eqref{Rhom}_{0}
or, equivalently, the absolute case proved by Kashiwara in
[
Kas84
]
.

Assume now that
\dim T\geqslant 1
and let
T_{0}\subset T
be a closed analytic subset of dimension
<\dim T
such that
T\smallsetminus T_{0}
is non singular of dimension
\dim T
. Let
\pi:S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
be a projective morphism of complex manifolds satisfying the two conditions:
\pi(S^{\prime})=T
and, setting
T^{\prime}_{0}:=\pi^{-1}(T_{0})
,
\pi^{\prime}:=\pi|_{S^{\prime}\smallsetminus T^{\prime}_{0}}:S^{\prime}\smallsetminus T^{\prime}_{0}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}T\smallsetminus T_{0}
is a biholomorphism. In particular
\dim S^{\prime}=\dim T<d
.

Note that, according to Proposition
3.10
,
L\pi^{*}\mathscr{M}
,
L\pi^{*}\mathscr{N}
are regular holonomic on
X\times\penalty S^{\prime}
and, by Theorem
3.15
,
R\pi_{*}L\pi^{*}\mathscr{N}
belongs to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
. By
\eqref{Rhom}_{d-1}
,
R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}}(L\pi^{*}\mathscr{M},L\pi^{*}\mathscr{N})\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}}(p^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}})
thus, by the properness of
\pi
and Lemma
2.16
,

R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},R\pi_{*}L\pi^{*}\mathscr{N})\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}).

The cones of the natural morphisms
R\pi_{*}L\pi^{*}\mathscr{N}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R\pi_{*}\pi^{*}\mathscr{N}
and
\mathscr{N}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R\pi_{*}\pi^{*}\mathscr{N}
are supported in
T_{0}
, so that, by the induction hypothesis we deduce that

\displaystyle R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},R\pi_{*}\pi^{*}\mathscr{N})\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}),

and thus

\displaystyle R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N})\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}).

∎

Step 2: Induction on
\dim\Supp_{X}\mathscr{M}

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
\dim S=d\geqslant 1
and let us assume that
\eqref{Rhom}_{d-1}
holds. Then
\eqref{Rhom}_{d,0}
holds.

Proof.

This is a consequence of Theorem
1
and adjunction. Indeed, we can assume that
\Supp_{X}\mathscr{M}=\{x\}
. Denoting by
i:\{x\}\times S\mathrel{\lhook\joinrel\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}X\times\penalty S
the inclusion, there exists a coherent
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module
\mathscr{M}_{0}
such that
\mathscr{M}={}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}i_{*}\mathscr{M}_{0}
by Kashiwara’s equivalence, and we have (cf.
[
Kas03
, Th. 4.33]
, which can be proved in a simple way in the present setting)

Ri_{*}R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{O}_{S}}(\mathscr{M}_{0},{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}i^{*}\mathscr{N})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N})[\dim X].

By Theorem
1
,
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}i^{*}\mathscr{N}
has
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-coherent cohomology, and the assertion follows.
∎

We are thus reduced to proving:

(6.10)

Let
\dim S=d\geqslant 1
. Assume that
\eqref{Rhom}_{d,k-1}
holds (with
k\geqslant 1
). Then
\eqref{Rhom}_{d,k}
holds.

We can reduce to the case
\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N}
are regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules. Let
Z
denote the
X
-support of
\mathscr{M}
. Since the assertion is local, we can assume that there exists a hypersurface
Y
of
X
such that
Y
contains the singular locus of
Z
and
\dim Z\cap Y<k
. Recall that localization along
Y\times\penalty S
preserves regular holonomicity (cf. Corollary
4.31
).

Lemma 6.11
.

It is enough to prove the assertion (
(6.10)
) for
\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N}
such that
\mathscr{M}=\mathscr{M}(*Y)
and
\mathscr{N}=\mathscr{N}(*Y)
.

Proof.

The assertion for
\mathscr{M}
follows from the property that
R\Gamma_{[Y]}\mathscr{M}
belongs to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{rhol}}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
(Corollary
4.32
) and has
X
-support of dimension
<k
.

For the assertion concerning
\mathscr{N}
, we recall the argument given at the end of the proof of
[
FMFS21
, Th. 3]
. It is enough to prove that (
(6.10)
) holds if
\mathscr{N}=R\Gamma_{[Y]}\mathscr{N}
. We have, by
[
MFS13
, (3)]
,

R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N})\simeq R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\boldsymbol{D}\mathscr{N},\boldsymbol{D}\mathscr{M}).

Since
\mathscr{N}
has
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-coherent cohomology and is supported on
Y\times\penalty S
, we have

R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\boldsymbol{D}\mathscr{N},(\boldsymbol{D}\mathscr{M})(*Y))=0.

Furthermore,
\boldsymbol{D}\mathscr{M}
being regular holonomic, so is
R\Gamma_{[Y]}(\boldsymbol{D}\mathscr{M})
by Corollary
4.32
, as well as
\mathscr{M}^{\prime}:=\boldsymbol{D}R\Gamma_{[Y]}(\boldsymbol{D}\mathscr{M})
. Finally, applying once more
[
MFS13
, (3) & (1)]
, we obtain

R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N})\simeq R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M}^{\prime},\mathscr{N}),

with
\dim\Supp_{X}\mathscr{H}^{j}\mathscr{M}^{\prime}<k
for any
j
, so the latter complex is
S
-
\mathbb{C}
-constructible by
\eqref{Rhom}_{d,k-1}
.
∎

Step 3: Reduction to the case where
\mathscr{M}
is of D-type

We take up the notation of the proof of Proposition
4.27
, so that
f:X^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}X
is a projective morphism inducing a biholomorphism
X^{\prime}\smallsetminus D\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}Z\smallsetminus Z\cap Y
, and we set
\delta=\dim X^{\prime}-\dim X
.

Lemma 6.12
.

Let
\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N}
be regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules such that
\mathscr{M}=\mathscr{M}(*Y)
. Then

Rf_{*}R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X^{\prime}\times\penalty S/S}}({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f^{*}\mathscr{M},{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f^{*}\mathscr{N})\simeq R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N}).

Proof.

There is a natural morphism of adjunction (cf.
[
Kas03
, Th. 4.33(1)]
)

Rf_{*}R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X^{\prime}\times\penalty S/S}}({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f^{*}\mathscr{M},{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f^{*}\mathscr{N})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f^{*}\mathscr{M}[\delta],\mathscr{N}).

We note that
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f^{*}\mathscr{M},{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f^{*}\mathscr{N}
are
f
-good. Therefore, this morphism is a quasi-isomorphism. Furthermore, the natural adjunction morphism
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f^{*}\mathscr{M}[\delta]\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{M}
already used in the proof of Proposition
4.27
is an isomorphism if
\mathscr{M}=\mathscr{M}(*Y)
.
∎

Step 4: The case where
\mathscr{M}
is of D-type along a normal crossing divisor

Recall Definition
4.23
and Proposition
4.26
. From Step 3, we are reduced to proving (
(6.10)
) in the following setting:

(6.13)

\dim S=d
,
\dim X=k
,
\mathscr{M}{}\simeq\widetilde{E}_{L}
is of D-type along a normal crossing divisor
D\subset X
, and
\mathscr{N}=\mathscr{N}(*D)
.

Since the assertion is local, we can assume that
(E_{L},\nabla)=(\widehat{E}_{G|X^{*}\times\penalty S},\widehat{\nabla})
as in (
4.17
) for some
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-coherent module
G
. We denote by
r\geqslant 0
the generic rank of
G
as an
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module. The case
r=0
means that
G
is a torsion
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module.

Let us consider
X,S,D
with
\dim S=d\geqslant 1
,
\dim X=k\geqslant 1
and
D
is a divisor with normal crossings in
X
. We shall argue by induction on
r
. For that purpose we consider, for
r\geqslant 0
, the assertion:

\eqref{Rhom}_{d,k,r}

For any such
X,S,D
, the property
\eqref{Rhom}_{d,k-1}
holds, as well as
\eqref{Rhom}_{d,k}
if
\mathscr{M}
is of D-type with
E_{L}=\widehat{E}_{G|X^{*}\times\penalty S}
and
G
being
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-coherent of generic rank
\leqslant r
, and
\mathscr{N}
being regular holonomic satisfying
\mathscr{N}=\mathscr{N}(*D)
.

Then, proving (
(6.13)
) amounts to proving
\eqref{Rhom}_{d,k,r}
for any
r\geqslant 0
, according to Lemma
6.11
.

Lemma 6.14
.

If
\eqref{Rhom}_{d-1}
holds, then

1.

\eqref{Rhom}_{d,k,0}
holds;

2.

if
r\geqslant 1
and if
\eqref{Rhom}_{d,k,r}
holds for any
G
which is
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-locally free and any
\mathscr{N}=\mathscr{N}(*D)
, then
\eqref{Rhom}_{d,k,r}
holds.

Proof.

The first point follows from Lemma
6.7
. In order to prove the second point, we choose (locally on
S
) a projective modification
\pi:S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}S
as in Proposition
4.16
. Since the cones of the natural morphisms
\mathscr{N}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R\pi_{*}\pi^{*}\mathscr{N}
and
R\pi_{*}L\pi^{*}\mathscr{N}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R\pi_{*}\pi^{*}\mathscr{N}
are S-supported in dimension
<d
, Lemmas
6.7
and
2.16
imply that (
(6.6)
) holds for
\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N}
on
X\times\penalty S
if it holds for
L\pi^{*}\mathscr{M},L\pi^{*}\mathscr{N}
on
X\times\penalty S^{\prime}
. Furthermore,
L^{j}\pi^{*}\mathscr{M},L^{j}\pi^{*}\mathscr{N}
are also
S^{\prime}
-supported in dimension
<d
if
j\neq 0
, so, by the same argument, (
(6.6)
) holds for
\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N}
if it holds
\pi^{*}\mathscr{M}
and
\pi^{*}\mathscr{N}
. Lastly, by Lemma
6.7
, we can replace
\pi^{*}\mathscr{M}
and
\pi^{*}\mathscr{N}
with their quotient
\mathscr{M}^{\prime},\mathscr{N}^{\prime}
by their
S^{\prime}
-torsion subsheaves. Note that we still have
\mathscr{N}^{\prime}=\mathscr{N}^{\prime}(*D)
and
\mathscr{M}^{\prime}
is of D-type with corresponding
E_{L^{\prime}}
isomorphic to
\widehat{E}_{G^{\prime}|X^{*}\times\penalty S^{\prime}}
, where
G^{\prime}
is
p^{\prime-1}\mathscr{O}_{S^{\prime}}
-locally free by the choice of
\pi
. The assumption in (
2
) therefore implies that
(
(6.6)
)
holds for any
G
of generic rank
r
, as wanted.
∎

Step 5: Induction on the generic rank of
\mathscr{M}

We use the same notation as in Step 4 of the proof of Theorem
4.15
.

Lemma 6.15
.

Assume that
\eqref{Rhom}_{d,k-1}
holds. Then
\eqref{Rhom}_{d,k,1}
holds.

Proof.

Assume
\mathscr{M}
is of D-type with
E_{L}=\widehat{E}_{G|X^{*}\times\penalty S}
and
G
being
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-coherent and of generic rank one, and let
\mathscr{N}
be regular holonomic with
\mathscr{N}=\mathscr{N}(*D)
. By a projective modification
we can assume that
G
is
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-locally free of rank one. Then, by Proposition
4.25
(
1
), we have
\mathscr{M}=\widehat{E}_{G}
.

The matrix of the relative connection
\widehat{\nabla}
on
\widehat{E}_{G}
in some local
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-basis of
G
writes
\mathrm{d}_{X\times\penalty S/S}\otimes\id+\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\alpha_{i}(s)\mathrm{d}x_{i}/x_{i}\otimes\id
. Let us denote by
\mathscr{N}x^{-\alpha}
the
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-module
\mathscr{N}(*D)
for which we add to the connection the relative
1
-form
-\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\alpha_{i}(s)\mathrm{d}x_{i}/x_{i}\otimes\id
. Then
\mathscr{N}x^{-\alpha}
is also regular holonomic, according to Corollary
4.35
. Since
\mathscr{M}=\mathscr{M}(*D)
and
\mathscr{N}=\mathscr{N}(*D)
, they also are
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*D)
-modules
and, as recalled before Corollary
4.35
, the functor

({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})x^{\alpha}
is an auto-equivalence of
\mathrm{Mod}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*D))
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N})

\displaystyle=R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*D)}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N})

\displaystyle\simeq R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*D)}(\mathscr{M}x^{-\alpha},\mathscr{N}x^{-\alpha})

\displaystyle=R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M}x^{-\alpha},\mathscr{N}x^{-\alpha}).

Furthermore,
\mathscr{M}x^{-\alpha}\simeq(\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D),\mathrm{d}_{X\times\penalty S/S})
. Since
\DR(\mathscr{N}x^{-\alpha})
is
S
-
\mathbb{C}
-constructible (cf.
[
MFS13
, Th. 3.7]
) and
R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}([\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}(*D)/\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}],\mathscr{N}x^{-\alpha})
also by
\eqref{Rhom}_{d,k-1}
, we conclude that
R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N})
belongs to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
.
∎

Lemma 6.16
.

Let us assume that
\eqref{Rhom}_{d,k,r-1}
holds (
r\geqslant 2
). Then
\eqref{Rhom}_{d,k,r}
holds.

Proof.

Let us assume that
r\geqslant 2
. Together with the assumptions of (
(6.13)
) in Step 4, we can now assume that
G
is
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-locally free of finite rank
r
(Lemma
6.14
) and thus, by Proposition
4.25
(
1
),
\mathscr{M}\simeq(\widehat{E}_{G},\widehat{\nabla})
. We argue in a way similar to that in Step 4 in the proof of Theorem
4.15
(
3
).

If each endomorphism
A_{i}(s)
occurring in the connection matrix of
\nabla
is scalar, i.e., of the form
\alpha_{i}(s)\id
, then Lemma
6.15
allows us to conclude, by obvious reduction to rank one, the proof of
(
(6.6)
)
for
\mathscr{M}=(\widehat{E}_{G},\widehat{\nabla})
and
\mathscr{N}
as above.

We can thus assume that some endomorphism
A_{i}(s)
, say
A_{1}(s)
, is not scalar. We use the notation of Step 4 in the proof of Theorem
4.15
(
3
), in particular (
(4.21)
) and (
(4.22)
). Recall that we consider the composition
S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt\pi\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\pi}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\Sigma\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt\sigma\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sigma}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}S
, with
\Sigma:=\{\det(\alpha_{1}\id-A_{1}(s))=0\}
and
\pi:S^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\Sigma
is a resolution of singularities.

Recall also that we set
G^{\prime}=\ker(\alpha_{1}\id-\mathsf{A}_{1}\circ\sigma\circ\pi)\subset(\sigma\circ\pi)^{*}G
and we consider the exact sequence
0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}G^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\sigma\circ\pi)^{*}G\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}G^{\prime\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0
. By construction and assumption,
we have
1\leqslant\rk G^{\prime}<\rk G
and thus
\rk G^{\prime\prime}<\rk G
.

By applying the induction hypothesis
\eqref{Rhom}_{d,k,r-1}
to
G^{\prime},G^{\prime\prime}
, we deduce that

R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}}(L(\sigma\circ\pi)^{*}\mathscr{M},L(\sigma\circ\pi)^{*}\mathscr{N})

belongs to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
, hence so does its pushforward by
\sigma\circ\pi
. Thus

R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},R(\sigma\circ\pi)_{*}L(\sigma\circ\pi)^{*}\mathscr{N}),

belongs to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
by Lemma
2.16
.

The cones of the natural morphisms

R(\sigma\circ\pi)_{*}L(\sigma\circ\pi)^{*}\mathscr{N}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R(\sigma\circ\pi)_{*}(\sigma\circ\pi)^{*}\mathscr{N}
,

(\sigma\circ\pi)_{*}(\sigma\circ\pi)^{*}\mathscr{N}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R(\sigma\circ\pi)_{*}(\sigma\circ\pi)^{*}\mathscr{N}
and

\sigma_{*}\sigma^{*}\mathscr{N}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\sigma\circ\pi)_{*}(\sigma\circ\pi)^{*}\mathscr{N}

are supported on
X\times\sigma(\mathrm{Sing}(\Sigma))
. Therefore,

R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},(\sigma\circ\pi)_{*}(\sigma\circ\pi)^{*}\mathscr{N})\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}).

Recalling (cf. (
(4.21)
)) that locally
\sigma_{*}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\Sigma}
is
\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}
-free of rank
\deg\sigma
,
\sigma_{*}\sigma^{*}\mathscr{N}
, which is isomorphic to
\sigma_{*}\mathscr{O}_{X\times\Sigma}\otimes_{\mathscr{O}_{X\times\penalty S}}\mathscr{N}
, is regular holonomic. Besides, according to (
(4.22)
),
\mathscr{N}
is a direct summand of
\sigma_{*}\sigma^{*}\mathscr{N}
. Therefore
R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N})
is a direct summand of
R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\sigma_{*}\sigma^{*}\mathscr{N})
, thus again by
\eqref{Rhom}_{d-1}
,
R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\sigma_{*}\sigma^{*}\mathscr{N})
is an object of
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{C}\textup{-c}}}(p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
, hence the same holds true for
R\mathscr{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}(\mathscr{M},\mathscr{N})
.
∎

This lemma concludes Step 5, therefore the proof of
\eqref{Rhom}_{d}
, and thereby that of Theorem
2
.
∎

As a consequence of Theorem
2
we can now generalize Proposition
4.26
:

Theorem 6.17
.

Let
Y
be a hypersurface in
X
. Then the category of
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules of D-type along
Y
is equivalent to the category of
S
-locally constant sheaves on
X^{*}\times\penalty S
with finite rank, under the functor
\mathscr{L}\mathchoice{\longmapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}{\mapsto}L=\mathscr{H}^{0}\DR(\mathscr{L}_{|X^{*}\times\penalty S})
with quasi-inverse
L\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\RH_{X}^{S}(j_{!}\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}L)[-d_{X}]
.

Proof.

Let us prove that the second functor takes values in the category of modules of
D
-type along
Y
. Let
L
be an
S
-locally constant sheaf on
X^{*}\times\penalty S
of finite rank. Since, by
[
FMFS21
, Prop. 2.6]
(valid without any restriction on
\dim S
),
\RH_{X}^{S}(j_{!}\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}L)
is localized along
Y
, and regular holonomic by Theorem
2
, hence
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}(*Y)
-coherent, it remains to prove that
\RH_{X}^{S}(j_{!}\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}L)[-d_{X}]
is in degree zero and that
\RH_{X}^{S}(j_{!}\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}L)[-d_{X}]|_{X^{*}\times S}\simeq\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times S}\otimes_{p_{X^{*}}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}L
. The first assertion follows by the usual Nakayama-type argument.
The second assertion follows from

[
MFS19
, Cor. 3.24]
(valid without any restriction on
\dim S
), which shows that
\RH_{X^{*}}^{S}(\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}L)[-d_{X}]\simeq\mathscr{O}_{X^{*}\times S}\otimes_{p^{-1}_{X^{*}}\mathscr{O}_{S}}L
.

Let
\mathscr{L}
be a
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-module of
D
-type along
Y
with associated
S
-local system
L
, that is
\DR(\mathscr{L})|_{X^{*}\times S}\simeq L
; we have a natural morphism
\DR(\mathscr{L})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}j_{*}L
.
By Corollary
4.33
,
\mathscr{L}
is regular holonomic.
According to Lemma
5.16
,
\DR(\RH^{S}_{X}(j_{!}\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}L)[-d_{X}])\simeq j_{*}L
thus, by Theorem
2
, there exists a natural morphism of localized regular holonomic
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}
-modules
\mathscr{L}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\RH_{X}^{S}(j_{!}\boldsymbol{D}^{\prime}L)[-d_{X}]
, which has to be an isomorphism since that is so on
X^{*}\times S
.
∎

Appendix: Complements

The aim of this appendix is twofold:

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

to prove Proposition
5.4
, correcting thus the statement of
[
MFS19
, Prop. 3.5]
(namely, contrary to loc. cit., the resolution in
5.4
may be not bounded below),

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

and to correct and complete the contents of
[
FMFS21
, §1.2.2]
.

Taking into account these corrections, the main results in both articles remain valid.

Furthermore, for the sake of completeness, we will give a detailed proof of Lemma
5.14
, by following the same strategy as in
[
KS96
, Th. 4.4]
.

In the following, we use the notation
\mathscr{O}_{V}
for
\mathbb{C}_{V}\otimes\mathscr{O}_{S}
, for any open set
V\subset S
. Recall that, for
\Omega,\Omega^{\prime}
open in
X
and
V
,
V^{\prime}
open in
S
, for any
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module
G
, we have:

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

\Hom_{p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(\mathbb{C}_{\Omega}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V},\mathbb{C}_{\Omega^{\prime}\times V^{\prime}}\otimes p_{X}^{-1}G)\simeq\Gamma(\Omega\times V;p_{X}^{-1}G)
if
\Omega\subset\Omega^{\prime}
and
V\subset V^{\prime}
.

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

If
\Omega\times V\cap\overline{\Omega^{\prime}\times V^{\prime}}=\varnothing
, then
\Hom_{p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(\mathbb{C}_{\Omega}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V},\mathbb{C}_{\Omega^{\prime}\times V^{\prime}}\otimes p_{X}^{-1}G)=0
.

We consider the family
\mathscr{S}=\mathscr{S}_{X}\times\mathscr{S}_{S}
of open sets
W=U\times V
of
X\times\penalty S
, where

a.

U
is connected open subanalytic relatively compact in
X
.

b.

V
is open and relatively compact in
S
.

This family satisfies the conditions of
[
KS96
, (A.7) & (A.8)]
, since each point of
X\times\penalty S
has a fundamental system of neighborhoods consisting of open sets in
\mathscr{S}
, and the intersection of two open sets of
\mathscr{S}
is a finite union of elements in
\mathscr{S}
.

Notice that for any
W_{1}=U_{1}\times V_{1},W_{2}=U_{2}\times V_{2}\in\mathscr{S}
we have

\Gamma(W_{1};\mathbb{C}_{W_{2}}\otimes p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})=\begin{cases}\Gamma(W_{1};p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{V_{2}})=\Gamma(V_{1};\mathscr{O}_{V_{2}})&\text{if }U_{1}\subset U_{2},\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(A.1
\,*
)

Definition A.2
.

In a way similar to
[
KS96
, §A.2]
, we define:

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

{\mathbf{A}}_{X}=\mathrm{Mod}_{\textup{w-}\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
;

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

{\mathbf{P}}_{X}
the category whose objects
\mathfrak{W}=(W_{i}=U_{i}\times V_{i})_{i\in I}

consist of locally finite families
of open subsets of
\mathscr{S}
,
and whose morphisms are described in a way similar to that of
[
KS96
, §A.2]
:

\Hom_{{\mathbf{P}}_{X}}(\mathfrak{W},\mathfrak{W}^{\prime})=\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i\in I}\Bigl(\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{j\in J\mid W^{\prime}_{j}\supset W_{i}}\Gamma(U_{i}\times\overline{V}_{i};p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})\Bigr),

where
\mathfrak{W}^{\prime}=(W^{\prime}_{j}=U^{\prime}_{j}\times V^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in J}
.

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

In the following we will write simply
{\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{P}}
in order to keep the notation clean.
The additive functor
L:{\mathbf{P}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\mathbf{A}}
given by

L(\mathfrak{W})=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{i\in I}\mathbb{C}_{U_{i}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{O}}_{V_{i}}=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{i\in I}(\mathbb{C}_{W_{i}}\otimes p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}),

and, for
\mathfrak{W},\,\mathfrak{W}^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{P}}
, for
\varphi\in\Hom_{{\mathbf{P}}}(\mathfrak{W},\mathfrak{W}^{\prime})
,
L(\varphi)
is given by the natural morphism

\Gamma(U_{i}\times\overline{V_{i}};p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\Gamma(U_{i}\times V_{i};\mathbb{C}_{U_{j}}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V_{j}})=\Gamma(W_{i};p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})

for each
i\in I,\,j\in J
such that
W_{i}\subset W_{j}
. We remark that
L
is a faithfull functor.

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

The additive bifunctor
H:{\mathbf{P}}^{\mathrm{op}}\times{\mathbf{A}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\mathbf{Ab}}
given, for
\mathfrak{W}\in{\mathbf{P}}
and
F\in{\mathbf{A}}
, by

H(\mathfrak{W},F)=\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i\in I}\Gamma(U_{i}\times\overline{V_{i}};F),

where, following the notations of
[
KS96
, App. A.1]
,
{\mathbf{Ab}}
denotes the category of abelian groups.

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

\alpha_{\mathfrak{W},F}:H(\mathfrak{W},F)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\Hom_{p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(L(\mathfrak{W}),F)
given by the restrictions

\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i\in I}\Gamma(U_{i}\times\overline{V_{i}};F)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathop{\textstyle\prod}\displaylimits_{i\in I}\Gamma(U_{i}\times V_{i};F)=\Hom_{p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}}(\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{i\in I}\mathbb{C}_{U_{i}}\boxtimes{\mathscr{O}}_{V_{i}};F).

For a morphism
u
in
{\mathbf{P}}
let us denote by
\ker u
(
resp.

\Ima u
) the kernel (
resp.
the image) of
L(u)
as a morphism in
{\mathbf{A}}
.

An object
F
of
{\mathbf{A}}
is called
{\mathbf{P}}
-coherent (
[
KS96
, App. A.1]
) if there exist
\mathfrak{W}\in{\mathbf{P}}
and an epimorphism
L(\mathfrak{W})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}F
in
{\mathbf{A}}
and if, for any
f\in H(\mathfrak{W}^{\prime},F)
, there exist
\mathfrak{W}^{\prime\prime}
in
{\mathbf{P}}
and a morphism
g:\mathfrak{W}^{\prime\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathfrak{W}^{\prime}
such that
\mathfrak{W}^{\prime\prime}\overset{g}{\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}\mathfrak{W}^{\prime}\overset{f}{\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}F
is exact; that is

L(\mathfrak{W}^{\prime\prime})\overset{L(g)}{\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}L(\mathfrak{W}^{\prime})\overset{\alpha(f)}{\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}}F
is exact in
{\mathbf{A}}
(and hence
f\circ g=0
since
L
is faithfull).

Proposition A.3
.

1.

The objects
({\mathbf{A}},{\mathbf{P}},L,H,\alpha)
as defined above satisfy Properties
(A.1)–(A.4)
of
[
KS96
, §A.1]
.

2.

An object of
{\mathbf{A}}
is
{\mathbf{P}}
-coherent if and only if it is
S
-
\mathbb{R}
-constructible.

The proof of
A.3
(
1
), which concerns the ring
p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
, essentially reduces that of
[
KS96
, Prop. A.6]
, which concerns the ring
\mathscr{O}_{S}
. However, the new ingredient with respect to
[
KS96
]
is the property that any
S
-weakly
\mathbb{R}
-constructible sheaf
F
admits an epimorphism
u:\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{k}(\mathbb{C}_{U_{k}}\boxtimes G_{k})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}F
for some locally finite family
(U_{k})
in
\mathscr{S}_{X}
and some
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-modules
G_{k}
, which can be assumed to be
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-coherent if
F
is
S
-
\mathbb{R}
-constructible. Details are given in the arXiv version of this paper.

Corollary A.4
.

The category of
{\mathbf{P}}
-coherent objects is closed under kernels, cokernels and extensions in
{\mathbf{A}}
.

Proof.

This is
[
KS96
, Prop. A.1]
, that we can apply according to Proposition
A.3
.
∎

As in
[
KS96
, App.]
, we denote by
\rD^{-}({\mathbf{P}})
(resp.
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}({\mathbf{P}})
) the triangulated category obtained by taking the quotient of
\rK^{-}({\mathbf{P}})
(resp.
\rK^{\mathrm{b}}({\mathbf{P}})
) with respect to the null system of complexes
\mathfrak{W}^{{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}
in
{\mathbf{P}}
such that
L(\mathfrak{W}^{{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}})
is acyclic in
{\mathbf{A}}
. We denote by
\rD^{-}_{\mathrm{coh}}({\mathbf{P}})
the full subcategory of
\rD^{-}({\mathbf{P}})
of objects
\mathfrak{W}^{{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}
such that
L(\mathfrak{W}^{{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}})
has
{\mathbf{P}}
-coherent cohomologies.

On the other hand, we denote by
\rD^{-}_{\mathrm{coh}}({\mathbf{A}})
(
resp.

\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}({\mathbf{A}})
) the full subcategory of
\rD^{-}({\mathbf{A}})

(
resp.

\rD^{\mathrm{b}}({\mathbf{A}})
) whose objects have
{\mathbf{P}}
-coherent cohomologies, i.e., according to Proposition
A.3
(
2
), are
S
-
\mathbb{R}
-constructible.
We also denote by
\rD^{-}_{\mathrm{b},\mathrm{coh}}({\mathbf{P}})
the full subcategory of
\rD^{-}({\mathbf{P}})
of objects
\mathfrak{W}^{{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}
such that
L(\mathfrak{W}^{{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}})\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}({\mathbf{A}})
.

In this context,
[
KS90
, Prop. 8.1.4]
holds with a similar proof and provides functorial isomorphisms, for any
F
in
{\mathbf{A}}
, any
\sigma\in\Delta
and any
x\in|\sigma|
:

G_{\sigma}(F):=p_{U(\sigma)\ast}(F|_{U(\sigma)\times S})\simeq F|_{\{x\}\times S},\quad R^{k}p_{U(\sigma)*}(F|_{U(\sigma)\times S})=0\quad\forall\,k\geqslant 1.

(A.5)

The proofs of
[
KS90
, Th. 8.1.10 & 8.4.5(i)]
can then be adapted to the relative setting
by replacing everywhere the functor
\Gamma(U(\sigma),{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})
with
p_{U(\sigma)*}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}|_{U(\sigma)})
,
yielding that the natural inclusion functor
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}({\mathbf{A}})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\textup{w-}\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
is an equivalence by providing an explicit quasi-inverse denoted there by
R\beta
. We deduce an equivalence

\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}({\mathbf{A}})=\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}(\mathrm{Mod}_{\textup{w-}\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}))\simeq\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}).

The following proposition, which is a consequence of
[
KS96
, Th. A.5]
and the properties explained above, corrects
[
FMFS21
, Prop. 1.6]
.

Proposition A.6
.

The natural functor

L:\rD^{-}_{\mathrm{coh}}({{\mathbf{P}}})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rD^{-}_{\mathrm{coh}}({\mathbf{A}})

is an equivalence of categories which induces an equivalence of categories (still denoted by
L
for short)

L:\rD^{-}_{\mathrm{b},\mathrm{coh}}({\mathbf{P}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}).

Proof of Proposition
5.4
.

We identify
\rD^{-}_{\mathrm{b},\mathrm{coh}}({\mathbf{P}})
with its essential image in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathrm{coh}}({\mathbf{A}})
, which is thus equivalent to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
, according to Proposition
A.6
. By definition, the objects
K^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}
in this essential image satisfy the property described in Proposition
5.4
.
∎

Remark A.7
.

In (
5.13
) we defined the natural transformation

\mu:{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\THH_{X}^{S}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\THH_{Y}^{S}(Rf_{*}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))

between functors from
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S/S})
.
Due to Proposition
A.6
we get (by composing with
L
) a natural transformation

\mu\circ L:{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\THH_{X}^{S}(L({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\THH_{Y}^{S}(Rf_{*}(L({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})))

between functors from
\rD^{-}_{\mathrm{b},\mathrm{coh}}({\mathbf{P}})
to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S/S})
. We now extend it, by relaxing the coherency condition, to a natural transformation between functors from
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}({\mathbf{P}})
to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S/S})
. Recall that we denote by
f
both maps
X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y
and
X\times\penalty S\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y\times\penalty S
. Let
W=U\times V\in\mathscr{S}
and consider the family
\{W\}
. By adjunction we have

f^{-1}Rf_{*}(\mathbb{C}_{W})\simeq f^{-1}Rf_{*}(\mathbb{C}_{U})\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{V},

and thus

f^{-1}Rf_{*}(\mathbb{C}_{W}\otimes p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})\simeq(f^{-1}Rf_{*}(\mathbb{C}_{U})\boxtimes\mathbb{C}_{V})\otimes p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}.

Since
f^{-1}Rf_{*}(\mathbb{C}_{U})
is a bounded complex with
\mathbb{R}
-constructible cohomologies, it is quasi-isomorphic to a bounded complex whose entries are locally finite sums
\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{i\in I}\mathbb{C}_{U_{i}}
where each
U_{i}\in\mathscr{S}_{X}
(cf.
[
DGS11
, Prop. A.1]
). Therefore the complex
f^{-1}Rf_{*}L(\{W\})
belongs to the essential image by
L
of a complex in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}({\mathbf{P}})
. The same property then holds for any
\mathfrak{W}\in{\mathbf{P}}
instead of
\{W\}
and then for any bounded complex
\mathfrak{W}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}({\mathbf{P}})
.

It is then meaningful to define
\mu_{L(\mathfrak{W}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})}:{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\THH_{X}^{S}(L(\mathfrak{W}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\THH_{Y}^{S}(Rf_{*}(L(\mathfrak{W}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))
as the composition of

{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\THH_{X}^{S}(L(\mathfrak{W}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\THH_{X}^{S}(f^{-1}Rf_{*}L(\mathfrak{W}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))

and

\eta_{Rf_{*}L(\mathfrak{W}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})}:={}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\THH_{X}^{S}(f^{-1}Rf_{*}L(\mathfrak{W}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\THH_{Y}^{S}(Rf_{*}L(\mathfrak{W}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})),

since all complexes on which
\THH^{S}
is applied are bounded complexes.

Proof of Lemma
5.14
.

The morphism
\mu_{F}
being defined, checking that it is an isomorphism is a local question on
Y\times S
.
If
F\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
has
non zero cohomologies only in the interval
[a,b]
,
the complexes
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\THH_{X}^{S}(F)
and
\THH_{Y}^{S}(Rf_{*}F)
can have non zero cohomologies only
for indices belonging to a finite interval
I=[-b-m,-a+n]
for suitable
m,n\in\mathbb{N}
only depending on
\dim X
and
\dim Y
.
Thus it is enough to show that
\mathscr{H}^{j}(\mu_{F})
is an isomorphism for any
j\in I
.
In view of Proposition
5.4
, we can replace
F
with a bounded
complex
K^{\prime{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}=0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}K^{-N}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}K^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}
with
N>-a+m+n
such that

K^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}=L(\mathfrak{W}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})
for some
\mathfrak{W}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\in\rK^{\mathrm{b}}({\mathbf{P}})
.
Thus we obtain a distinguished triangle
K^{-N}[N]\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}L(\mathfrak{W}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt\tau\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\tau}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}F\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt+1\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}
, and both

\mathscr{H}^{j}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\THH_{X}^{S}(K^{-N}[N])
and
\mathscr{H}^{j}\THH_{Y}^{S}(K^{-N}[N])
vanish for
j\in I
. Hence, for any

j\in I
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{j}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\THH_{X}^{S}(F)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathscr{H}^{j}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\THH_{X}^{S}(L(\mathfrak{W}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})),

\displaystyle\mathscr{H}^{j}\THH_{Y}^{S}(Rf_{*}F)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathscr{H}^{j}\THH_{Y}^{S}(Rf_{*}L(\mathfrak{W}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})),

hence we are reduced to prove
that, for any
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, the morphism

\mathscr{H}^{j}(\mu_{L(\mathfrak{W}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})}):\mathscr{H}^{j}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\THH_{X}^{S}(L(\mathfrak{W}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathscr{H}^{j}\THH_{Y}^{S}(Rf_{*}L(\mathfrak{W}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))

(defined in Remark
A.7
) is an isomorphism.

Let
(y,s)\in Y\times S
. Let
\Omega_{Y}
be a relatively compact subanalytic neighbourhood of
y
. By the assumption on
f
we can find an open relatively compact subanalytic neighbourhood
\Omega_{X}
of
f^{-1}\Omega_{Y}\cap\Supp_{X}F
.
By restricting to
\Omega_{X}\times S
, we can reduce to study the case of
\mathbb{C}_{W}\otimes p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
, for some
W=U\times V\in\mathscr{S}
.
By the assumption on
f
, we have an isomorphism of functors

R\Gamma_{Y\times V}(Rf_{*}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\simeq Rf_{*}(R\Gamma_{X\times V}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})).

Thus we have isomorphisms

Rf_{*}\THH_{X}^{S}(\mathbb{C}_{U}\boxtimes(\mathbb{C}_{V}\otimes\mathscr{O}_{S}))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{R}f_{*}R\Gamma_{X\times V}\THH_{X}^{S}(\mathbb{C}_{U}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}R\Gamma_{Y\times V}Rf_{*}\THH_{X}^{S}(\mathbb{C}_{U}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{S}).

Similarly

\THH_{Y}^{S}(Rf_{*}(\mathbb{C}_{U}\boxtimes(\mathbb{C}_{V}\otimes\mathscr{O}_{S})))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\THH_{Y}^{S}(Rf_{*}(\mathbb{C}_{U})\boxtimes(\mathbb{C}_{V}\otimes\mathscr{O}_{S})))\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbb{R}\Gamma_{Y\times V}\THH_{Y}^{S}(Rf_{*}(\mathbb{C}_{U})\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{S}).

Therefore we are led to prove the statement for
F=\mathbb{C}_{U}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{S}
.

We will now follow the proof of
[
KS96
, Th. 4.4]
which contains the statement in the absolute case.
We decompose
f
by the graph embedding so that we first assume that
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y
is a closed embedding and next we treat the case of a smooth morphism.

Step 1

Let us assume that
f:X\!\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\!Y
is the embedding of a closed manifold. As in the proof of
[
FMFS21
, Prop. 2.4]
, let us start by proving the statement for
F^{\prime}=\mathbb{C}_{Z\times S}\otimes p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
, with
Z=X\smallsetminus U
. The conclusion for
U
will easily follow by functoriality, by considering the exact sequence

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{C}_{U\times S}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{C}_{X\times\penalty S}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{C}_{Z\times S}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0.

We have
\THH^{S}_{X}(F^{\prime})\simeq\Gamma_{Z\times S}(\Db_{X\times\penalty S})
regarded as a
\mathscr{D}_{Y_{\mathbb{R}}\times S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}
-module. We note that the local structure of distributions supported by a submanifold entail that

\Gamma_{X\times\penalty S}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S})\simeq f_{*}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\leftarrow X/S}\otimes_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}\Db_{X\times\penalty S}).

Then

\displaystyle{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{*}\Gamma_{Z\times S}(\Db_{X\times\penalty S})

\displaystyle\simeq f_{*}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\leftarrow X/S}\otimes_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S/S}}\Gamma_{Z\times S}(\Db_{X\times\penalty S}))

\displaystyle\simeq\Gamma_{Z\times S}(\Db_{Y\times\penalty S})\simeq\tho(f_{*}\mathbb{C}_{Z\times X},\Db_{Y\times\penalty S})\simeq\THH^{S}_{Y}(f_{*}(\mathbb{C}_{Z\times S}\otimes p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})).

Step 2

Let us now assume that
f
is smooth and
f\times\id_{S}
is proper on the support of
F
.
The question being local we may assume as in (ii) of the proof of
[
KS96
, Th. 3.5]
that
f:X=Y\times\mathbb{R}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\penalty\mathbb{R}
is the projection.
Recall that
F\simeq\mathbb{C}_{U\times S}\otimes p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
. Let
\overline{U}=Z
. Then
Z
is closed subanalytic in
X
and the assumption entails that
f|_{Z}
is proper.

As in Step 1, we prove first the statement for
\mathbb{C}_{Z^{\prime}\times S}\otimes p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
where
Z^{\prime}
is an arbitrary closed analytic subset of
X
such that
f|_{Z^{\prime}}
is proper. By (3.9) of Lemma 3.6 in loc. cit. we may assume that for any
x\in Y
,
Z^{\prime}\cap f^{-1}(x)
is a closed interval in
\mathbb{R}
containing
0
. With this assumption we have

\tho(Rf_{*}\mathbb{C}_{Z^{\prime}\times S},\Db_{Y\times\penalty S})\simeq\tho(\mathbb{C}_{f(Z^{\prime})\times S},\Db_{Y\times\penalty S})\simeq\Gamma_{f(Z^{\prime})\times S}(\Db_{Y\times\penalty S}).

Noting that

\mathscr{D}_{Y\leftarrow X/S}
is
\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S/S}
-isomorphic to
\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S/S}/\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S/S}\partial_{t}
, where
t
is the coordinate in
\mathbb{R}
, the result follows by the exact sequence

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}f_{*}\Gamma_{Z^{\prime}\times S}(\Db_{X\times\penalty S})\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt\partial_{t}\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\partial_{t}}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}f_{*}\Gamma_{Z^{\prime}\times S}(\Db_{X\times\penalty S})\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt\int{}\cdot\mathrm{d}t\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\int{}\cdot\mathrm{d}t}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\Gamma_{f(Z^{\prime})\times S}(\Db_{Y\times\penalty S})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0

proved in
[
Kas84
, Lem. 4.5]
. The isomorphism for
U
follows by functoriality considering the exact sequence

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{C}_{U\times S}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{C}_{Z\times S}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{C}_{\delta(U)\times S}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0.

∎

Remark A.8
.

In order to prove that the morphism

\tau_{F}:L\pi^{*}R\rho_{S*}(F)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}R\rho_{S^{\prime}*}L\pi^{*}(F)

of Lemma
5.7
is an isomorphism
for any
F\in\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
, we may use the same argument of Lemma
5.14

to reduce to the case
F\!=\!\mathbb{C}_{U\times V}\!\otimes\!p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S}
.

Added to the final arXiv version

In this addition, written for the convenience of the reader, we explain the proofs of Proposition
A.3
and of Theorem
5.10
, and we justify the property used for the proof of Lemma
5.14
, namely that the morphism
\mu_{f}
in this lemma is compatible with composition of morphisms (we used it for the composition of a graph embedding and a projection).

We will make use of the next lemma.

Lemma A.9
.

Let
F
be an
S
-weakly
\mathbb{R}
-constructible sheaf on
X\times\penalty S
. Then there exist

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

a locally finite covering
(U(\sigma))_{\sigma\in\Delta}
of
X
by elements of
\mathscr{S}_{X}
,

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

for each
\sigma\in\Delta
an
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module
G_{\sigma}(F)
on
S
,

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

and an epimorphism
\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{\sigma\in\Delta}\mathbb{C}_{U(\sigma)}\boxtimes G_{\sigma}(F)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}F
.

If
F
is
S
-
\mathbb{R}
-constructible, then all
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-modules
G_{\sigma}(F)
can be chosen coherent.

Proof.

We will use the notations of
[
KS90
, §8.1]
. The subanalytic triangulation theorem (
[
KS90
, Th. 8.2.5]
) allows us to consider a simplicial complex
{\mathbf{T}}=(T,\Delta)
on
X
with respect to which
F
is

S
-weakly
\mathbb{R}
-constructible. Recall (cf. (
A.5
)) that, for any
\sigma\in\Delta
and any
x\in|\sigma|
, the natural morphism
G_{\sigma}(F)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}F|_{\{x\}\times S}
is an isomorphism and that
F|_{\{x\}\times S}
is a coherent
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module, as explained in
[
MFS13
, §2.2]
. By adjunction, there exists a canonical morphism

p_{U(\sigma)}^{-1}G_{\sigma}(F)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}F_{|U(\sigma)\times S},

and by extension a canonical morphism

\mathbb{C}_{U(\sigma)\times S}\otimes p_{U(\sigma)}^{-1}G_{\sigma}(F)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}F_{|U(\sigma)\times S},

thus a canonical morphism

u_{G_{\sigma}(F)}:\mathbb{C}_{U(\sigma)\times S}\otimes p^{-1}G_{\sigma}(F)\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}F.

For any
\sigma
, for any
x\in|\sigma|
, and any
s\in S
, the germ

u_{G_{\sigma}(F),\,(x,s)}:(\mathbb{C}_{U(\sigma)}\boxtimes G_{\sigma}(F))_{(x,s)}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}F_{(x,s)}

is an isomorphism.
We now define
K:=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{\sigma\in\Delta}\mathbb{C}_{U(\sigma)}\boxtimes G_{\sigma}(F)
. Then

u:=\sum_{\sigma\in\Delta}u_{G_{\sigma(F)}}:K\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}F

(A.10)

is an epimorphism.
∎

Proof of Proposition
A.3
(
1
)

We explain how to reduce to the arguments given for the proof of
[
KS96
, Prop. A.6]
, noting that the proof of Property (A.1) is obvious.

Proof of
(A.2)
in
[
KS96
]
.

Given a morphism
\psi:\mathfrak{W}=(W_{i})_{i\in I}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathfrak{W}^{\prime}=(W^{\prime}_{j})_{j\in J}
in
{\mathbf{P}}
, (A.2) asserts the existence of a morphism
\varphi:\mathfrak{W}^{\prime\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathfrak{W}
in
{\mathbf{P}}
such that
\Ima L(\varphi)=\ker L(\psi)
(which implies
\psi\circ\varphi=0
since
L
is faithful).

Recall Definition
A.2
. The morphism
\psi
is given by a family
(\psi_{i,j})_{i\in I,j\in J}
with
\psi_{i,j}\in\Gamma(U_{i}\times\overline{V_{i}};p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
. Since
U_{i}
is connected,
[
MFS19
, Prop. A.1(2)]
) implies that
\Gamma(U_{i}\times\penalty\overline{V_{i}};p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})=\Gamma(\overline{V_{i}};\mathscr{O}_{S})
and each
\psi_{i,j}
corresponds to some
g_{i,j}\in\Gamma(\overline{V_{i}};\mathscr{O}_{S})
. As a consequence, for any
(x,s)\in X\times\penalty S
, there exists a neighborhood
W(x,s)\in\mathscr{S}
of
(x,s)
such that each
\psi_{i,j}
is the restriction to
W(x,s)\cap(U_{i}\cap\overline{V}_{i})
of an element
\widetilde{\psi}_{i,j}\in\Gamma(W(x,s);p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
.

From this point, the proof is identical to that of
[
KS96
, p. 66–67]
.∎

Proof of
(A.3)
in
[
KS96
]
.

Property (A.3) asserts that, given an epimorphism
u:F^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}F
in
\mathrm{Mod}_{\textup{w-}\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
,
\mathfrak{W}=(W_{i})_{i\in I}
in
{\mathbf{P}}
and
\varphi\in H(\mathfrak{W},F)
, there exists
\mathfrak{W}^{\prime}
in
{\mathbf{P}}
, a cover
g:\mathfrak{W}^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathfrak{W}
(i.e.,
L(g)
is an epimorphism) and a morphism
\psi:\mathfrak{W}^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}F^{\prime}
, all data being such that the following diagram is commutative:

\textstyle{\mathfrak{W}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{g}
\textstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{W}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\varphi}
\textstyle{F^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{u}
\textstyle{F}

Let us set
\varphi=(\varphi_{i})_{i\in I}
,
\varphi_{i}\in\Gamma(U_{i}\times\overline{V}_{i};F)
. We claim that, for each
(x,s)\in X\times\penalty S
, there exists an open neighborhood
W(x,s)=U_{x}\times V_{s}\in\mathscr{S}
of
(x,s)
such that, for any
i\in I
,
U_{x}\cap U_{i}
is the finite union of open sets
U_{x,i,j}\in\mathscr{S}_{X}
, each of which satisfying the following property, where we set
W_{i,j}(x,s)=U_{x,i,j}\times V_{s}
:

\varphi_{i}|_{W_{i,j}(x,s)\cap(U_{i}\times\overline{V}_{i})}=u(\varphi^{\prime}_{i,j}(x,s))\\
\text{for some }\varphi^{\prime}_{i,j}(x,s)\in\Gamma(W_{i,j}(x,s)\cap(U_{i}\times\overline{V}_{i}),F^{\prime}).

(A.11)

Indeed, for any
(x,s)\in X\times\penalty S
, let us choose
W=U\times V\in\mathscr{S}
such that
(x,s)\in W
. Then the set
I_{W}=\{i\in I\mid W_{i}\cap W\neq\varnothing\}
is finite. As in Lemma
A.9
, we consider a simplicial complex
{\mathbf{T}}=(T,\Delta)
on
X
, compatible with
U
and the finite families
(U\cap U_{i})_{i\in I_{W}}
, and with respect to which both
F
and
F^{\prime}
are
S
-weakly
\mathbb{R}
-constructible. Let
i\in I
.

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

We first note that (
A.11
) is empty for
i\notin I_{W}
.

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

If
(x,s)\!\notin\!\overline{W}_{i}
, we can find a neighborhood
W_{i}(x,s)\!\in\!\mathscr{S}
such that
W_{i}\cap W_{i}(x,s)\!=\!\varnothing
and (
A.11
) for such an
i
and
W_{i}(x,s)
is empty.

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

If
(x,s)\in\overline{W}_{i}
, let
\sigma\in\Delta
be such that
x\in U(\sigma)
. Then
U(\sigma)\cap U_{i}
is a union of some open sets of the form
U(\tau_{j})
. Then, for each such
\tau_{j}
,
\varphi_{i}|_{U(\tau_{j})\times\overline{V}_{i}}
corresponds to
\varphi_{i}|_{\{x^{\prime}\}\times\overline{V}_{i}}
for some
x^{\prime}\in|\tau_{j}|
, by means of (
A.5
).

There exists an open neighborhood
V_{i,s}\subset V
of
s
such that
\varphi_{i}|_{\{x^{\prime}\}\times\overline{V}_{i}}
extends to a section
\widetilde{\varphi}_{i}|_{\{x^{\prime}\}\times V_{i,s}}
and that

\widetilde{\varphi}_{i}|_{\{x^{\prime}\}\times V_{i,s}}=u|_{\{x^{\prime}\}\times V_{i,s}}(\varphi^{\prime}_{i,j}(x^{\prime},s))
for some
\varphi^{\prime}_{i,j}(x^{\prime},s)\in\Gamma(\{x^{\prime}\}\times V_{i,s};F^{\prime})
. Thus
\varphi^{\prime}_{i,j}(x^{\prime},s)
uniquely defines
\varphi^{\prime}_{i,j}(x,s)\in\Gamma(U(\tau_{j})\times V_{i,s};F^{\prime})
such that
u(\varphi^{\prime}_{i,j}(x,s))=\widetilde{\varphi}_{i}|_{U(\tau_{j})\times V_{i,s}}
by using (
A.5
) once more. The finiteness of the set of indices
j
allows us to choose
V_{i,s}
independent of
j
.

We set

U_{x}=U(\sigma)
,

V_{s}=\mathop{\textstyle\bigcap}\displaylimits_{i\in I_{W}}V_{i,s}
and

W_{i,j}(x,s)=U(\tau_{j})\times V_{i,s}
.
Then
(
A.11
) is satisfied for such choices.

From this point, the proof is identical to that in
[
KS96
, p. 67]
.∎

Proof of
(A.4)
in
[
KS96
]
.

Assume we are given
\mathfrak{W}=(W_{i})_{i\in I},\widetilde{\mathfrak{W}}=(\widetilde{W}_{j})_{j\in J}
in
{\mathbf{P}}
and
\eta\in H(\mathfrak{W},L(\widetilde{\mathfrak{W}}))
. Property (A.4) amounts to finding
\mathfrak{W}^{\prime}
in
{\mathbf{P}}
, together with a cover
\psi:\mathfrak{W}^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathfrak{W}
in
\Hom_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathfrak{W}^{\prime},\mathfrak{W})
(i.e.,
L(\varphi)
is an epimorphism) and a morphism
\varphi\in\Hom_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathfrak{W}^{\prime},\widetilde{\mathfrak{W}})
such that
L(\varphi)=\alpha(\eta\circ\psi)
in
\Hom(L(\mathfrak{W}^{\prime}),L(\widetilde{\mathfrak{W}}))
. By definition,
\eta=(\eta_{i,j})
with
\eta_{i,j}\in\Gamma(U_{i}\times\overline{V}_{i},\mathbb{C}_{\widetilde{W}_{j}}\otimes p^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
.

According to (
A.1

∗

), we can regard
\eta_{i,j}
as an element of
\Gamma(\overline{V}_{i};\mathscr{O}_{\widetilde{V}_{j}})
, which is zero if
U_{i}\not\subset\widetilde{U}_{j}
. In particular, the
S
-support of
\eta_{i,j}
(i.e., the projection to
S
of its support) is a compact subset of
\widetilde{V}_{j}
. The argument of
[
KS96
, p. 67–68]
yields open subsets
V_{i,j,n}
and
V^{\prime}_{i,j,n}
of
V_{i}
(in our present notation), and we argue with
W_{i,j,n}:=U_{i}\times V_{i,j,n}
and
W^{\prime}_{i,j,n}:=U_{i}\times V^{\prime}_{i,j,n}
exactly as in loc. cit. to conclude the proof that Property (A.4) holds true.∎
