Proof of Proposition
A.3
(
2
)

The proof will be achieved with Lemmas
A.13
and
A.14
, and relies on the following lemma.

Lemma A.12
.

Let
(U_{j})_{j\in J}
be a locally finite family of open subsets of
X
belonging to
\mathscr{S}_{X}
, and, for each
j\in J
, let
G_{j}
be a coherent
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-module. Then
K=\mathop{\textstyle\bigoplus}\displaylimits_{j\in J}(\mathbb{C}_{U_{j}}\boxtimes G_{j})
is
{\mathbf{P}}
-coherent.

Proof.

Proving
{\mathbf{P}}
-coherency of
K
amounts to showing

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

[
KS96
, (A.5)]

the existence of a cover
\psi:\mathfrak{W}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}K
, i.e., an element of
H(\mathfrak{W},K)
such that
\alpha_{\mathfrak{W},K}(\psi)\in\Hom(L(\mathfrak{W}),K)
is an epimorphism,

\scriptscriptstyle\bullet

[
KS96
, (A.6)]

and for each morphism
\psi:\mathfrak{W}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}K
, the existence of
\mathfrak{W}^{\prime}
and
\varphi\in\Hom_{{\mathbf{P}}}(\mathfrak{W}^{\prime},\mathfrak{W})
such that
\mathfrak{W}^{\prime}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt\varphi\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varphi}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\mathfrak{W}\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt\psi\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\psi}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}K
is exact.

For the first condition, we recall that each
G_{j}\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathscr{O}_{S})
has a cover
\mathfrak{V}_{j}
(
[
KS96
, Prop. A.8]
) and, since
(U_{j})_{j\in J}
is locally finite on
X
, the family
\mathfrak{W}=(U_{j}\times\mathfrak{V}_{j})_{j\in J}
is locally finite on
X\times\penalty S
and defines a cover of
K
.

For the second condition, the proof is the same as that of Property (A.2), if we replace in that proof
\psi\in\Hom_{\mathbf{P}}(\mathfrak{W},\mathfrak{W}^{\prime})
with
\psi\in H(\mathfrak{W},K)
. The arguments are then completely parallel.
∎

Lemma A.13
.

Let
F
be an
S
-
\mathbb{R}
-constructible sheaf on
X\times\penalty S
. Then
F
is
{\mathbf{P}}
-coherent.

Proof.

We consider the epimorphism (
A.10
) and we note that
K
is
{\mathbf{P}}
-coherent, by Lemma
A.12
. Since both
F
and
K
are
S
-
\mathbb{R}
-constructible, so is
\ker u
. Applying (
A.10
) to
\ker u
yields a similar epimorphism
u^{\prime}:K^{\prime}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\ker u
so that
F=\coker u^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{Mod}_{\textup{w-}\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
. From Corollary
A.4
it follows that
F
is
{\mathbf{P}}
-coherent.
∎

Lemma A.14
.

If
F\in\mathrm{Mod}_{\textup{w-}\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
is
{\mathbf{P}}
-coherent, then it is
S
-
\mathbb{R}
-constructible.

Proof.

We have to prove that, for each
x\in X
,
F|_{\{x\}\times S}
is
\mathscr{O}_{S}
-coherent. According to
[
KS96
, Th. A.5]
, there exists
\mathfrak{W}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\in\rD^{-}_{\mathrm{coh}}({\mathbf{P}})
and an isomorphism
L(\mathfrak{W}^{{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}})\simeq F
in
\rD^{-}({\mathbf{A}})
. Therefore,
L(\mathfrak{W}^{{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}})|_{\{x\}\times S}\simeq F|_{\{x\}\times S}
in
\rD^{-}(\mathscr{O}_{S})
. We note that
\mathfrak{W}^{{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}}|_{\{x\}\times S}
is a complex in
\rD^{-}_{\mathrm{coh}}({\mathbf{P}}(\mathscr{O}_{S}))
introduced in
[
KS96
, A.2]
and thus the coherence of
F|_{\{x\}\times S}
follows by the equivalence of categories of
[
KS96
, Th. A.9]
.
∎

Detailed proof of Theorem
5.10

Recall that for a real analytic manifold
X
,
\mathscr{A}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}
denotes the sheaf of rings of real analytic functions on
X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}
. The sheaf
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}
consists of differential operators with real analytic coefficients on
X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}
which commute with the holomorphic differential operators on
S
. The functor
\THH^{S}_{X}
is defined from
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rD^{+}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S})
. It will be more convenient to use right
\mathscr{D}
-modules, so we will make use of the functor
\THH^{S,\vee}_{X}
obtained from
\THH^{S}_{X}
by side-changing, that is, by tensoring with the sheaf
\omega_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}
of real analytic forms of maximal degree
\dim_{\mathbb{R}}X+\dim_{\mathbb{C}}S
(since we only deal with forms with anti-holomorphic degree with respect to
S
).

For a real analytic map
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y
, the pushforward functor
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{!}
for right
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}
-modules is defined by means of the transfer module
\mathscr{D}_{(X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y)\times S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}
and its bounded
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}
-locally free resolution
\Sp_{(X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y)\times S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}
: we have (with the usual structure of
(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S},\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S})
bi-module)

\mathscr{D}_{(X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y)\times S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}=\mathscr{A}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes_{f^{-1}\mathscr{A}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}}f^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}

and (by means of the sheaf
\Theta_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}
of real analytic vector fields on
X\times\penalty S_{R}
which commute with holomorphic vector fields on
S
, and the associated Spencer complex of
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}
)

\Sp(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S})\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}}\mathscr{D}_{(X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y)\times S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}\simeq\Sp(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S})\otimes_{f^{-1}\mathscr{A}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}}f^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}.

A reminder on integration of currents

Let
\Db_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}
denote the sheaf of distributions on
X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}
, that we regard as a left
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}
-module. Furthermore, we consider the shifted real analytic de Rham complex
\Omega_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}

with term in degree zero being the sheaf
\omega_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}
. We denote by
\Db^{\vee}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}
denote the right
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}
-module
\omega_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}}\Db_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}
. The distributional de Rham complex

(\Omega_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}}\Db_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}})\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}}\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}

(the left structure of
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}
is used for defining the complex and the right structure of
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}
induces the right structure of the cohomologies) is a resolution of
\Db^{\vee}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}
as a right
\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}
-module.

For
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y
as above, we set

\mathscr{C}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}:=\Db^{\vee}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}}\Sp_{(X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y)\times S_{\mathbb{R}}/S},

so that the pushforward of
\Db^{\vee}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}
can be expressed as

\displaystyle{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{!}\Db^{\vee}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}

\displaystyle\simeq f_{!}(\Db^{\vee}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}}\Sp_{(X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y)\times S_{\mathbb{R}}/S})=f_{!}\mathscr{C}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}

\displaystyle\simeq f_{!}(\Db^{\vee}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}}\Sp(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S})\otimes_{f^{-1}\mathscr{A}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}}f^{-1}\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S})

\displaystyle\simeq f_{!}(\Db^{\vee}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}\otimes_{\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}}\Sp(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}))\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}}\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}

\displaystyle\simeq f_{!}(\Omega_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}}\Db_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}})\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}}\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S},

with a suitable definition of the differential of the complexes involved above.

On the other hand, the integration of currents is a morphism of complexes

{\textstyle\int\!}_{f}:f_{!}(\Omega_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}}\Db_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}(\Omega_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}\otimes_{\mathscr{A}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}}\Db_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}).

which satisfies, for a composition
f=h\circ g:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Z\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y
:

{\textstyle\int\!}_{f}={\textstyle\int\!}_{h}\circ{\textstyle\int\!}_{g}.

This integration morphism can be enhanced at the
\mathscr{D}
-module level and we obtain:

Lemma A.15
.

There exists a morphism of right
\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}
-modules

{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}{\textstyle\int\!}_{f}:{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{!}\Db^{\vee}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\Db^{\vee}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}

which is compatible with composition of morphisms, in the sense that the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{!}\Db^{\vee}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}{\textstyle\int\!}_{f}}
\textstyle{{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}h_{!}({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}g_{!}\Db^{\vee}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}h_{!}({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}{\textstyle\int\!}_{g})}
\textstyle{{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}h_{!}\Db^{\vee}_{Z\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}{\textstyle\int\!}_{h}}
\textstyle{\Db^{\vee}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}}

Proof of Theorem
5.10
.

We will first construct a natural transformation

\varphi_{f}(L({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})):{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{!}\THH^{S,\vee}_{X}(f^{-1}L({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))_{|{{\mathbf{P}}}_{Y}^{\mathrm{op}}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\THH^{S,\vee}_{Y}(L({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))_{|{{\mathbf{P}}}_{Y}^{\mathrm{op}}},

(A.16)

where
{\mathbf{P}}_{Y}
is the category given in Definition
A.2
, and we use
{\mathbf{P}}_{Y}^{\mathrm{op}}
because the functors
\THH^{S,\vee}
are contravariant. Here, we regard both
\THH^{S,\vee}_{Y}(L({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))
and
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{!}\THH^{S,\vee}_{X}(f^{-1}L({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))
as functors from
{\mathbf{P}}_{Y}^{\mathrm{op}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S})
, although
\THH^{S,\vee}_{Y}(L({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))
takes values in
\mathrm{Mod}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S})
(see below).

Step 1

Given
Y^{\prime}=Y\smallsetminus\Omega
with
\Omega\in\mathscr{S}_{Y}
and
V\in\mathscr{S}_{S}
, we have (cf.
[
FMFS21
, (2)]
)

\THH^{S,\vee}_{Y}(\mathbb{C}_{Y^{\prime}}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V})=\Gamma_{Y^{\prime}\times V}(\Db^{\vee}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}})\in\mathrm{Mod}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S})

(we consider right
\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S}
-modules here), and since
f^{-1}(\mathbb{C}_{Y^{\prime}}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V})\simeq\mathbb{C}_{f^{-1}Y^{\prime}}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V}
, we have

\THH^{S}_{X}(f^{-1}(\mathbb{C}_{Y^{\prime}}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V}))\simeq\Gamma_{f^{-1}(Y^{\prime})\times V}(\Db^{\vee}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}).

On noting that
f_{!}\Gamma_{f^{-1}(Y^{\prime})\times V}=\Gamma_{Y^{\prime}\times V}f_{!}
, we obtain a morphism

\textstyle{{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{!}\bigl[\Gamma_{f^{-1}(Y^{\prime})\times V}(\Db^{\vee}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}})\bigr]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\simeq}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{Y^{\prime}\times V}\bigl[{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{!}(\Db^{\vee}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}})\bigr]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{Y^{\prime}\times V}({}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}{\textstyle\int\!}_{f})}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{Y^{\prime}\times V}\Db^{\vee}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}}
\textstyle{{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{!}\THH^{S,\vee}_{X}(f^{-1}(\mathbb{C}_{Y^{\prime}}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\varphi_{f}(\mathbb{C}_{Y^{\prime}}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V})}
\textstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{\THH^{S,\vee}_{Y}(\mathbb{C}_{Y^{\prime}}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\wr}

From Lemma
A.15
we obtain:

\varphi_{f}(\mathbb{C}_{Y^{\prime}}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V})=\varphi_{h}(\mathbb{C}_{Y^{\prime}}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V})\circ{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}h_{!}(\varphi_{g}(\mathbb{C}_{h^{-1}(Y^{\prime})}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V})).

(A.17)

Step 2

For
\Omega=Y\smallsetminus Y^{\prime}
as above, let us apply the triangulated functor
\THH_{Y}^{S,\vee}
to the exact sequence

0\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{C}_{\Omega}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{C}_{Y}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\mathbb{C}_{Y^{\prime}}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0.

We still obtain an exact sequence, due to the isomorphism of distinguished triangles

\textstyle{\THH^{S,\vee}_{Y}(\mathbb{C}_{Y^{\prime}}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{\THH^{S,\vee}_{Y}(\mathbb{C}_{Y}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{\THH^{S,\vee}_{Y}(\mathbb{C}_{\Omega}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V})\displaystyle\mathchoice{\xrightarrow{\textstyle\kern 4.0pt+1\kern 3.0pt}}{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\longrightarrow}}}{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\wr}
\textstyle{0\displaystyle\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\Gamma_{Y^{\prime}\times V}\Db^{\vee}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{Y\times V}\Db^{\vee}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma_{Y\times V}T\mathscr{H}om(\mathbb{C}_{\Omega\times V},\Db^{\vee}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}})\displaystyle\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}0}

Let us apply the functor
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{!}\THH^{S,\vee}_{X}(f^{-1}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))
to the same exact sequence. We realize the corresponding distinguished triangle in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S})
as an exact sequence of complexes in
\rC^{b}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S})
(setting
\mathscr{C}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}_{f^{-1}(Y^{\prime})\times V}=\Gamma_{f^{-1}(Y^{\prime})\times V}\mathscr{C}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}_{X\times\penalty S_{R}}
), and there is a unique morphism
\varphi_{f}(\mathbb{C}_{\Omega}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V})
in
\rC^{b}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S})
making the following diagram commutative:

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{f_{!}\mathscr{C}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}_{f^{-1}(Y^{\prime})\times V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\varphi_{f}(\mathbb{C}_{Y^{\prime}}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V})}
\textstyle{f_{!}\mathscr{C}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}_{X\times V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\varphi_{f}(\mathbb{C}_{Y}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V})}
\textstyle{f_{!}\mathscr{C}^{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}_{f^{-1}(\Omega)\times V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\varphi_{f}(\mathbb{C}_{\Omega}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V})}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\THH^{S,\vee}_{Y}(\mathbb{C}_{Y^{\prime}}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\THH^{S,\vee}_{Y}(\mathbb{C}_{Y}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\THH^{S,\vee}_{Y}(\mathbb{C}_{\Omega}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

We regard
\varphi_{f}(\mathbb{C}_{\Omega}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V})
as a morphism in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S})
, and given a composition
f=h\circ g
, the relation (
A.17
) implies a similar relation.

Step 3

In order to obtain (
A.16
), we need to check that it is compatible with morphisms in
\mathscr{S}
. Due to the description of morphisms (Definition
A.2
), we are left with considering

\Omega\subset\Omega^{\prime}\subset Y
and
V\subset V^{\prime}\subset S
and a section

\alpha\in\Gamma(\Omega\times\overline{V};p_{Y}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
defining a morphism

\Omega\times V\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\Omega^{\prime}\times V^{\prime}
in
{\mathbf{P}}_{Y}
.
Then the commutativity
(\alpha\cdot)\circ\varphi_{f}(\mathbb{C}_{\Omega^{\prime}}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V^{\prime}})=\varphi_{f}(\mathbb{C}_{\Omega}\boxtimes\mathscr{O}_{V})\circ(\alpha\cdot)
is readily checked.

In such a way we have constructed a natural transformation (
A.16
). The relation (
A.17
) holds for
\varphi_{f}
.

Step 4

In order to end the proof of the theorem, we show how to extend
\varphi_{f}
as a natural transformation between the extended functors

\THH^{S,\vee}_{Y}(L({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})),{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{!}\THH^{S,\vee}_{X}(f^{-1}L({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})):\rD^{+}({\mathbf{P}}_{Y}^{\mathrm{op}})=\rD^{-}({\mathbf{P}}_{Y})^{\mathrm{op}}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rD^{+}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S})
, which will suffice, according to Proposition
A.6
.

Firstly, since
f
has finite cohomological dimension, the above functors extend as functors from
\rC^{+}({\mathbf{P}}_{Y}^{\mathrm{op}})
to
\rD^{+}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S})
, and by Step 3,
\varphi_{f}
extends as a natural transformation between these functors. Then the latter functors define functors
\rD^{+}({\mathbf{P}}_{Y}^{\mathrm{op}})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rD^{+}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S})
, and
\varphi_{f}
extends similarly as a natural transformation between them.

Lastly, (
A.17
) also extends as

\varphi_{f}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})=\varphi_{h}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})\circ{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}h_{!}(\varphi_{g}(h^{-1}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))).\qed

(A.18)

Complement to the proof of Lemma
5.14

We keep the setting of Theorem
5.10
. For
f:X\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}Y
, we denote the counit of the adjunction
(f^{-1},Rf_{*})
on
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
by

\delta_{f}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}):f^{-1}Rf_{*}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}).

For a composition
f=h\circ g
, the following relation is standard:

\delta_{f}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})=\delta_{g}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})\circ g^{-1}(\delta_{h}(Rg_{*}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})).

(A.19)

As we need to work both with
Rf_{*}
(because of the above adjunction) and
Rf_{!}
(because of Theorem
5.10
), we will restrict
Rf_{*}
as acting on the sub-category
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}},f!}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
consisting of
S
-
\mathbb{R}
-constructible complexes with
f
-proper support, so that
Rf_{*}
coincides with
Rf_{!}
on this sub-category and has essential image in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}(p_{Y}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
. From now on, we only consider
\delta_{f}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})
for
{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
being an object or morphism in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}},f!}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
. Note however that
f^{-1}Rf_{*}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})
is an object or a morphism in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
and possibly not in
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}},f!}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
, but (
A.19
) remains meaningful (and valid) since
Rg_{*}
is a functor from
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}},f!}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
to
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}},h!}(p_{Z}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})
. We will thus replace below the
*
-pushforwards with the
!
-pushforwards.

Applying the contravariant functor
\THH^{S}_{X}
to this restricted
\delta_{f}
leads to a natural transformation between functors
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}},f!}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rD^{+}(\mathscr{D}_{X\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S})
:

\varepsilon_{f}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})=\THH^{S}_{X}(\delta_{f}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})):\THH^{S}_{X}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\THH^{S}_{X}(f^{-1}Rf_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})),

which satisfies, for a composition
f=h\circ g
, the following relation:

\varepsilon_{f}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})=\THH^{S}_{X}\bigl(g^{-1}(\delta_{h}(Rg_{!}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\bigr)\circ\varepsilon_{g}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}).

The natural transformation
\mu_{f}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})
from the functor
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{!}\THH_{X}^{S}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}):\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}},f!}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rD^{+}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S})
to the functor

\THH_{Y}^{S}(Rf_{!}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}):\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}}}(p_{X}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rD^{+}(\mathscr{D}_{Y\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S})
, as defined in Lemma
5.14
, now reads, by means of
\varphi_{f}
of Theorem
5.10
,

\mu_{f}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})=\varphi_{f}(Rf_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\circ{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{!}(\varepsilon_{f}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})).

We aim at proving that the following diagram of natural transformations commutes:

\textstyle{{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}h_{!}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}g_{!}\THH_{X}^{S}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}h_{!}(\mu_{g}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))}
\textstyle{{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}h_{!}\THH^{S}_{Z}(Rg_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mu_{h}(Rg_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))}
\textstyle{\THH^{S}_{Y}(Rh_{!}Rg_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{!}\THH_{X}^{S}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mu_{f}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})}
\textstyle{\THH^{S}_{Y}(Rf_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))}

where the vertical equalities are defined from the standard isomorphisms.

Commutativity amounts to the equality, as natural transformations,

\varphi_{f}(Rf_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\circ{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{!}(\varepsilon_{f}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))=\varphi_{h}(Rf_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\circ{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}h_{!}(\varepsilon_{h}(Rg_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})))\circ{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}h_{!}\mu_{g}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}),

which is implied, according to (
A.18
) and the isomorphism
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}f_{!}={}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}h_{!}{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}g_{!}
, by

\displaystyle\varphi_{g}(h^{-1}(Rf_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})))\circ{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}g_{!}(\varepsilon_{f}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{h}(Rg_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\circ\mu_{g}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})

\displaystyle=\varepsilon_{h}(Rg_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\circ\varphi_{g}(Rg_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\circ{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}g_{!}(\varepsilon_{g}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})),

and itself is implied by the equality

\varphi_{g}(h^{-1}(Rf_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})))\circ{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}g_{!}\THH^{S}_{X}\bigl(g^{-1}(\delta_{h}(Rg_{!}{\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\bigr)=\varepsilon_{h}(Rg_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\circ\varphi_{g}(Rg_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})).

It is thus enough to apply the equality

\varphi_{g}(h^{-1}Rh_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\circ{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}g_{!}\THH^{S}_{X}\bigl(g^{-1}(\delta_{h}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\bigr)=\THH^{S}_{Z}(\delta_{h}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\circ\varphi_{g}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})

to
Rg_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})
. This is an equality of natural transformations

{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}g_{!}\THH_{X}^{S}(g^{-1}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\THH_{Z}^{S}(h^{-1}Rh_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})),

both source and target regarded as functors
\rD^{\mathrm{b}}_{{\mathbb{R}\textup{-c}},h!}(p_{Z}^{-1}\mathscr{O}_{S})\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\rD^{+}(\mathscr{D}_{Z\times\penalty S_{\mathbb{R}}/S})
. We will prove the latter equality, which amounts to the commutativity of the diagram of natural transformations

\textstyle{{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}g_{!}\THH_{X}^{S}(g^{-1}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}g_{!}\THH_{X}^{S}(g^{-1}(\delta_{h}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})))}
\textstyle{\varphi_{g}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})}
\textstyle{{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}g_{!}\THH_{X}^{S}(g^{-1}h^{-1}Rh_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\varphi_{g}(h^{-1}Rh_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))}
\textstyle{\THH_{Z}^{S}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\THH_{Z}^{S}(\delta_{h}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))}
\textstyle{\THH_{Z}^{S}(h^{-1}Rh_{!}({\raisebox{1.0pt}{${\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}$}}))}

The commutativity follows from the property that
\varphi_{g}
is a natural transformation
{}_{\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}}g_{!}\THH_{X}^{S}\circ g^{-1}\mathchoice{\longrightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}{\rightarrow}\THH_{Z}^{S}
and the lower horizontal natural transformation is the image by
\varphi_{g}
of the upper one.∎
